\delta x^{\pm\pm}=a^{\pm\pm}
for some constant
a^{\pm\pm}
. The variation
\delta\mathcal{A}
of the superspace Lagrangian is given by

\begin{split}\delta\mathcal{A}&=D_{+}\left(\delta\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}\right)+D_{-}\left(\delta\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}\right)+\partial_{++}\left(\delta\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)\cr&+\partial_{--}\left(\delta\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\delta\Phi\right)+D_{-}\left(\delta\Phi D_{+}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)\right)\cr&-\frac{1}{2}\left(D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\delta\Phi\right)+D_{+}\left(\delta\Phi D_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)\right)\cr&-\delta\Phi\left(-\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\Phi}+D_{+}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+\partial_{++}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}+\partial_{--}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right.\cr&\left.-D_{+}D_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right).\end{split}

(4)

Here we have chosen to symmetrize the term involving
D_{+}D_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}
using
\left\{D_{+},D_{-}\right\}=0
.

The last two lines of (
4
) are the superspace equation of motion; we now specialize to the case of on-shell variations, for which this last term vanishes. Further, the left side of (
4
) is
\delta\mathcal{A}=a^{++}\partial_{++}\mathcal{A}+a^{--}\partial_{--}\mathcal{A}
, which is a total derivative. We use
\partial_{\pm\pm}=D_{\pm}D_{\pm}
to express (
4
) in the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&=a^{++}D_{+}\Big[\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{+}\mathcal{A}\Big]\\
&+a^{++}D_{-}\Big[\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+D_{-}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)\Big]\\
&+a^{--}D_{+}\Big[\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)\Big]\\
&+a^{--}D_{-}\Big[\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+D_{-}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{-}\mathcal{A}\Big].\end{split}

(5)

This equation gives a conservation law for a superfield
\mathcal{T}
which we define by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{++-}

\displaystyle=\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{+}\mathcal{A},

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{+++}

\displaystyle=\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+D_{-}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{---}

\displaystyle=\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)

(6)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{--+}

\displaystyle=\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+D_{-}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{-}\mathcal{A}.

Equation (
5
) implies that, when the equations of motion are satisfied,
\mathcal{T}
obeys the superspace conservation laws:

\displaystyle D_{+}\mathcal{T}_{++-}+D_{-}\mathcal{T}_{+++}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle D_{+}\mathcal{T}_{---}+D_{-}\mathcal{T}_{--+}

\displaystyle=0.

(7)

We are now in a position to propose the supercurrent-squared deformation. Consider a one-parameter family of superspace Lagrangians labeled by
t
, which satisfy the ordinary differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=\mathcal{T}_{+++}^{(t)}\mathcal{T}_{---}^{(t)}-\mathcal{T}_{--+}^{(t)}\mathcal{T}_{++-}^{(t)},

(8)

where
\mathcal{T}^{(t)}
is the supercurrent superfield (
6
) computed from the superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(t)}
. This uniquely defines the supercurrent-squared deformation of an initial Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}
at finite deformation parameter
t
. We note that one can perform a similar Noether procedure in a
(0+1)
-dimensional theory and define an analogous deformation constructed from current bilinears in supersymmetric quantum mechanics
[
us:susy
]
.

2
Reduction to components for a free theory

To illutrate the relationship between the flow equation (
8
) and the usual
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
operator, let us explicitly compute the components of the supercurrent-squared deformation for a free
(1,1)
superspace Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi,

(9)

where
\Phi
is a superfield with component expansion

\displaystyle\Phi=\phi+i\theta^{+}\psi_{+}+i\theta^{-}\psi_{-}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}f.

(10)

The entries of
\mathcal{T}
, defined by (
6
), for the free theory are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++-}&=\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\Phi-D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{+++}&=-\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\Phi,\\
\mathcal{T}_{---}&=\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\Phi,\\
\mathcal{T}_{--+}&=-\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi-D_{-}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right).\end{split}

(11)

In components, (
11
) is

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{++-}=

\displaystyle-i\psi_{+}f+\theta^{+}\left(-f\partial_{++}\phi+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)+\theta^{-}\left(-f^{2}-\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)

\displaystyle+i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\left(-\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-\partial_{++}\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\phi-f\partial_{++}\psi_{-}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}f+\partial_{++}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\phi-\psi_{-}f\right)\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{+++}=

\displaystyle-i\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi-\theta^{+}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{+}+\left(\partial_{++}\phi\right)^{2}\right)-\theta^{-}\left(f\partial_{++}\phi+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)

\displaystyle-i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\left(2\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{++}\psi_{-}+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}f-f\partial_{++}\psi_{+}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{---}=

\displaystyle\,i\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi+\theta^{+}\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-f\partial_{--}\phi\right)+\theta^{-}\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+\left(\partial_{--}\phi\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}f-f\partial_{--}\psi_{-}-2\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right),

(12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{--+}=

\displaystyle-i\psi_{-}f+\theta^{+}\left(f^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)+\theta^{-}\left(-f\partial_{--}\phi-\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)

\displaystyle+i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\left(-\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{++}\psi_{-}+f\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-\partial_{--}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\phi-\psi_{+}\partial_{--}f+\partial_{--}\left(\psi_{+}f+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\phi\right)\right).

To compare with the non-superspace
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation, we identify the components of the usual stress tensor
T
for the theory of a free boson
\phi
and fermions
\psi_{\pm}
which one obtains by performing the integrals over
\theta^{\pm}
. In our conventions, these take the form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}T_{++++}&=\left(\partial_{++}\phi\right)^{2}+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{+},\\
T_{----}&=\left(\partial_{--}\phi\right)^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{-}.\end{split}

(13)

We will also drop terms involving the auxiliary field
f
, since in the bosonic part of the supercurrent-squared deformation, these terms vanish after integrating out
f
using its equation of motion. Then the bilinears appearing in our flow equation (
8
) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}&=\psi_{+}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi+i\theta^{+}\left(\psi_{+}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-T_{++++}\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi\right)\\
&\quad+i\theta^{-}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi T_{----}+\psi_{+}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi\right)-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\big(T_{++++}T_{----}\\
&\quad+2\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)-\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\big),\\
\mathcal{T}_{++-}\mathcal{T}_{--+}&=-2\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right).\end{split}

(14)

The superspace integral of the deformation
\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}+\mathcal{T}_{++-}\mathcal{T}_{--+}
picks out the top component, which is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int&d^{2}\theta\,\left(\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}+\mathcal{T}_{++-}\mathcal{T}_{--+}\right)=\\
&-T_{++++}T_{----}-2\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)-\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}.\end{split}

(15)

We see that (
15
) contains the usual
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation, given in our bi-spinor notation by
-T_{++++}T_{----}
, along with extra terms which are all proportional to the fermion equations of motion,
\partial_{\pm\pm}\psi_{\mp}=0
. These added terms vanish on-shell, which means that the supercurrent-squared deformation is on-shell equivalent to the usual bosonic
{T\overline{T}}
deformation. In the next subsection, we will verify that these additional terms do not affect the energy levels of the theory deformed by supercurrent-squared, which means that the same inviscid Burgers’ relation between the deformed and undeformed energy levels holds for our supersymmetric deformation as for the ordinary
{T\overline{T}}
flow.

3
Relationship with the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet

The
(1,1)
superfield
\mathcal{T}
contains the conserved stress-energy tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
and the supercurrent
S_{\mu\alpha}
. Such current multiplets have received much attention in the literature; the first construction for four-dimensional theories was the FZ multiplet
[
Ferrara:1974pz
]
, which was later shown to be a special case of the more general
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet
[
dumitrescuSupercurrentsBraneCurrents2011a
]
.

For the two-dimensional theories we consider here, it is known that the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet is the most general multiplet containing the stress tensor and supercurrent, subject to assumptions that the multiplet be indecomposable
1
1

1

A representation is
indecomposable
if it is not isomorphic to a direct sum of representations. All irreducible representations are indecomposable, but not all indecomposable represenations are irreducible.
and contain no other operators with spin greater than one. Since our supercurrent superfield
\mathcal{T}
satisfies these properties, it must be equivalent to the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet. As we will show, the four superfields contained in
\mathcal{T}
are identical to the four superfields of the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet, up to terms which vanish on-shell and therefore do not affect the conservation equations for the currents.

The
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet is a reducible but indecomposable set of two superfields
\mathcal{S}
and
\chi
satisfying the constraints

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{\mp}\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}&=D_{\pm}\chi_{\pm},\\
D_{-}\chi_{+}&=D_{+}\chi_{-}.\end{split}

(16)

In components, the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet for
(1,1)
theories contains the usual stress tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
, the supercurrent
S_{\mu\alpha}
, and a vector
Z_{\mu}
which is associated with a scalar central charge:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{+++}&=S_{+++}+\theta^{+}T_{++++}+\theta^{-}Z_{++}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{++}S_{-++},\\
\mathcal{S}_{---}&=S_{---}+\theta^{+}Z_{--}+\theta^{-}T_{----}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{--}S_{+--},\\
\chi_{+}&=S_{-++}+\theta^{+}Z_{++}+\theta^{-}T_{++--}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{++}S_{+--},\\
\chi_{-}&=S_{+--}+\theta^{+}T_{++--}+\theta^{-}Z_{--}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{--}S_{-++}.\end{split}

(17)

In terms of these component fields, the constraints (
16
) give conservation equations for the currents:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{++}T_{----}+\partial_{--}T_{++--}&=0=\partial_{++}T_{--++}+\partial_{--}T_{++++},\\
\partial_{++}S_{+--}+\partial_{--}S_{+++}&=0=\partial_{++}S_{---}+\partial_{--}S_{-++},\\
\partial_{++}Z_{--}+\partial_{--}Z_{++}&=0.\end{split}

(18)

We claim that the components (
12
) of our superspace supercurrent are the same as those in the two superfields
\mathcal{S}
and
\chi
appearing in the
(1,1)

\mathcal{S}
-multiplet (
17
), up to signs and terms which vanish on-shell. In particular, after discarding terms which are proportional to the equations of motion, we find the identifications:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}=\mp\mathcal{T}_{\pm\pm\pm},\qquad\chi_{+}=\mathcal{T}_{++-},\qquad\chi_{-}=-\mathcal{T}_{--+}.

(19)

We will check this explicitly for the free theory,
\mathcal{A}=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
, for which we computed the components of
\mathcal{T}
in section (
2
). Writing only those terms that survive when the component equations of motion
f=0
,
\partial_{++}\psi_{-}=0=\partial_{--}\psi_{+}
, and
\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\phi=0
are all satisfied, (
12
) becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++-}&\overset{\text{on-shell}}{=}0,\\
\mathcal{T}_{+++}&\overset{\text{on-shell}}{=}-i\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi-\theta^{+}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{+}+\left(\partial_{++}\phi\right)^{2}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{---}&\overset{\text{on-shell}}{=}i\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi+\theta^{-}\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+\left(\partial_{--}\phi\right)^{2}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{--+}&\overset{\text{on-shell}}{=}0.\end{split}

(20)

For the free
(1,1)
superfield considered here, the supercurrent is given in our conventions by

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{+++}&=\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi,\\
S_{---}&=\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi,\\
S_{+--}&=0=S_{-++},\end{split}

(21)

while the stress tensor components are as in (
12
). To find expressions for the scalar central charge current
Z_{\pm\pm}
, we use the supersymmetry algebra implied by the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet constraints, which gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\{Q_{\pm},S_{\pm\pm\pm}\right\}&=T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm},\\
\left\{Q_{\pm},S_{\pm\mp\mp}\right\}&=T_{\pm\pm\mp\mp},\\
\left\{Q_{\pm},S_{\mp\pm\pm}\right\}&=Z_{\pm\pm},\\
\left\{Q_{\pm},S_{\mp\mp\mp}\right\}&=Z_{\mp\mp}.\end{split}

(22)

Note that the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet constraints only hold when the conservation equations for the currents hold, so the relations (
22
) should be viewed as an on-shell algebra. Acting with the supercharges
Q_{\pm}
on the stress tensor and supercurrent components, one finds that
Z_{--}\sim\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}
and
Z_{++}\sim\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}
, both of which vanish when the fermion equations of motion are satisfied.

Thus, after imposing the equations of motion, we can write our supercurrent superfield components as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++-}=\chi_{+}=0\,,&\qquad\mathcal{T}_{--+}=0=-\chi_{-},\\
\mathcal{T}_{+++}=-S_{+++}-\theta^{+}T_{++++}=-\mathcal{S}_{+++}\,,&\qquad\mathcal{T}_{---}=S_{---}+\theta^{-}T_{----}=\mathcal{S}_{---}.\end{split}

Since terms which vanish on-shell do not affect conservation equations, one can view
\mathcal{T}
as an improvement transformation of the
\mathcal{S}
-multiplet. The constraint equation
D_{\mp}\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}-D_{\pm}\chi_{\pm}=0
is expressed by our conservation equations (
7
).

4
Equivalence to
T\overline{T}
for Spectrum

In this section we prove the theory deformed by (
8
) satisfies the same flow equation for the finite-volume energy levels as the usual
{T\overline{T}}
deformation. In this sense, the supercurrent-squared deformation has the same solvability properties as
{T\overline{T}}
.

We begin with the superspace conservation equation given in (
7
). It is straightforward to solve these constraints in components by using the conservation of the stress energy tensor:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{T}_{+++}=H_{+++}-\theta^{+}T_{++++}-\theta^{-}W_{++}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}G_{+++},\\
&\mathcal{T}_{---}=H_{---}+\theta^{-}T_{----}+\theta^{+}W_{--}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}G_{---},\\
&\mathcal{T}_{--+}=H_{--+}-\theta^{+}T_{--++}-\theta^{-}W_{--}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}G_{--+},\\
&\mathcal{T}_{++-}=H_{++-}+\theta^{-}T_{++--}+\theta^{+}W_{++}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}G_{++-}.\end{split}

(23)

Here
(H_{\pm\pm\pm},H_{\mp\mp\pm})
are the lowest components of
\mathcal{T}
while
(G_{\pm\pm\pm},G_{\mp\mp\pm})
are its highest components. The conservation law in (
7
) implies the following constraints on
G
and
H
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&G_{\mp\mp\pm}=\partial_{\pm\pm}H_{\mp\mp\mp},\\
&G_{\pm\pm\pm}=\partial_{\pm\pm}H_{\mp\pm\pm}.\end{split}

(24)

In terms of these components, the deformation in (
8
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=

\displaystyle-\int d^{2}\theta\left(\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}+\mathcal{T}_{++-}\mathcal{T}_{--+}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(T_{++++}T_{----}-T_{++--}T_{--++}\right)

(25)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(H_{+++}G_{---}-G_{+++}H_{---}-H_{++-}G_{--+}+G_{++-}H_{--+}\right).

The first term
-\left(T_{++++}T_{----}-T_{++--}T_{--++}\right)
of (
4
) is the usual
T\bar{T}
deformation. To understand how the second bracket changes the spectrum, we consider the two-point correlation function:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}=\left\langle H_{+++}(x)G_{---}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle G_{+++}(x)H_{---}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

(26)

\displaystyle-\left\langle H_{++-}(x)G_{--+}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle+\left\langle G_{++-}(x)H_{--+}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle.

Up to contact terms that vanish at separated points, we can replace
G
by using the conservation equation (
24
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}=\left\langle H_{+++}(x)\partial^{\prime}_{--}H_{{--+}}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\partial_{++}H_{++-}(x)H_{---}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

(27)

\displaystyle-\left\langle H_{++-}(x)\partial^{\prime}_{++}H_{---}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle+\left\langle\partial_{--}H_{+++}(x)H_{--+}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle.

Here
\partial^{\prime}
means the derivative with respect to the coordinate
x^{\prime}
. Now we can use translational invariance to move the derivative from
x^{\prime}
to
x
. Then the first term cancels the fourth term and the third term cancels the second one, because both
H
and
G
are fermionic, so that
\mathcal{C}
vanishes at separated points. This implies that the extra term can have no effect on the spectrum – the presence of this term is only to make the action supersymmetric. The theory remains solvable, like the usual
{T\overline{T}}
deformation, with the same relation between deformed and undeformed energy levels.

2
Theories with
(1,1)
Supersymmetry

In this section, we consider the supercurrent-squared deformation of a theory involving a single
(1,1)
superfield
\Phi
, both in the free case and with a superpotential.

1
Free
(1,1)
superfield

First consider an undeformed superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
. We make the following ansatz for the deformed Lagrangian at finite
t
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=F\left(\,t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,\,t\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}\right)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi.

(1)

Here
F
may only depend on the two dimensionless combinations which we define by

\displaystyle x=t\,\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,\qquad y=t\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}.

(2)

In order to reduce to the free theory as
t\to 0
, we must also impose the boundary condition
F(0,0)=1
.

After computing the components of the supercurrent-squared deformation and simplifying, the flow equation (
8
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}F&=\left(\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}-\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi\right)F^{2}\\
&\quad-2F\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi\right)\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi+\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}\right)\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}.\end{split}

(3)

In terms of the dimensionless variables
x
and
y
, equation (
3
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}x+\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}y=(y-x)F^{2}-2F\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}x(x+y).

(4)

Supplemented with the boundary condition
F(0,0)=1
, the partial differential equation (
3
) uniquely determines the deformed Lagrangian at finite
t
.

As a check, we would like to verify that the bosonic structure of the solution to (
3
) reduces to the known results for the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformed theory of a free boson. We will argue that, in fact, it suffices to set
y=0
in (
3
) and note that the result agrees with the flow equation obtained in the purely bosonic case
[
cavagliaBarTDeformed2D2016
]
.

Indeed, let us write the components of the superfield
\Phi
as
\Phi=\phi+i\theta^{+}\psi_{+}+i\theta^{-}\psi_{-}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}f
. To probe the bosonic structure, it suffices to set
\psi_{\pm}=0
, perform the superspace integration, and then integrate out the auxiliary field
f
using its equation of motion. Thus consider an arbitrary superspace integral of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=\int d^{2}\theta\,F^{(t)}(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi.

(5)

The lowest component of the superfield
y=t(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}
is to
tf^{2}
, and the higher components will not contribute to the bosonic part because they come multiplying
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
, which is already proportional to
\theta^{+}\theta^{-}
after setting the fermions to zero.

Thus the purely bosonic piece of the physical Lagrangian associated with a superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=F^{(t)}(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=F^{(t)}\left(t\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi,tf^{2}\right)\left(f^{2}+4\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\right).

(6)

The equation of motion for the auxiliary field
f
is

\displaystyle 2tf\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}\left(f^{2}+4\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\right)+2fF=0,

(7)

which admits the solution
f=0
. The Lagrangian for the bosonic field
\phi
is then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=4F^{(t)}\left(t\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi,0\right)\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi.

(8)

Therefore, to determine the terms in the Lagrangian which involve only
\phi
, we may solve the simpler partial differential equation

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}x=-xF^{2}-2Fx^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial x},

(9)

which holds upon setting
y=0
in (
4
). But this is precisely the equation discussed in section
1
, whose solution is equation (
25
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=\frac{\sqrt{1+4t\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi}-1}{2t}.

(10)

We see that the supercurrent-squared deformation of the free superfield is indeed a generalization of the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of a free boson, in the sense that it yields the same modification to the purely bosonic terms in the action but also includes additional terms which affect only the fermions.

2
On-Shell Simplification of Flow Equation

In the previous subsection, we investigated the purely bosonic part of the physical Lagrangian along the supercurrent-squared flow, after imposing the equation of motion for the auxiliary field
f
. This allowed us to simplify the flow equation and present a closed-form solution for the Lagrangian of the boson
\phi
, which reproduces the known square-root result.

In fact, a similar procedure can be performed in superspace rather than in components. We will now demonstrate that, if we are willing to go “partially on-shell” in the superspace Lagrangian – by imposing one implication of the superspace equations of motion, rather than the full equations – then the superspace flow equation will also simplify in a similar way. We can then solve this simplified flow equation and write an exact superspace Lagrangian. Since we have imposed part of the equations of motion, the resulting expression is only equivalent to the true solution on-shell (i.e. up to field redefinitions), but if we are willing to accept this shortcoming, this procedure allows for an explicit solution along the flow.

We begin with the general superspace Lagrangian (
5
). The function
F^{(t)}(x,y)
can be expanded as a power series in
y
to write

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=\int d^{2}\theta\,\left[F^{(t)}(x,0)+F^{(t)}_{y}(x,0)y+\cdots\right]\,D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\,.

(11)

On the other hand, the superspace equation of motion (
2
) for this Lagrangian is

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=D_{+}\left[F^{(t)}(x,y)\,D_{-}\Phi\right]-D_{-}\left[F^{(t)}(x,y)\,D_{-}\Phi\right]+\partial_{++}\left[t\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial x}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\partial_{--}\left[t\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial x}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right]+D_{+}D_{-}\left[2t\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial y}\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right]\,.

(12)

Now suppose that we multiply both sides of (
2
) by the product
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
. Both
D_{+}\Phi
and
D_{-}\Phi
are fermionic, so any term which was already proportional to either of those factors will vanish upon such a multiplication. The only terms which survive are

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=F^{(t)}(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)-2tD_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial y}\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{3}

\displaystyle=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)\cdot\left[F^{(t)}(x,y)-2t\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial y}(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}\right]\,.

(13)

Thus the equations of motion require that either
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)=0
on-shell or
F^{(t)}(x,y)-2t\frac{\partial F^{(t)}}{\partial y}(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}=0
on-shell. But the latter equation cannot hold identically since, at least for
t
small enough, it would require that
F^{(t)}(x,y)=0
and thus the action vanishes. Since
y\sim(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle yD_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi=0

(14)

on-shell. This means that, when the equations of motion are satisfied, we may replace the Taylor expansion (
11
) with

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=\int d^{2}\theta\,F^{(t)}(x,0)\,D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\,,

(15)

or said differently, we may simply set
y=0
whenever it appears in a superspace expression multiplying
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
. In particular, since the supercurrent-squared flow equation (
4
) appears inside such a superspace integral, we may set
y=0
there to find

\displaystyle x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}+xF^{2}+2x^{2}F\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}=0\,.

(16)

The general solution to this differential equation, as we have seen, is

\displaystyle F(x)=\frac{1}{2x}\left(-1+\sqrt{1+4c_{1}x}\right)\,,

(17)

and the initial condition
F(0)=1
fixes
c_{1}=1
. Thus we obtain a solution to the flow equation

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=\int d^{2}\theta\,\frac{1}{2x}\left(-1+\sqrt{1+4x}\right)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\,,

\displaystyle x=t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi\,,

(18)

which is on-shell equivalent to the true solution to the flow equation (involving both
x
and
y
). The advantage of this approach is that it allows us to see the square-root structure of the solution to the supercurrent-squared flow directly in superspace. We will see that a similar on-shell analysis is possible in the case of the
(2,2)
supercurrent-squared deformation, where the analysis leading up to equation (
14
) is replaced by the calculation in Appendix (
7.E
).

3
Interacting
(1,1)
superfield

Next, we consider the case with a superpotential: that is, we begin from the undeformed superspace Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+h(\Phi),

(19)

where
h(\Phi)
is an arbitrary function (it need not give rise to a theory with infinitely many integrals of motion). After performing the superspace integral, the physical Lagrangian is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(0)}=\int d^{2}\theta\,\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi+\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}+f^{2}+h^{\prime}(\phi)f.

(20)

Integrating out the auxiliary field using its equation of motion
f=-\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime}(\phi)
, we see that the physical potential
V
is given by
V=-\frac{1}{4}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{2}
.

We might expect that both the kinetic and potential terms are modified by a finite supercurrent-squared deformation, which would lead us to make the ansatz

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=F(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+G(t,\Phi),

(21)

where
G
is a new function to be determined, and
x=t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
,
y=t\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}
as above. However, the deformation does not induce any change in the potential
h
, so in fact we may put
G=h
for all
t
. To see this, we can write down the supercurrent-squared deformation associated with the ansatz (
21
), which gives

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}F(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+\frac{\partial}{\partial t}G(t,\Phi)=

(22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{t}\Bigg(\left(y-x\right)F^{2}-2Fx(x+y)\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}+\left(G^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2G^{\prime}\sqrt{y}\left(x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}-F\right)-2\sqrt{y}xG^{\prime}\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}\Bigg)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi.

The details of the calculation leading to (
22
) are discussed in Appendix
7.A
. We see that deformation is proportional to
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
, so it does not source any change in the potential
h(\Phi)
; thus we may take
G(h,\Phi)=h(\Phi)
in our ansatz. This leaves us with a single partial differential equation for
F
, namely

\displaystyle x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}+y\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}

\displaystyle=\left(y-x\right)F^{2}-2Fx(x+y)\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}+\left(h^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2h^{\prime}\sqrt{y}\left(x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}-F\right)-2\sqrt{y}xh^{\prime}\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}.

(23)

In the second line, we have used the constraint that
F
can depend only on the dimensionless combinations
x=t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
and
y=t\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}
.

As in the free case, we would like to study the purely bosonic terms in the physical Lagrangian resulting from (
23
) and compare them to known results. Here the auxiliary will play a more important role since
f=0
is no longer a solution.

We can expand both the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}=\int d^{2}\theta\left(F(x,y)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+h(\Phi)\right)
and the auxiliary field
f
as power series in
t
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}t^{j}\mathcal{L}^{(j)},\qquad f=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}t^{j}f^{(j)},

(24)

and then integrate out the auxiliary order-by-order in
t
. Doing so to order
t^{3}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}&=-\frac{1}{4}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{2}+\frac{x}{t}+t\left(\frac{1}{16}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{4}-\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^{2}\right)+t^{2}\left(-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^{2}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{2}-\frac{1}{64}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{6}+2\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^{3}\right)\\
&\quad+t^{3}\left(\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^{3}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{2}+\frac{1}{256}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{8}-5\left(\frac{x}{t}\right)^{4}\right)+\mathcal{O}(t^{4}),\end{split}

(25)

after setting the fermions to zero. Up to conventions, this matches the Taylor expansion of the known result
[
cavagliaBarTDeformed2D2016
,
bonelliBarDeformationsClosed2018
]
for the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of a boson with a generic potential
V
, which is given in our conventions as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(t)}=-\frac{1}{2t}\frac{1-2tV}{1-tV}+\frac{1}{2t}\sqrt{\frac{t\left(4V+\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\right)}{1-tV}+\frac{(1-2tV)^{2}}{(1-tV)^{2}}}.

(26)

Again the physical potential
V
is related to
h
via
V=-\frac{1}{4}h^{\prime}(\phi)^{2}
. We have checked explicitly that the bosonic part of the series solution to the PDE (
23
) matches the Taylor expansion of (
26
) up to
\mathcal{O}(t^{7})
.

3
Theories with
(0,1)
Supersymmetry

In this section we study the deformation of a theory with chiral
(0,1)
supersymmetry; a
(0,1)
scalar superfield
\Phi
consists of a scalar and a real fermion,
\Phi=\phi+i\theta^{+}\psi_{+}
. The Lagrangian in superspace is a function of
D_{+}\Phi
,
\partial_{++}\Phi
,
\partial_{--}\Phi
, as well as
\Phi
itself.

1
Free
(0,1)
superfield

The free theory is defined by the Lagrangian,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}&=\int d\theta^{+}\ D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,\\
&=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi+\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}.\end{split}

(1)

Following the approach of section
1
, we first look for conservation laws for a given superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}
. They take the form,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\partial_{--}\mathcal{S}_{+++}+D_{+}\mathcal{T}_{++--}=0,\\
&\partial_{--}\mathcal{S}_{--+}+D_{+}\mathcal{T}_{----}=0,\end{split}

(2)

where
\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm+}
and
\mathcal{T}_{{\pm\pm}{--}}
are superfields given by:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{S}_{+++}=\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi,\\
&\mathcal{S}_{--+}=\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi-\mathcal{A},\\
&\mathcal{T}_{++--}=\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi+D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)-D_{+}\mathcal{A},\\
&\mathcal{T}_{----}=\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi+D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right).\end{split}

(3)

We define the supercurrent-squared deformation as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=\mathcal{S}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{----}-\mathcal{S}_{--+}\mathcal{T}_{++--}.

(4)

To understand what the deformation (
4
) does to a
(0,1)
theory, consider an undeformed Lagrangian in superspace

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=g(\Phi)D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,

(5)

where
g(\Phi)
is an arbitrary differentiable function of the superfield. A free theory corresponds to a constant
g(\Phi)
. To find the deformed theory
\mathcal{A}^{(t)}
, we first make a general ansatz for the deformed Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=f(t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi)D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,

(6)

where
f(x)
is some differentiable function. Using the expression for the supercurrents given in (
3
) and imposing the initial condition
f(x\rightarrow 0)=g(\Phi)
, we find the function
f(x)
satisfies the same differential equation found in (
9
). Its solution is given by

\displaystyle f(x)=\frac{\sqrt{1+4xg(\Phi)}-1}{2x}.

(7)

2
Reduction of
(1,1)
to
(0,1)

Any theory with
(1,1)
global supersymmetry can also be viewed as a theory with
(0,1)
global supersymmetry. Up to possible field redefinitions, we should therefore be able to relate the
(1,1)
theory deformed by the supercurrent-squared deformation defined in (
6
) to the
(0,1)
of (
4
), which we would have used if we had simply restricted to
(0,1)
supersymmetry.

To be more precise, consider a
(1,1)
theory whose physical Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
is given by the integral of a superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}
over
(1,1)
superspace. We can also view this as a
(0,1)
theory,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\int d^{2}\theta\,\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}=\int d\theta^{+}\,\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}.

(8)

The flow equation defining the supercurrent-squared deformation of
\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}
is
\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}=\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}-\mathcal{T}_{--+}\mathcal{T}_{++-}
. By performing the integral over
\theta^{-}
, this induces a flow for
\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}
due to (
8
), namely

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}

\displaystyle=\int d\theta^{+}\,\left(\mathcal{T}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{---}-\mathcal{T}_{--+}\mathcal{T}_{++-}\right).

(9)

For instance, let us consider the deformation of the free theory
\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}=D_{+}\Phi^{(1,1)}D_{-}\Phi^{(1,1)}
. This can be written as an integral over
(0,1)
superspace as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int d^{2}\theta\,D_{+}\Phi^{(1,1)}D_{-}\Phi^{(1,1)}&=\int d\theta^{+}\Big(-i\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\phi-i\psi_{-}f-\theta^{+}\big(f^{2}+\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\\
&\hskip 70.0pt+\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\big)\Big)\\
&=-\int d\theta^{+}\,\left(D_{+}\Phi^{(0,1)}\partial_{--}\Phi^{(0,1)}+\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right).\end{split}

(10)

Here we have written the integrand on the right side of (
10
) as a superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}\left(\Phi^{(0,1)},\Psi_{-}\right)
for a superfield
\Phi^{(0,1)}=\phi+i\theta^{+}\psi_{+}
of the form discussed above, along with an extra Fermi superfield
\Psi_{-}=i\psi_{-}+\theta^{+}f
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}\left(\Phi^{(0,1)},\Psi_{-}\right)=D_{+}\Phi^{(0,1)}\partial_{--}\Phi^{(0,1)}+\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}.

(11)

For comparison, we compute the supercurrent-squared deformation to leading order in
t
; that is, we compute the tangent vector
\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}^{(1,1)}}{\partial t}|_{t=0}
to the free theory along the flow and compare it to that of the free
(0,1)
theory with an extra fermion.

The components of the supercurrent superfield associated with the free theory, after integrating out the auxiliary using
f=0
, are given in equation (
14
). Using these and performing the integral over
\theta^{-}
, the reduced flow equation (
9
) at
t=0
becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}|_{t=0}&=i\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi T_{----}+\psi_{+}\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\phi\right)+\theta^{+}\Big(T_{++++}T_{----}\\
&\quad+2\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\Big),\end{split}

(12)

where we have used
T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm}=\left(\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi\right)^{2}+\psi_{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}\psi_{\pm}
.

We know that the solution to (
12
) must represent a solvable deformation of the original
(0,1)
theory because it descends from a solvable deformation in the parent
(1,1)
theory. On the other hand, one can construct the flow equation (
4
) directly in the
(0,1)
theory. This must also yield a solvable deformation since it is built out of currents which satisfy a superspace conservation equation of the same form as in the
(1,1)
supercurrent multiplet. One might suspect that these two deformations should be the same, up to field redefinitions which do not affect the spectrum. To check this, let us compare the leading-order deformations for these two cases in components. After including the contributions
\partial_{\pm\pm}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{\pm}\Psi_{-}}
to
\mathcal{T}_{\pm\pm--}
due to the fermion
\Psi_{-}
, the currents (
3
) for this theory are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{+++}&=D_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi\\
&=i\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi+\theta^{+}\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{+}+\left(\partial_{++}\phi\right)^{2}\right),\\
\mathcal{S}_{--+}&=-\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\\
&=-i\psi_{-}f-\theta^{+}\left(f^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{++--}&=\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi+\Psi_{-}\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}-D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi+\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)\\
&=-\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-f^{2}+i\theta^{+}\left(\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\phi\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{----}&=\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}-\Psi_{-}\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\\
&=\left(\partial_{--}\phi\right)^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+i\theta^{+}\left(-f\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+\psi_{-}\partial_{--}f+2\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right).\end{split}

(13)

The bilinears appearing in the
(0,1)
deformation are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{----}&=i\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi T_{----}+\theta^{+}\big(T_{++++}T_{----}\\
&\hskip 70.0pt+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}f-f\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+2\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)\big),\\
\mathcal{S}_{--+}\mathcal{T}_{++--}&=i\psi_{-}f\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+f^{2}\right)+\theta^{+}\big(\left(f^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)\left(f^{2}+\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)\\
&\hskip 70.0pt+\psi_{-}f\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\phi-\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)\big),\end{split}

(14)

and thus the
(0,1)
flow equation at
t=0
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(0,1)}|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{+++}\mathcal{T}_{----}-\mathcal{S}_{--+}\mathcal{T}_{++--}

(15)

\displaystyle=i\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi T_{----}-i\psi_{-}f\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}+f^{2}\right)

(16)

\displaystyle+\theta^{+}\Big(T_{++++}T_{----}+\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\phi\left(\psi_{-}\partial_{--}f-f\partial_{--}\psi_{-}+2\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)

(17)

\displaystyle-\left(f^{2}+\psi_{-}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}\right)\left(f^{2}+\psi_{+}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)-\psi_{-}f\left(\psi_{+}\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\phi-\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\psi_{+}\right)\Big).

The deformations (
12
) and (
15
) agree up to terms proportional to the equations of motion
f=0
and
\partial_{++}\psi_{-}=0
. At this order in
t
, such terms can be removed by making a field redefinition involving
f
and
\psi_{-}
. If this can be repeated order-by-order in
t
, as we suspect should be the case, then the two flows are genuinely equivalent and give rise to deformed theories with the same energies.

Chapter 3
(2,2)
Supercurrent-Squared Deformations

In this chapter, we will extend the supercurrent-squared deformation of Chapter
2
to theories with more supersymmetry, focusing on the case of
(2,2)
theories. Our discussion is based on the paper “
{T\overline{T}}
Flows and
(2,2)
Supersymmetry”
[
Chang:2019kiu
]
.

1
D=2\;\,{\cal N}=(2,2)
Supercurrent Multiplets

As in the case of
(1,1)
theories and
(0,1)
theories considered in the previous chapter, the manifestly supersymmetric construction of
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
for
(2,2)
theories is built from bilinears in fields of the supercurrent multiplet. In this section we review the structure of such multiplets in a general
D=2
theory with
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry.

1
The
{\cal S}
-multiplet

For Lorentz invariant supersymmetric theories, there is an essentially unique
supermultiplet which contains the stress-energy tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
, the supercurrent
S_{\mu\alpha}
, and no other operators
with spin larger than one, under the assumption that the multiplet be indecomposable
[
Dumitrescu:2011iu
]
. That is, though in general such a multiplet is not an irreducible representation of the supersymmetry algebra, it cannot be separated into a direct sum of supersymmetry multiplets. By “essentially unique,” we mean that this
{\cal S}
-multiplet
is unique up to improvement terms which preserve the superspace constraint equations. The
{\cal S}
-multiplet can be defined in any theory with
D=2

{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry.

For two-dimensional theories with
(2,2)
supersymmetry, the
{\cal S}
-multiplet consists of superfields

\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm}
,
\chi_{\pm}
, and
\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}
which satisfy the constraints:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\mathcal{S}_{\mp\mp}=\pm\left(\chi_{\mp}+\mathcal{Y}_{\mp}\right)~,

(1a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\chi_{\pm}=0~,\qquad\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\chi_{\mp}=\pm C^{(\pm)}~,\qquad D_{+}\chi_{-}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\chi}_{+}=k~,

(1b)

\displaystyle D_{\pm}\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}=0~,\qquad\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\mathcal{Y}_{\mp}=\mp C^{(\pm)}~,\qquad D_{+}\mathcal{Y}_{-}+D_{-}\mathcal{Y}_{+}=k^{\prime}~.

(1c)

Here
k
and
k^{\prime}
are real constants and
C^{(\pm)}
is a complex constant.
The
{\cal S}
-multiplet contains
8+8
independent real component operators and the constants
k,k^{\prime},C^{(\pm)}

[
Dumitrescu:2011iu
]
.
The expansion in components of
{\cal S}_{\pm\pm}
,

\chi_{\pm}
, and
\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}
are given
for convenience in Appendix

7.C
.

Among the various component fields
it is important to single out
the complex supersymmetry current
S_{\alpha}{}_{\mu}

and the energy-momentum tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
.
The complex supersymmetry current, associated to
S_{+\pm\pm}
and
S_{-\pm\pm}
, is conserved:
\partial^{\mu}S_{\alpha}{}_{\mu}=0
.
The energy-momentum tensor,
associated with
T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm}
and
T_{++--}=T_{--++}
, is real, conserved (
\partial^{\mu}T_{\mu\nu}=0
),
and symmetric (
T_{\mu\nu}=T_{\nu\mu}
).
In light-cone notation the conservation equations are given by

\displaystyle\partial_{++}S_{+--}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{--}S_{+++}(x)\,,

(2a)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\bar{S}_{+--}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{--}\bar{S}_{+++}(x)\,,

(2b)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}T_{----}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{--}\Theta(x)\,,

(2c)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Theta(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\partial_{--}T_{++++}(x)\,,

(2d)

where we have defined as usual

\displaystyle\Theta(x):=T_{++--}(x)=T_{--++}(x)~.

(3)

To conclude this subsection, let us describe the ambiguity in the form of the
{\cal S}
-multiplet which is parametrized by a choice of
improvement term.
If
{\cal U}
is a real superfield, we are free to modify the
{\cal S}
-multiplet superfields as follows

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{\pm\pm}

\displaystyle\to{\cal S}_{\pm\pm}+[D_{\pm},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}]{\cal U}~,

(4a)

\displaystyle\chi_{\pm}

\displaystyle\to\chi_{\pm}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{\pm}{\cal U}~,

(4b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}-D_{\pm}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}{\cal U}~,

(4c)

which keeps invariant the conservation equations (
1
).
In general the
{\cal S}
-multiplet is a reducible representation of supersymmetry and some of its component can consistently be set
to zero by a choice of improvement.
The reduced Ferrara-Zumino supercurrent multiplet, which plays a central role in this chapter, is described next.

2
The Ferrara-Zumino (FZ) multiplet and old-minimal supergravity

If there exists a well-defined superfield
{\cal U}
such that
\chi_{\pm}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{\pm}{\cal U}
, then we may
use the transformation (
4
) to set
\chi_{\pm}=0
in the
{\cal S}
-multiplet. If in addition
k=C^{(\pm)}=0
,
then we will rename
\mathcal{S}_{\pm\pm}
to
\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}
; then the fields
\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}
and
\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}
satisfy the constraints

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\mathcal{J}_{\mp\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\mathcal{Y}_{\mp}~,

(5a)

\displaystyle D_{\pm}\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0~,

(5b)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\mathcal{Y}_{\mp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0~,

(5c)

\displaystyle D_{+}\mathcal{Y}_{-}+D_{-}\mathcal{Y}_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle k^{\prime}~.

(5d)

These are the defining equations for the Ferrara-Zumino (FZ) multiplet

(\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm},\mathcal{Y}_{\pm})
. In this case,
\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}
turns out to be associated to the axial
U(1)_{A}

R
-symmetry current and satisfies the conservation equation

\partial_{--}{\cal J}_{++}-\partial_{++}{\cal J}_{--}=0~.

(6)

This multiplet, which has
4+4
real components,
is the dimensionally-reduced version of the
D=4

{\cal N}=1
FZ-multiplet
[
Ferrara:1974pz
]
;
see Appendix
7.C
for more details. All of the models we consider in section
3
have the property that

\chi_{\pm}
can be improved to zero; that is, they all have a well-defined FZ-multiplet.

In Chapter
2
, we obtained the components of the supercurrent superfield by finding the Noether currents associated with translations in superspace. One could use a similar Noether procedure in the case
(2,2)
superspace, as is done for
4D\,\mathcal{N}=1
in
[
Magro:2001aj
]
. However, we will find it more convenient to avoid the Noether procedure in this chapter and instead use the supersymmetrized version of the Hilbert definition of the stress tensor. Just as the bosonic Hilbert stress tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
represents the response function of the Lagrangian to a linearized perturbation
h_{\mu\nu}
of the metric, the supercurrent multiplets correspond to linearized couplings to supergravity. Therefore, we can compute the components of the supercurrent by coupling our
(2,2)
theories to one of the formulations of
2D
supergravity, which we now briefly review.

Different formulations of off-shell supergravity couple to different supercurrent multiplets. If a theory has a well-defined FZ-multiplet, as is the case for all the examples found in section
3
, then the theory can be consistently coupled to the old-minimal supergravity prepotentials
H^{\pm\pm}
and
\sigma
. The nomenclature “old-minimal” is again inherited from
D=4

{\cal N}=1
supergravity; see
[
Gates:1983nr
,
Buchbinder:1998qv
]
for pedagogical reviews and references. Here
H^{\pm\pm}
is the conformal supergravity prepotential—the analogue of the traceless part of the metric—and
\sigma
is a chiral conformal compensator.

We refer the reader to
[
Grisaru:1994dm
,
Grisaru:1995dr
,
Grisaru:1995kn
,
Grisaru:1995py
,
Gates:1995du
]

and references therein for an exhaustive description of
D=2

\,{\cal N}=(2,2)
off-shell supergravity in superspace, which we will use in
our analysis; see also Appendix
7.D
. For the scope of this work, it will be enough to know the
structure of linearized old-minimal supergravity. For instance, at the linearized level the gauge symmetry of the supergravity
prepotentials
H^{\pm\pm},\,\sigma
and
\bar{\sigma}
, can be parameterized as follows

\displaystyle\delta H^{++}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}L^{+}-D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{+}\right)~,

(7a)

\displaystyle\delta H^{--}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}L^{-}-D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{-}\right)~,

(7b)

\displaystyle\delta\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(D_{+}L^{+}-D_{-}L^{-}\right)~,

(7c)

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\sigma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{i}{2}D_{-}D_{+}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{+}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{-}\right)~,

(7d)

in terms of unconstrained spinor superfields
L^{\pm}
and their complex conjugates.

The conservation law (
5
) for the FZ-multiplet can be derived by using the previous gauge transformations.
The linearized supergravity couplings for a given model are written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{linear}}

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(H^{++}\mathcal{J}_{++}+H^{--}\mathcal{J}_{--}\right)-\int d^{2}\theta\,\sigma\,\mathcal{V}-\int d^{2}\bar{\theta}\,\bar{\sigma}\,\overline{\mathcal{V}}~,

(8)

with
{\cal V}
a chiral superfield and
{\overline{\cal V}}
its complex conjugate. Assuming the matter superfields satisfy their equations of motion,
the change in the Lagrangian (
8
) under the gauge transformation (
7
) is

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{L}_{\text{linear}}

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(\delta H^{++}\mathcal{J}_{++}+\delta H^{--}\mathcal{J}_{--}\right)-\int d^{2}\theta\,\delta\sigma\,\mathcal{V}-\int d^{2}\bar{\theta}\,\delta\bar{\sigma}\,\overline{\mathcal{V}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}\int d^{4}\theta\,\Big\{\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}L^{+}-D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{+}\right)\mathcal{J}_{++}+\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}L^{-}-D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{-}\right)\mathcal{J}_{--}

\displaystyle\qquad~~~~~~~~~~~~-\left(D_{+}L^{+}-D_{-}L^{-}\right)\mathcal{V}-\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{+}-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muL\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{-}\right)\overline{\mathcal{V}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{2}\int d^{4}\theta\,\Big\{L^{+}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mathcal{J}_{++}+D_{+}\mathcal{V}\right)+L^{-}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mathcal{J}_{--}-D_{-}\mathcal{V}\right)+\text{c.c.}\Big\}~,

(9)

where we have integrated by parts. Demanding that the variation vanishes for any gauge parameter
L^{\pm}
gives

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mathcal{J}_{++}+D_{+}\mathcal{V}=0~,~~~~~~\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mathcal{J}_{--}-D_{-}\mathcal{V}=0~.

(10)

This matches the constraints (
5
) for the FZ-multiplet if we identify

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\pm}=D_{\pm}\mathcal{V}~,

(11)

and set
k^{\prime}=0
.

As we will soon see, studying
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations requires consideration of a
composite operator constructed out of the square of the supercurrent multiplet.
Hence to solve the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow equations we need to be able to calculate the supercurrent multiplet explicitly.
The coupling to supergravity provides a straightforward prescription for computing the FZ-multiplet for matter models
that can be coupled to old-minimal supergravity.
1
1

1

Though we will not need it in this thesis, it is worth mentioning that the non-minimal supergravity results of

[
Grisaru:1994dm
,
Grisaru:1995dr
,
Grisaru:1995kn
,
Grisaru:1995py
,
Gates:1995du
]
allow the computation of the supercurrent
multiplet for more general classes of models.

In particular, for a given
{\cal N}=(2,2)
matter theory we will:

1.

Begin with an undeformed superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
in flat
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace.

2.

Minimally couple
\mathcal{L}
to the supergravity superfield prepotentials
H^{\pm\pm}
,
\sigma
and
\bar{\sigma}
.

3.

Extract the superfields
\mathcal{J}^{\pm\pm}
,
\mathcal{V}
and
\overline{\mathcal{V}}

which couple linearly to
H^{\pm\pm}
,

\sigma
and
\bar{\sigma}
, respectively, in the D-
and F-terms of (
8
).

Thanks to the analysis given above, the superfields
\mathcal{J}^{\pm\pm}
,
\mathcal{V}
and
\overline{\mathcal{V}}

will automatically satisfy the FZ-multiplet constraints (
10
).
A detailed description of the computation of the FZ-multiplet for the models relevant for our discussion is given in Appendix

7.D
.

2
The
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
Operator and
{\cal N}=(2,2)
Supersymmetry

After having reviewed in the previous section the structure of the
{\cal S}
-multiplet, we are
ready to describe
{\cal N}=(2,2)

T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations.

1
The
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
operator

Given a
D=2

{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetric theory with an
{\cal S}
-multiplet,
we define the supercurrent-squared deformation of this theory, denoted
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
in analogy with
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
, by the flow equation

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu~,

(1)

where
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
is constructed from current bilinears with

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle\equiv-\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{S}_{++}\mathcal{S}_{--}-\left(\int d\theta^{-}d\theta^{+}\,\chi_{+}\chi_{-}+\int d\overline{\theta}^{-}d\theta^{+}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{Y}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mathcal{Y}_{-}+\text{c.c.}\right)~,

(2)

and where the factor of
\frac{1}{8}
is chosen for later convenience.
This deformation generalizes the results we recently obtained for
D=2
theories possessing
{\cal N}=(0,1)
,
{\cal N}=(1,1)
and

{\cal N}=(0,2)
supersymmetry
[
Chang:2018dge
,
Baggio:2018rpv
,
Jiang:2019hux
]
to theories with
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry.

Let us recall the form of the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
composite operator
[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
, which we denote

\displaystyle T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)=T_{++++}(x)\,T_{----}(x)-\big[\Theta(x)\big]^{2}~.

(3)

An important property of the
{\cal N}=(0,1)
,
{\cal N}=(1,1)
and
{\cal N}=(0,2)
cases is that the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
operator turns out to be
the bottom component of a long supersymmetric multiplet. This is true up to both total vector derivatives (
\partial_{++}
and
\partial_{--}
),
and terms that vanish upon using the supercurrent conservation equations (Ward identities). For this reason, in the supersymmetric
cases studied previously, the original
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of
[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
is manifestly supersymmetric and
equivalent to the deformations constructed in terms of the full superspace integrals of primary supercurrent-squared composite
operators
[
Chang:2018dge
,
Baggio:2018rpv
,
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

Remarkably, despite the much more involved structure of the
(2,2)

{\cal S}
-multiplet compared to theories with fewer
supersymmetries, it is possible to prove that the following relation holds:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)=T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)+{\rm EOM^{\prime}s}+\partial_{++}(\cdots)+\partial_{--}(\cdots)~.

(4)

In (
4
), we use
{\rm EOM^{\prime}s}
to denote terms that are identically zero when (
1
) are used.
Showing (
4
) requires using (
6
)–(
12
), along with several cancellations, integration by parts
and the use of the
(2,2)

{\cal S}
-multiplet conservation equations
(
1
).

In fact, the specific combination of current superfields given in (
2
) was chosen precisely for (
4
) to hold.
The combination (
4
) is also singled out by being invariant under the improvement transformation
(
4
).
The important implication of (
4
) is that the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation for an
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetric quantum field
theory is manifestly supersymmetric and equivalent to the
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of eq. (
2
).

Note that in the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
case the deformation we have introduced in (
2
) is conceptually different from the cases
with less supersymmetry. Specifically, the deformation is not given by the descendant of a
single
composite superfield.
On the other hand, suppose the
{\cal S}
-multiplet is such that
C^{(\pm)}=k=k^{\prime}=0
and it is possible to improve the superfields

\chi_{\pm}
and
{\cal Y}_{\pm}
to a case where

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{\pm}=D_{\pm}{\cal V}~,~~~\overline{{\cal Y}}_{\pm}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{{\cal V}}~,

(5a)

\displaystyle\chi_{+}=i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\cal B}~,~~~\chi_{-}=i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}{\cal B}~,~~~\bar{\chi}_{+}=-iD_{+}{\cal B}~,~~~\bar{\chi}_{-}=-iD_{-}\overline{\cal B}~,

(5b)

with
{\cal V}
chiral and
{\cal B}
twisted-chiral:

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}{\cal V}=0~,~~~D_{\pm}\overline{\cal V}=0~,

(6a)

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}{\cal B}=D_{-}{\cal B}=0~,~~~D_{+}\overline{\cal B}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\cal B}=0~.

(6b)

In this case (
2
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{S}_{++}\mathcal{S}_{--}+\left(\int d\theta^{-}d\theta^{+}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\cal B}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\cal B}-\int d\overline{\theta}^{-}\,d\theta^{+}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\mathcal{V}}D_{-}\mathcal{V}+\text{c.c.}\right)

(7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(\mathcal{S}_{++}\mathcal{S}_{--}-2\mathcal{B}\overline{\mathcal{B}}-2\mathcal{V}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right)~,

and we see that, up to EOM’s,
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
is the bottom component of a long supersymmetric multiplet.
In this situation, once we define the composite superfield

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta):=-\mathcal{S}_{++}(\zeta)\mathcal{S}_{--}(\zeta)+2\mathcal{B}(\zeta)\overline{\mathcal{B}}(\zeta)+2\mathcal{V}(\zeta)\overline{\mathcal{V}}(\zeta)~,

(8)

eq. (
4
) turns into the equivalent result
2
2

2

In the subsequent discussion by
\theta=0
we will always mean
\theta^{\pm}={\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}=0
.

\displaystyle\int d^{4}\theta\,{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)=D_{-}D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)+{\rm EOM^{\prime}s}+\partial_{++}(\cdots)+\partial_{--}(\cdots)~,

(9)

stating that the D-term of the operator
{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)
is equivalent to the standard
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
operator.

For a matter theory that can be coupled to old-minimal supergravity, leading to the FZ-multiplet described by (
10
),
the operator
{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)
further simplifies thanks to the fact that the twisted-(anti-)chiral operators
{\cal B}
and
\overline{{\cal B}}

disappear. For these cases, the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow turns into the following equation

\partial_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}=\frac{1}{8}\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(\mathcal{J}_{++}\mathcal{J}_{--}-2\mathcal{V}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right)~.

(10)

This will be our starting point in analyzing
{\cal N}=(2,2)
deformed models in section
3
.

2
Point-splitting and well-definedness

The
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
operator (
3
) is quite magical because it is a well-defined irrelevant composite local operator,
free of short distance divergences
[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
. In fact, this property generalizes to the larger class of operators

\displaystyle[A_{s}(x)\,A^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}}(x)-B_{s+2}(x)\,B_{s^{\prime}-2}(x)]

(11)

where
(A_{s},B_{s+2})
and
(A^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}},B^{\prime}_{s^{\prime}-2})
are two pairs of conserved currents with spins
s
and
s^{\prime}
. The operator

T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
is a particular example with
s=s^{\prime}=0
. As proven in
[
Smirnov:2016lqw
]
, these composite operators of
“Smirnov-Zamolodchikov”-type have a well-defined point splitting which is free of short-distance divergences. In the case of

{\cal N}=(0,1)
and
{\cal N}=(1,1)
supersymmetric
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations, the entire supermultiplet whose bottom component is

T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
is comprised of well-defined Smirnov-Zamolodchikov-type operators
[
Baggio:2018rpv
,
Chang:2018dge
]
. In the

{\cal N}=(0,2)
case, the primary
3
3

3

We denote as primary operator the top component of a supersymmetric multiplet even when the theory is not
superconformal.

operator whose bottom component is
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
is not of Smirnov-Zamolodchikov-type. Nevertheless, also in this case it was recently
shown that, thanks to supersymmetry, the whole multiplet is well-defined
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

In the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
case it is clear that the situation is more complicated than any of the cases mentioned above.
First, in the general situation, according to (
2
), the
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation is a linear combination of a D-term together
with chiral and twisted-chiral F-terms contributions. Though the F-terms might be protected by standard perturbative
non-renormalization theorems (see, for example,
[
Gates:1983nr
,
Buchbinder:1998qv
]
for the
D=4

{\cal N}=1
case which
dimensionally reduces to
D=2

{\cal N}=(2,2)
), the D-term associated to the
{\cal S}_{++}{\cal S}_{--}
operator has no clear reason to be
protected in general from short-distance divergences in point-splitting regularization, and hence has no obvious reason to be
well-defined. This indicates that there might be a clash between supersymmetry and a point-splitting procedure in the general setting.

We will not attempt to analyze this issue in full generality in the current work; instead our aim is to describe a subclass of models
for which the
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation turns out to be well-defined. A natural restriction to impose is that the
{\cal S}
-multiplet is
constrained by (
5
) and the
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation is therefore described by the D-term
(
7
). By trivially extending the arguments used in
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
for the
{\cal N}=(0,2)
case, it is not difficult to
show that these restrictions are sufficient to imply that the multiplet described by the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
primary operator
{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)
of
(
8
) is indeed well-defined despite not being of Smirnov-Zamolodchikov-type. As in the
{\cal N}=(0,2)
,
unbroken
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry turns out to be the reason for this to happen.

Let us quickly explain how this works for the FZ-multiplet and the deformation (
10
), which are the main players in this chapter. Note, however, that the same argument extends to more general cases where both chiral and twisted-chiral
current superfields,
\chi_{\pm}
and
{\cal Y}_{\pm}
,
satisfying (
5
)
are turned on.
We also refer to
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
for details that we will skip in the following discussion, which are trivial extensions from the

(0,2)
to the
(2,2)
case.

A first indication of the well-definedness of the multiplet associated to
{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)
comes by looking at the vacuum expectation
value of its lowest component. Define the primary composite operator

\displaystyle O(x):=-j_{--}(x)j_{++}(x)+2v(x)\bar{v}(x)={\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(12)

and its point-split version

\displaystyle O(x,x^{\prime}):=-j_{--}(x)j_{++}(x^{\prime})+v(x)\bar{v}(x^{\prime})+\bar{v}(x)v(x^{\prime})~,

(13)

where

\displaystyle j_{\pm\pm}(x):={\cal J}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,~~~v(x):={\cal V}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,~~~\bar{v}(x):=\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~.

(14)

Note that equation (
10
) implies the following relation among the component operators

\displaystyle\big[\overline{Q}_{\pm},j_{\mp\mp}(x)\big]=\pm\big[Q_{\mp},v(x)\big]~,~~~\big[Q_{\pm},j_{\mp\mp}(x)\big]=\pm\big[\overline{Q}_{\mp},\bar{v}(x)\big]~,

(15)

with
Q_{\pm}
and
\overline{Q}_{\pm}
denoting the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supercharges.
4
4

4

Given an operator
F(x)
defined as the
\theta=0
component of the superfield
{\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)
,
F(x):={\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}
,
then its supersymmetry transformations are such that
\big[Q_{\pm},F(x)\big\}={\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}{\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=D_{\pm}{\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}

and

\big[\overline{Q}_{\pm},F(x)\big\}=\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\pm}{\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=\overline{D}_{\pm}{\mathcal{F}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}
.

By then using
\partial_{\pm\pm}=i\{Q_{\pm},\overline{Q}_{\pm}\}
,
\{Q_{+},Q_{-}\}=\{\overline{Q}_{+},\overline{Q}_{-}\}=0
,

[\overline{Q}_{\pm},v(x)]=[Q_{\pm},\bar{v}(x)]=0
,
super-Jacobi identities,
together with the conservation equations (
15
),
and the assumption that the vacuum is invariant under supersymmetry, it is straightforward to show that vacuum expectation value
of
O(x,x^{\prime})
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\left\langle j_{--}(x)j_{++}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left\langle\left[\{Q_{+},\overline{Q}_{+}\},j_{--}(x)\right]j_{++}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

(16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left\langle\left\{Q_{+},[Q_{-},v(x)]\right\}j_{++}(x^{\prime})+\left\{\overline{Q}_{+},[\overline{Q}_{-},\bar{v}(x)]\right\}j_{++}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\left\langle[Q_{+},v(x)][Q_{-},j_{++}(x^{\prime})]+[\overline{Q}_{+},\bar{v}(x)][\overline{Q}_{-},j_{++}(x^{\prime})]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left\langle[Q_{+},v(x)][\overline{Q}_{+},\bar{v}(x^{\prime})]+[\overline{Q}_{+},\bar{v}(x)][Q_{+},v(x^{\prime})]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\left[i\{\overline{Q}_{+},Q_{+}\},v(x)\right\}\bar{v}(x^{\prime})+\left[i\{Q_{+},\overline{Q}_{+}\},v(x)\right\}\bar{v}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\left\langle v(x)\bar{v}(x^{\prime})+v(x)\bar{v}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle~,

and, after performing a similar calculation for

\left\langle\partial_{--}j_{--}(x)j_{++}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle=-\left\langle j_{--}(x)\partial^{\prime}_{--}j_{++}(x^{\prime})\right\rangle
,
it is clear that the relation

\partial_{\pm\pm}\left\langle O(x,x^{\prime})\right\rangle=0

(17)

holds.
Therefore,
\left\langle O(x,x^{\prime})\right\rangle
is independent of the positions and free of short distance divergences.
It is worth noting that similarly to the argument showing that the two point function of two chiral or twisted-chiral operators is
independent of the positions
x
and
x^{\prime}
, the previous analysis for
\left\langle O(x,x^{\prime})\right\rangle
necessarily relies on unbroken

{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry.

The argument given above can be generalized to a statement about operators in superspace in complete analogy to the

{\cal N}=(0,2)
case of
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
. Let us investigate the short distance singularities in the bosonic coordinates by defining a
point-split version of the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
primary
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
operator,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta):=-{\cal J}_{--}(x,\theta)\,{\cal J}_{++}(x^{\prime},\theta)+{\cal V}(x,\theta)\,\overline{{\cal V}}(x^{\prime},\theta)+\overline{{\cal V}}(x,\theta)\,{\cal V}(x^{\prime},\theta)~.

(18)

We want to show that the preceding bilocal superfield is free of short distance divergences in the limit
x\to x^{\prime}
.
A straightforward calculation shows that

\displaystyle\hskip-12.0pt\partial_{++}{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big\{iD_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta)\left[D^{\prime}_{-}{\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]+i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}_{-}{\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})+D^{\prime}_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]

(19)

\displaystyle+i({\mathcal{Q}}_{+}+{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{+})\left[(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)){\cal V}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]+i(\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{+}+\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\prime}_{+})\left[(D_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta))\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+i({\mathcal{Q}}_{-}+{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{-})\left[(D_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta)){\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]+i(\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{-}+\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\prime}_{-})\left[(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)){\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle+(\partial_{++}+\partial^{\prime}_{++})\left[{\bar{\theta}}^{+}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)){\cal V}(\zeta^{\prime})+{\bar{\theta}}^{-}(D_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta)){\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle-(\partial_{++}+\partial^{\prime}_{++})\left[\theta^{+}(D_{+}{\cal V}(\zeta))\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta^{\prime})+\theta^{-}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)){\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta^{\prime})\right]\Big\}\Big|_{\theta=\theta^{\prime}}~.

Note that the first line in the preceding expression
is zero because of the FZ conservation equations (
10
), which hold up to contact terms in correlation functions.
The other lines are either total vector derivatives or
supersymmetry transformations of bilocal operators. A
similar equation holds for
\partial_{--}{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)
showing
that the operator
{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{\pm\pm}{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,+\,\textrm{EOM's}\,+\,[P,\cdots]\,+\,[Q,\cdots]~,

(20)

where
[P,\cdots]
and
[Q,\cdots]
schematically indicate a translation and supersymmetry transformation of some bilocal superfield
operator.
5
5

5

See Appendix A of
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
for the relation between the operators
({\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}+{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{\pm})
,

(\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{\pm}+\overline{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\prime}_{\pm})
and the generators of supersymmetry transformations on bilocal superfields such as

{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)
. The extension of that analysis from
{\cal N}=(0,2)
to
{\cal N}=(2,2)
is straightforward.

To conclude, by employing an OPE argument completely analogous to the one originally given by Zamolodchikov in

[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
and extended to the
{\cal N}=(0,2)
supersymmetric case in
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
, one can show that
eq. (
20
) implies

{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)={\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)\,+\,{\rm derivative~terms}~.

(21)

Here “derivative terms” indicate superspace covariant derivatives

D_{A}=(\partial_{\pm\pm},D_{\pm},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm})

acting on local superfield operators while
{\mathcal{O}}(\zeta)
arises from the regular, non-derivative part of the OPE of
{\mathcal{O}}(x,x^{\prime},\theta)
.
As a result the integrated operator

\displaystyle S_{{\mathcal{O}}}=\int d^{2}x\,d^{4}\theta\,\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}{\mathcal{O}}(x,x+\varepsilon,\theta)=\int d^{2}x\,d^{4}\theta:{\mathcal{O}}(x,x,\theta):~,

(22)

which can be considered as a definition of the integrated

\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
operator,
6
6

6

Note that, consistently, one can show that

\{Q_{+},[\overline{Q}_{+},\{Q_{-},[\overline{Q}_{-},O(x,x^{\prime})]\}]\}=T_{----}(x)T_{++++}(x^{\prime})-\Theta(x)\Theta(x^{\prime})+\textrm{EOM's}+[P,\cdots]

(23)

implying
that the descendant of the point-split
primary operator
O(x)
is equivalent, up to Ward identities and total vector derivatives (
\partial_{\pm\pm}
), to
the point-split version of the descendant
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
operator.

is free of short distance divergences and well-defined in complete analogy to the non-supersymmetric case

[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
and the
{\cal N}=(0,1)
,
{\cal N}=(1,1)
, and
{\cal N}=(0,2)
cases

[
Baggio:2018rpv
,
Chang:2018dge
,
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

3
Deformed
(2,2)
Models

In this section, we will apply our supercurrent-squared deformation (
10
) to a few examples of

\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
supersymmetric theories for a chiral multiplet
\Phi
. The superfield
\Phi
can be written in components as

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi+\theta^{+}\psi_{+}+\theta^{-}\psi_{-}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}F-i\theta^{+}\overline{\theta}^{+}\partial_{++}\phi-i\theta^{-}\overline{\theta}^{-}\partial_{--}\phi

(1)

\displaystyle-i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\overline{\theta}^{-}\partial_{--}\psi_{+}-i\theta^{-}\theta^{+}\overline{\theta}^{+}\partial_{++}\psi_{-}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\overline{\theta}^{-}\overline{\theta}^{+}\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\phi~,

where
\phi
is a complex scalar field,
\psi_{\pm}
are Dirac fermions, and
F
is a complex auxiliary field.
The multiplet
\Phi
satisfies the chirality constraint
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\Phi=0
.

We denote the physical Lagrangian by
\mathcal{L}
and the superspace D-term Lagrangian by
\mathcal{A}
, so that

\displaystyle S=\int d^{2}x\,\mathcal{L}=\int d^{2}x\,d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{A}~.

(2)

A broad class of
two-derivative theories for a chiral superfield can be described by superspace Lagrangians of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\int d^{4}\theta\,K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})+\int d^{2}\theta\,W(\Phi)+\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\Phi})~,

(3)

where
K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})
is a real function called the Kähler potential and
W(\Phi)
is a holomorphic function called the
