superpotential. These are
{\cal N}=(2,2)
Landau-Ginzburg models. In order for the kinetic terms of the component fields of
\Phi
to
have the correct sign, we will assume that
K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}=\frac{\partial^{2}K}{\partial\Phi\partial\overline{\Phi}}
is positive.

Although we will not expand on this point in detail, all the results found in this section can be derived almost identically for the case of
a generic model of a single scalar twisted-chiral superfield
\mathcal{Y}
,

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mathcal{Y}=D_{-}\mathcal{Y}=0
,
and its conjugate. This is not surprising since theories containing only chiral superfields are physically equivalent to theories
formulated in terms of twisted-chiral superfields; see,
for example,
[
Grisaru:1994dm
,
Grisaru:1995dr
,
Grisaru:1995kn
,
Grisaru:1995py
,
Gates:1995du
]

for a discussion of this equivalence in models with global and local supersymmetry. There are also many more involved
(2,2)

theories that one might also want to study involving chiral, twisted-chiral and semi-chiral superfields; see,
for example,
[
Caldeira:2018ynv
]
for a recent discussion and references.
For this analysis, we have chosen to consider only models based on a single
chiral multiplet.

1
Kähler potential

First we will set the superpotential
W
to zero and begin with an undeformed superspace Lagrangian of the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=\int d^{4}\theta\,K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})

(4)

for some Kähler potential
K
. To leading order around this undeformed theory, the FZ supercurrents are

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}D_{\pm}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi}~,

(5a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0~,

(5b)

where
K_{\Phi}=\frac{\partial K}{\partial\Phi},K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}=\frac{\partial^{2}K}{\partial\Phi\partial\overline{\Phi}}
, etc.
Therefore, at first order the supercurrent-squared deformation driven by

{\mathcal{O}}=\left(-\mathcal{S}_{++}\mathcal{S}_{--}+2\mathcal{V}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right)

will source a four-fermion contribution in the D-term, giving

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(1)}=\mathcal{L}^{(0)}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}~.

(6)

Next, we would like to find the all-orders solution for the deformed theory. We make the ansatz that, at finite deformation parameter

\lambda
, the Lagrangian takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\int d^{4}\theta\Big\{K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})+f(\lambda,x,\overline{x},y)K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\Big\}~,

(7)

where we define the combinations

\displaystyle x=K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}~,\qquad y=K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)~.

(8)

Using the results in Appendix

7.D
, one finds that the
superfields
\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}
and
\mathcal{V}
appearing in
our supercurrent-squared deformation, computed for the Lagrangian
(
7
),
are given by

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\mathcal{J}_{++}

\displaystyle=2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\left[1+f(x+\overline{x}-3y)+x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}(\overline{x}-y)+\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}(x-y)+y\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x+\overline{x}-2y)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}\left[-f-x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+y\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-2iK_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\left[-f+(x-\overline{x})\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+(x-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-2iK_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\left[f+(x-\overline{x})\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+(y-\overline{x})\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]~,

(9)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\mathcal{J}_{--}

\displaystyle=2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\left[1+f(x+\overline{x}-3y)+x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}(\overline{x}-y)+\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}(x-y)+y\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(x+\overline{x}-2y)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}\left[-f-x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+y\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad-2iK_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\left[-f+(\overline{x}-x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+(\overline{x}-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-2iK_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\left[f+(\overline{x}-x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+(y-x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]~,

(10)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-25.0pt\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle=2K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}\left(f+y\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}\right)\Big[-i\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}+\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi D_{+}\Phi

\displaystyle\quad-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}-i\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\Big]~.

(11)

The supercurrent-squared flow then induces a differential equation for the superspace Lagrangian

\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
(where, again,
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}
) given by

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\lambda}\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{8}{\mathcal{O}}=\frac{1}{8}\left(\mathcal{J}_{++}\mathcal{J}_{--}-2\mathcal{V}\overline{\mathcal{V}}\right)~.

(12)

Given our ansatz (
7
), we see that

\displaystyle\frac{d\mathcal{A}_{\lambda}}{d\lambda}=\frac{df}{d\lambda}K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}\,D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}~.

(13)

On the other hand, plugging in our expressions (
9
), (
10
), and (
11
)
for the supercurrents into the right hand side of (
12
) also gives a result proportional to

K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
. Equating the coefficients, we find a differential equation for
f
:

\displaystyle\hskip-15.0pt\frac{df}{d\lambda}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{2}\Bigg\{-\overline{x}y\left[f+(\overline{x}-x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+(y-x)\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]^{2}-xy\left[f+(x-\overline{x})\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+(y-\overline{x})\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+2(x-y)(y-\overline{x})\left[f+y\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right]^{2}+x\overline{x}\left[f+(\overline{x}-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+(x-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[1+(x+\overline{x}-3y)f+(x+\overline{x}-2y)y\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+\overline{x}(x-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}+x(\overline{x}-y)\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right]^{2}\Bigg\}~.

(14)

In particular, this shows that our ansatz (
7
) for the finite-
\lambda
superspace action is consistent:
the supercurrent-squared deformation closes on an action of this form. It could have been otherwise: the flow equation might have
sourced additional terms proportional, say, to two-fermion combinations
D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}
, or required dependence on other
dimensionless variables like
\lambda(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}
, but these complications do not arise in the case where the undeformed
theory only has a Kähler potential.

On dimensional grounds,
f
must be proportional to
\lambda
times a function of the dimensionless combinations
\lambda x

and
\lambda y
. Thus, although the differential equation for
f
determined by (
14
) is complicated,
one can solve order-by-order in
\lambda
. The solution to
\mathcal{O}(\lambda^{3})
is

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x,\overline{x},y)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{\lambda}{2}+\lambda^{2}\left(\frac{x+\overline{x}}{4}-\frac{3}{4}y\right)

(15)

\displaystyle+\lambda^{3}\left(\frac{x^{2}+\overline{x}^{2}+3x\overline{x}}{8}+\frac{37}{24}y^{2}-\frac{25}{24}\left(x+\overline{x}\right)y\right)+\cdots~.

(16)

We were unable to find a closed-form expression for
f
to all orders in
\lambda
. However, the differential equation
simplifies dramatically when we impose the equations of motion for the theory, and in this case one can write down an exact formula.
This is similar to the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow of the free action for a real
{\cal N}=(1,1)
scalar multiplet that was analyzed in

[
Baggio:2018rpv
,
Chang:2018dge
]
.

We claim that, on-shell, one may drop any terms where
y\sim(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi})
multiplies the
four-fermion term
|D\Phi|^{4}\equiv D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
. This is shown explicitly in Appendix

7.E
and follows directly from the superspace equation of motion and nilpotency of the fermionic terms

D_{\pm}\Phi
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi}
. It is also an intuitive statement associated to the
fact that for these models, on-shell,
{\cal N}=(2,2)

supersymmetry is not broken.
In fact, note that the superfields
(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)
and

(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi})
have as their lowest components the
auxiliary fields
F
and
\overline{F}
. If supersymmetry is not broken, the vev of
F
has to be zero,

\langle F\rangle=0
, which implies that
the auxiliary field
F
is on-shell at least quadratic
in fermions and, more precisely, can be proven
to be at least linear in
\psi_{\pm}=D_{\pm}\Phi|_{\theta=0}

and
\bar{\psi}_{\pm}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi}|_{\theta=0}
.
From this argument it follows that on-shell

(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)
is at least linear in

D_{\pm}\Phi
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi}
, and then the two conditions

(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)|D\Phi|^{4}=0
and
y|D\Phi|^{4}=0
follow.

After removing from (
14
)
the
y
-dependent terms which vanish on-shell,
we find a simpler differential equation
for the function
f
,

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{-x\overline{x}\left[f+x\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+\overline{x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}\right]^{2}+\left[1+(x+\overline{x})f+x\overline{x}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\overline{x}}\right)\right]^{2}\right\}~,

(17)

whose solution is

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x,\overline{x},y=0)=\frac{\lambda}{1-\frac{\lambda}{2}(x+\overline{x})+\sqrt{1-\lambda(x+\overline{x})+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\left(x-\overline{x}\right)^{2}}}~.

(18)

Thus we have shown that the supercurrent-squared deformed Lagrangian at finite
\lambda
is equivalent on-shell
to the following superspace Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})+\frac{\lambda K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}A+\sqrt{1-\lambda K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}A+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{4}B^{2}}}\right)~,

(19)

where

\displaystyle A=\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}+\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi~,\qquad B=\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi}-\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi~.

(20)

When
K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})=\overline{\Phi}\Phi
,
it is simple to show that this model represents
an
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)

off-shell supersymmetric extension of the
D=4
Nambu-Goto string in an appropriate gauge—
often referred to as a static gauge in presence of a
B
field, though it can be more naturally described as uniform light-cone
gauge
[
Arutyunov:2004yx
,
Arutyunov:2005hd
]
(see refs.
[
Baggio:2018gct
,
Frolov:2019nrr
]
for a discussion of this point).
In particular, by setting various component fields to zero and performing the superspace integrals, one can show that
(
19
) matches the expected answer for
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations in previously known non-supersymmetric cases.
For instance, setting the fermions to zero and integrating out the auxiliary fields

F
and
\overline{F}

gives the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of the complex free boson

\phi
, whose Lagrangian is

{\cal L}_{\lambda,\text{bos}}=\frac{\sqrt{1+2\lambda a+\lambda^{2}b^{2}}-1}{4\lambda}=\frac{a}{4}-\lambda\frac{\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}}{1+\lambda a+\sqrt{1+2\lambda a+\lambda^{2}b^{2}}}~,

(21)

where

a=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}+\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\phi~,\qquad b=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}-\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\phi~.

(22)

The Lagrangian (
21
) indeed describes the
D=4
light-cone gauge-fixed Nambu-Goto string model.

Alternatively, setting all the bosons to zero
in (
19
) can be shown to give the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of a complex free fermion.
These calculations are similar to those in the case of the
(0,2)
supercurrent-squared action,
which are presented in
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
. In fact, it can even be easily shown
that an
{\cal N}=(0,2)
truncation of (
19
)
gives precisely the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of a free
{\cal N}=(0,2)
chiral multiplet that was derived in
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

It is worth highlighting that, unlike the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
case,
an off-shell

(0,2)
chiral scalar multiplet contains only physical degrees
of freedom and no auxiliary fields.
Interestingly, related to this fact, it turns out that
(up to integration by parts and total derivatives)
the
{\cal N}=(0,2)
off-shell supersymmetric extension of the

D=4
Nambu-Goto string action in light-cone gauge is unique
and precisely matches the off-shell
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of a free

{\cal N}=(0,2)
chiral multiplet action
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

In the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
case, because of the presence of the auxiliary field
F
in the chiral multiplet
\Phi
,
there are an infinite set of inequivalent
{\cal N}=(2,2)
off-shell extensions of the Lagrangian (
21
) that are all equivalent
on-shell.
A representative of these equivalent actions is described by
(
19
) when
K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})=\overline{\Phi}\Phi
.

The non-uniqueness of dynamical systems described by actions
of the form (
19
)
can also be understood by noticing that, for example,
it is possible to perform a class of redefinitions that leaves the action (
19
) invariant on-shell.
As a (very particular) example,
note that we are free to perform a shift
of the form

\displaystyle D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)\longrightarrow D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)+a\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)^{2}

(23)

for any real number
a
. In terms of
A
and
B
, (
23
) implements the shifts

\displaystyle\begin{split}A\longrightarrow A+a\left(\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}+2y+\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)^{2}\right)~,\qquad B\longrightarrow B\end{split}

(24)

in (
19
).
The resulting Lagrangian would enjoy the same on-shell
simplifications described in Appendix
7.E

and would turn out to be on-shell equivalent to
the Lagrangian (
19
).
In this infinite set of on-shell
equivalent actions, a particular choice would represent
an exact solution of the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow equation
(
12
)–(
14
),
whose leading terms in a
\lambda
series expansion are given in (
16
).
Another representative in this on-shell equivalence class
is the simplified model described by (
19
).

These types of redefinition
and on-shell equivalentness
are not a surprise, nor really new.
In fact, they are of the same nature as redefinitions
that have been studied in detail in
[
GonzalezRey:1998kh
]

(see also
[
Kuzenko:2011tj
]
for a description of these
types of “trivial symmetries”)
in the context of

D=4

{\cal N}=1
chiral and linear superfield models possessing a non-linearly realised additional supersymmetry

[
Bagger:1997pi
,
GonzalezRey:1998kh
]
.
As in (
24
), the field redefinition in
this context does not affect the dynamics of the physical fields—
it basically corresponds only to an arbitrariness in the definition of the
auxiliary fields that always appear quadratically
in the action and then are set to zero (up to fermion terms
that will not contribute due to nilpotency in the action)
on-shell.
Although here we only focused on discussing the on-shell ambiguity
of the solution of the
{\cal N}=(2,2)

T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow,
we expect that the exact solution of the flow equations
with
y
nonzero (
12
)–(
14
)
can be found by a
field redefinition of the kind we made in the action (
19
).

It is also interesting to note that similar
freedoms and field redefinitions
are also described in the construction
of
D=4

{\cal N}=1
supersymmetric Born-Infeld actions; see, for example,
[
Bagger:1996wp
]
.
In fact, as will be analyzed in more detail elsewhere

[
SUSYDBITTbar
]
,
it can be shown that the Lagrangian (
19
)
is structurally of the type described by Bagger and Galperin
for the
D=4

{\cal N}=1
supersymmetric Born-Infeld action

[
Bagger:1996wp
]
.
The equivalence can be formally shown by identifying

W_{+}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}
,
W_{-}=D_{-}\Phi
,
W^{2}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi
, and

D^{\alpha}W_{\alpha}=D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}
to match their conventions.
As a consequence, we can show that our solution for the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow possesses a second non-linearly realised
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry, besides the
(2,2)
supersymmetry which is made manifest by the superspace construction, which is discussed in much greater detail in Chapter
4
. We note that the presence of a second supersymmetry is analogous to what happens in the
{\cal N}=(0,2)
case
[
Jiang:2019hux
]
.

2
Adding a superpotential

Now suppose we begin with an undeformed theory that has a superpotential
W(\Phi)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(0)}=\int d^{4}\theta\,K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})+\left(\int d^{2}\theta\,W(\Phi)\right)+\left(\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\,\overline{W}(\overline{\Phi})\right)~.

(25)

As shown in Appendix
7.D
, the superpotential F-term gives a contribution

\delta\mathcal{V}=2W(\Phi)
to the field
\mathcal{V}
which appears in supercurrent-squared.
To leading order in the deformation parameter, the Lagrangian takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(0)}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{L}^{(0)}+\mathcal{L}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}^{(0)}+\lambda\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(\frac{1}{2}K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}^{2}D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}+W(\Phi)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\Phi})\right)~.

(26)

In addition to the four-fermion term which we saw in section
1
, we see that the deformation modifies the
Kähler potential, adding a term proportional to
|W(\Phi)|^{2}
.

Next consider the second order term in
\lambda
. For convenience, we use the combination

|D\Phi|^{4}=D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
, which is the four-fermion combination that appeared at first order. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(2)}=\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4}\int d^{4}\theta\,\Big(x+\overline{x}-3y-2|W^{\prime}(\Phi)|^{2}+WD_{-}D_{+}+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\Big)|D\Phi|^{4}~.

(27)

The new terms involving supercovariant derivatives of
|D\Phi|^{4}
will generate contributions with two fermions in the D-term.

As we continue perturbing to higher orders, the form of the superspace Lagrangian becomes more complicated.
It is no longer true that the supercurrent-squared flow closes on a simple ansatz with one undetermined function,
as it did in the case with only a Kähler potential. Indeed, the finite-
\lambda
deformed superspace Lagrangian
in the case with a superpotential will depend not only on the variables
x
,
\overline{x}
,
and
y
as in section
1
,
but also, for example,
on combinations like
\partial_{++}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
, which can appear multiplying the two-fermion term

D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
in the superspace Lagrangian. To find the full solution, one would need to determine several functions contributing to the D-term—one multiplying the four-fermion term
|D\Phi|^{4}
as in the Kähler case; one for the deformed Kähler potential which may
now depend on
x
,
y
, and other combinations; and four functions multiplying the two-fermion terms

D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
,
D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
, etc. Each function can depend on several dimensionless combinations.

In the presence of a superpotential,
the situation might further be complicated by the fact that
supersymmetry can be spontaneously broken.
This would make it impossible, for example, to use on-shell
simplifications like
y|D\Phi|^{4}=0
that we employed in the section
1
, where
supersymmetry is never spontaneously broken.

It should be clear that the case with a superpotential is significantly
more involved and rich than just a pure Kähler potential.
In this case, we have not attempted to find a solution of the

T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow equation in closed form.
However, it is evident from the form of supercurrent-squared
eq. (
12
)—which is always written as a D-term integral of current bilinears—
that this deformation will only affect the
D
term and not the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superpotential
W
appearing in the chiral integral.
Therefore the superpotential, besides being protected from perturbative quantum corrections, is also protected from corrections
along the supercurrent-squared flow.

3
The physical classical potential

In view of the difficulty of finding the all-orders deformed superspace action for a theory with a superpotential,
we now consider the simpler problem of finding the local-potential approximation (or zero-momentum potential) for the bosonic complex scalar

\phi
contained in the superfield
\Phi
.
We stress that our analysis here is purely classical
and we will make a couple of
comments about possible quantum effects
later in this section.
For simplicity, we will also restrict to
the case in which the Kähler
potential is flat,
K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})=\overline{\Phi}\Phi
.
By “zero-momentum potential” we mean the physical potential

V(\phi)
which appears in the
Lagrangian after performing the superspace integral in the deformed theory and then setting
\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0
.
For instance, consider the undeformed Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{(0)}=\int d^{4}\theta\,\overline{\Phi}\Phi+\int d^{2}\theta\,W(\Phi)+\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\Phi})~.

(28)

When we ignore all terms involving derivatives and the fermions
\psi_{\pm}
, the only contributions to the physical Lagrangian
(after performing the superspace integral) come from an
|F|^{2}
term from the kinetic term, plus the term

W(\Phi)=W(\phi)+W^{\prime}(\phi)\theta^{+}\theta^{-}F
. This gives us the zero-momentum, zero-fermion component action

\displaystyle S=\int d^{2}x\,\left(|F|^{2}+W^{\prime}(\phi)F+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\overline{F}\right)~.

(29)

We may integrate out the auxiliary field
F
using its equation of motion
\overline{F}=-W^{\prime}(\phi)
, which yields

\displaystyle S=\int d^{2}x\,\left(-|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}\right)~,

(30)

so the zero-momentum potential for
\phi
is
V=|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}
, as expected.
Note that the previous potential might have extrema that breaks
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry
while supersymmetric vacua will always set

\langle F\rangle=\langle W^{\prime}(\phi)\rangle=0
. We will
assume supersymmetry of the undeformed theory
not to be spontaneously broken in our discussion.

Now suppose we deform by the supercurrent-squared operator to second order in
\lambda
,
which gives the superspace expression (
27
). If we again perform the superspace
integral and discard any terms involving derivatives or fermions, we now find the physical Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|F|^{2}+FW^{\prime}+\overline{F}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}+\lambda\left(\frac{1}{2}|F|^{4}-|F|^{2}|W^{\prime}|^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}|F|^{4}\left(W^{\prime}F+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}\overline{F}\right)

(31)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\lambda^{2}|W^{\prime}|^{2}|F|^{4}+\frac{3}{4}\lambda^{2}|F|^{6}~.

Remarkably, the equations of motion for the auxiliary
F

in (
31
) admit the solution

F=-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
,
\overline{F}=-W^{\prime}(\phi)
, which is the same
as the unperturbed solution.
This for instance implies that if we start
from a supersymmetric vacua in the undeformed theory we will
remain supersymmetric along the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow.
On the one hand, this
is not a surprise considering that we know the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

flow preserves the structure of the spectrum, and in particular should leave a zero-energy supersymmetric vacuum unperturbed.
On the other hand, it is a reassuring check to see
this property explicitly appearing in our analysis.

Returning to (
31
) and
integrating out the auxiliary fields gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=-|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\lambda|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{4}-\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{6}~.

(32)

These are the leading terms in the geometric series
\frac{-|W^{\prime}|^{2}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda|W^{\prime}|^{2}}
.
In fact, up to conventions for the scaling of
\lambda
, one could have predicted this outcome from the form of the
supercurrent-squared operator and the known results for
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations of a bosonic theory with a potential

[
Cavaglia:2016oda
]
.
We know that, up to terms which vanish on-shell, the effect of adding supercurrent-squared to the physical Lagrangian
is to deform by the usual
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
operator. However, in the zero-momentum sector, we see that the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation
reduces to deforming by the square of the potential:

\displaystyle T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=\mathcal{L}^{2}\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=V^{2}~.

(33)

Therefore, it is easy to solve for the deformed potential if we deform a physical Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=f(\lambda,\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi)+V(\lambda,\phi)
by
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
,
since the flow equation for the potential term is simply

\displaystyle\partial_{\lambda}\mathcal{L}=\frac{\partial V}{\partial\lambda}=V^{2}~,

(34)

which admits the solution

\displaystyle V(\lambda,\phi)=\frac{V(0,\phi)}{1-\lambda V(0,\phi)}~.

(35)

We can apply this result to the Lagrangian (
29
), treating the entire expression involving
the auxiliary field
F
as a potential (since it is independent of derivatives). The deformed theory has a zero-momentum
piece which is therefore equivalent to

\displaystyle S(\lambda)\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=\int d^{2}x\,\frac{\left(|F|^{2}+W^{\prime}(\phi)F+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\overline{F}\right)}{1-\lambda\left(|F|^{2}+W^{\prime}(\phi)F+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\overline{F}\right)}~,

(36)

at least on-shell. Integrating out the auxiliary now gives

\displaystyle S(\lambda)\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=\int d^{2}x\,\frac{-|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}}{1-\lambda|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}}

(37)

as the deformed physical potential. This matches the first few terms of (
32
),
up to a convention-dependent factor of
\frac{1}{2}
in the scaling of
\lambda
.

Now one might ask what superspace Lagrangian would yield the physical action (
37
)
after performing the
d\theta
integrals. One candidate is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda)\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}\sim\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(\overline{\Phi}\Phi-\lambda|W(\Phi)|^{2}\right)+\int d^{2}\theta\,W(\Phi)+\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\Phi})~,

(38)

where here
\sim
means “this superspace Lagrangian gives an equivalent zero-momentum physical potential for the boson

\phi
on-shell.”

It is important to note that (
38
) is
not
the true solution for the deformed superspace
Lagrangian using supercurrent-squared. The genuine solution involves a four-fermion term, all possible two-fermion terms, and more
complicated dependence on the variable
y=\lambda(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi})
in the zero-fermion term. However, if
one were to perform the superspace integral in the true solution and then integrate out the auxiliary field
F
using its equation of
motion, one would obtain the same zero-momentum potential for
\phi
as we find by performing the superspace integral in
(
38
) and integrating out
F
.

The form (
38
) is interesting because it shows that the effect of supercurrent-squared on the
physical potential for
\phi
can be interpreted as a change in the Kähler metric, which for this Lagrangian is

\displaystyle K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}=1-\lambda|W^{\prime}(\Phi)|^{2}~.

(39)

When one performs the superspace integrals in (
38
), the result is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}|F|^{2}+W^{\prime}(\phi)F+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)\overline{F}~,

(40)

which admits the solution
F=-\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)}{K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}}
. Substituting this solution gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\Big|_{\partial_{\pm\pm}\phi=0}=\frac{-|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}}{K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}}=\frac{-|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}}{1-\lambda|W^{\prime}(\phi)|^{2}}~,

(41)

which agrees with (
37
).

As already mentioned, supersymmetric vacua of the original, undeformed, theory are associated with critical points of the
superpotential
W(\phi)
. Any vacuum of the undeformed theory will persist in the deformed theory: near a point where

W^{\prime}(\phi)=0
, we see that the physical potential
V(\phi)=\frac{|W^{\prime}|^{2}}{1-\lambda|W^{\prime}|^{2}}
also vanishes (away from the pole

|W^{\prime}|^{2}=\frac{1}{\lambda}
, the deformed potential is a monotonically increasing function of
|W^{\prime}|^{2}
). Further, the auxiliary field
F

does not acquire a vacuum expectation value because
F=-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)
remains a solution to its equations of motion in the
deformed theory. Once more, this indicates that supersymmetry is unbroken along the whole
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow if is in the undeformed
theory.

However, this classical analysis suggests that the soliton spectrum of the theory has changed dramatically at any finite deformation
parameter
\lambda
. There are now generically poles in the physical potential
V(\phi)
at points where
|W^{\prime}|^{2}=\frac{1}{\lambda}

which might separate distinct supersymmetric vacua of the theory.
For instance, if the original theory had a
double-well superpotential
with two critical points
\phi_{1}
,
\phi_{2}
where

W^{\prime}(\phi_{i})=0
, then this undeformed theory supports BPS
soliton solutions which
interpolate between these two vacua. But if the superpotential

W
reaches a value of order
\frac{1}{\lambda}
at some point
between
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
, then this soliton solution appears
naively forbidden in the deformed theory because it requires
crossing an infinite potential barrier. Another way of seeing
this is by considering the effective Kähler potential (
39
),
which would change sign at some point between the two
supersymmetric vacua in the deformed theory and thus give rise
to a negative-definite Kähler metric.

Our discussion has been purely classical. As we emphasised in the introduction, a fully quantum analysis of this problem is desirable, though subtle because of the non-local nature of the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation.
7
7

7

For an analysis of correlation functions in theories deformed by supercurrent-squared operators, which may offer a first step towards understanding the quantum properties of these deformations, see
[
Ebert:2020tuy
]
.
The advantage of performing such an analysis in models with extended supersymmetry is that holomorphy and associated non-renormalization theorems provide control over the form of any possible quantum corrections. For example, the superpotential for the models studied in this work is not renormalized perturbatively along the flow. It would be interesting to examine the structure of perturbative quantum corrections along the lines of
[
Rosenhaus:2019utc
]
, but in superspace with manifest supersymmetry. It should be possible to absorb any quantum corrections visible in perturbation theory by a change in the
D
-term Kähler potential
meaning that at least the structure of the
supersymmetric vacua would be preserved.

Chapter 4
Non-Linearly Realized Symmetries

In this chapter, we will investigate certain additional symmetries which are present in some
{T\overline{T}}
and supercurrent-squared deformed models. The treatment of this chapter follows the paper “Non-Linear Supersymmetry and
{T\overline{T}}
-like Flows”
[
Ferko:2019oyv
]
.

It was briefly mentioned in Chapter
3
that our solution (
19
) for the supercurrent-squared deformation of a chiral multiplet possesses a second non-linearly realized supersymmetry – which we will demonstrate in more detail shortly – but there are already examples of such additional symmetries in the non-supersymmetric context. For instance, we reviewed in Section
2
that the
{T\overline{T}}
deformation of a free boson is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+2\lambda\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial^{\mu}\phi+1}-1\right)\,.

(1)

Ignoring the constant
-\frac{1}{2\lambda}
, this is of the same form as the Dirac Lagrangian which describes the transverse fluctuations
\phi
on a brane. The Dirac action for a
p
-brane is usually written

\displaystyle S_{\text{Dirac}}=-T_{p}\int d^{p+1}\xi\,\sqrt{-\det( \gamma)}\,,

(2)

where
T_{p}
is the tension of the
Dp
-brane and

\displaystyle\gamma_{ab}=\frac{\partial X^{\mu}}{\partial\xi^{a}}\frac{\partial X^{\nu}}{\partial\xi^{b}}\eta_{\mu\nu}

(3)

is the pullback of the target space metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}
onto the brane’s worldvolume. We can choose static gauge which identifies the
p+1
worldvolume coordinates
\xi^{a}
with the target space coordinates
X^{a}
, and the remaining worldvolume coordinates are transverse oscillations:

\displaystyle\begin{split}X^{a}&=\xi^{a}\,,\qquad\qquad\;\,a=0,\cdots,p\,,\\
X^{I}&=2\pi\alpha^{\prime}\phi^{I}(\xi)\,,\quad I=p+1,\cdots D-1\,.\end{split}

(4)

Here
D
is the total dimension of the target spacetime and
\alpha^{\prime}
is a parameter which is proportional to the square of the string length. In the case of a
D1
-string embedded in a three dimensional spacetime, so that
p=1
and
D=3
, the Dirac action can be written as

\displaystyle S_{\text{Dirac}}=-T_{p}\int d^{2}\xi\sqrt{1+2\pi\alpha^{\prime}\partial^{a}\phi\partial_{a}\phi}\,,

(5)

which is the same form as the
{T\overline{T}}
deformed Lagrangian (
1
). Here
\alpha^{\prime}
plays the role of the
{T\overline{T}}
parameter
\lambda
.

It is well-known that the
Dp
-brane Lagrangian (
2
) possesses a non-linearly realized symmetry for any
p
. We will describe this symmetry in the case (
5
) of a
D1
-string. In this case, for either fixed index
a\in\{0,1\}
, the action (
5
) is invariant under the transformation

\displaystyle\delta\phi=\xi^{a}+\phi\partial^{a}\phi\,.

(6)

The interpretation of this symmetry is that the presence of the brane as an embedded hypersurface spontaneously breaks part of the Poincaré symmetry of the ambient spacetime. The scalars
\phi
are Nambu-Goldstone bosons of this spontaneously broken symmetry, which is then non-linearly realized. One can view the transformation (
6
) as a rotation which rotates the transverse direction
\phi
into the worldvolume direction
\xi^{a}
, breaking static gauge, along with a compensating worldvolume diffeomorphism which then restores static gauge. This symmetry, and its extensions to the Dirac-Born-Infeld action which includes a gauge field, are nicely discussed in
[
Gliozzi:2011hj
,
Casalbuoni:2011fq
,
Maxfield:2016vpw
]
.

1
D=2\;\,{\cal N}=(2,2)
Flows and Non-Linear
{\cal N}=(2,2)
Supersymmetry

In this section, we are going to explore in detail how the non-linear supersymmetries – which we briefly alluded to in Chapter
3
– arise for the simplest
{\cal N}=(2,2)

{T\overline{T}}
flows. The undeformed models are supersymmetrized theories of free scalars, while the deformed models are
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetric extensions of the
D=4
gauge-fixed Nambu-Goto string.

1
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformations with
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry

In Chapter
3
, supersymmetric flows for various theories were studied; the conclusion of Section
2
was that
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
operator of a supersymmetric theory is related to a supersymmetric
descendant operator
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)=T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)+{\rm EOM}+\partial_{++}(\cdots)+\partial_{--}(\cdots)~.

(1)

The previous equation states the equivalence of
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
and
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)
up to total derivatives and terms that vanish on-shell, which we have indicated with “
{\rm EOM}
”.

The simplest cases of deformed models, on which we will focus in this section, are
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformed theories of free scalars, fermions and auxiliary fields. In the case of
D=2

{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry, a scalar multiplet can have several different off-shell representations

[
Gates:1984nk
,
Buscher:1987uw
,
Grisaru:1997pg
,
Lindstrom:2005zr
]
. The two cases we will consider here are chiral and twisted-chiral supermultiplets, which are the most commonly studied cases.

In
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace, parametrized by coordinates
\zeta^{M}=(x^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm})
, let
the complex superfields
X(x,\theta)
and
Y(x,\theta)
satisfy chiral
and twisted-chiral constraints, respectively,

\displaystyle\bar{D}_{\pm}X=0~,\quad\bar{D}_{+}Y=D_{-}Y=0~.

(2)

In this chapter, we use slightly different conventions for the supercovariant derivatives than in Chapter
3
. These were referred to as
D^{\prime}_{\pm}
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\prime}_{\pm}
in equation (
13
), but we will drop the primes in the following discussion. For convenience, the conventions are repeated here:

\displaystyle D_{\pm}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{\pm}}+i\bar{\theta}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}\,,\;\;\bar{D}_{\pm}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\theta}^{\pm}}-i\theta^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}~\;\;,

(3)

\displaystyle Q_{\pm}=i\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta^{\pm}}+\bar{\theta}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}~,\;\;\bar{Q}_{\pm}=-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\theta}^{\pm}}-\theta^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}\;\;.

(4)

These satisfy

\displaystyle D_{\pm}^{2}=\bar{D}_{\pm}^{2}=0~,\qquad\{D_{\pm},\bar{D}_{\pm}\}=-2i\partial_{\pm\pm}~,\qquad[D_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=[\bar{D}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=0~,\quad

(5)

\displaystyle Q_{\pm}^{2}=\bar{Q}_{\pm}^{2}=0~,\qquad\{Q_{\pm},\bar{Q}_{\pm}\}=-2i\partial_{\pm\pm}~,\qquad[Q_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=[\bar{Q}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=0~.\quad

(6)

There is one more caveat worth mentioning: in much of the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
literature, twisted-chiral multiplets, often denoted
\Sigma
in this context, naturally arise as field strengths for
{\cal N}=(2,2)
vector superfields
V
. The lowest component of such a superfield is a complex scalar, but the top component proportional to
\bar{\theta}^{-}\theta^{+}
encodes the gauge-field strength along with a real auxiliary field. On the other hand, there are twisted chiral superfields denoted
Y
whose bottom component is a complex scalar and whose top component is just a complex auxiliary field. In this work we restrict to the latter case.
The free Lagrangians for these supermultiplets are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{c}}_{0}=\int d^{4}\theta\,X\bar{X}~,\qquad\mathcal{L}^{\text{tc}}_{0}=-\int d^{4}\theta\,Y\bar{Y}~.

(7)

In
[
Chang:2019kiu
]
it was shown that the following Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{c}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{4}\theta\left(X\bar{X}+\frac{\lambda D_{+}X\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\bar{X}D_{-}X\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\bar{X}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda A+\sqrt{1-\lambda A+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}B^{2}}}\right)~,

(8a)

with

\displaystyle A=\partial_{++}X\partial_{--}\bar{X}+\partial_{++}\bar{X}\partial_{--}X~,\quad B=\partial_{++}X\partial_{--}\bar{X}-\partial_{++}\bar{X}\partial_{--}X~,

(8b)

is a solution of the flow equation (
10
) on-shell, and hence describes the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformation of the free chiral supermultiplet Lagrangian (
7
).

A simple way to generate
the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformation of the free twisted-chiral theory is to remember that a twisted-chiral multiplet can be obtained from a chiral one
by acting with a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

automorphism
on the Grassmann coordinates
of
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace:

\displaystyle\theta^{+}\leftrightarrow\theta^{+}~,\quad\theta^{-}\leftrightarrow-\,\bar{\theta}^{-}~.

(9)

This leaves the
D_{+}
and
\bar{D}_{+}
derivatives invariant while it exchanges
D_{-}
with
\bar{D}_{-}
. As a result,
the chiral and twisted-chiral differential constraints (
2
) are mapped into each others under the automorphism (
9
).
1
1

1

In the literature this
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
automorphism
(
9
) is often called the “mirror-map” or “mirror-image” because it exchanges the vector and axial
U(1)
R-symmetries.

Under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}

automorphism (
9
), the Lagrangian (
8a
) turns into the following twisted-chiral Lagrangian

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\text{tc}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int d^{4}\theta\left(Y\bar{Y}+\frac{\lambda D_{+}Y\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\bar{Y}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}YD_{-}\bar{Y}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda A+\sqrt{1-\lambda A+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{2}B^{2}}}\right)~,

(10a)

where

\displaystyle A=\partial_{++}Y\partial_{--}\bar{Y}+\partial_{++}\bar{Y}\partial_{--}Y~,\quad B=\partial_{++}Y\partial_{--}\bar{Y}-\partial_{++}\bar{Y}\partial_{--}Y~.

(10b)

Thanks to the map (
9
), by construction the Lagrangian (
10a
) is a
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformation and its superspace Lagrangian

{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm tc}_{\lambda}
,

{\mathcal{L}}^{\rm tc}_{\lambda}=\int d^{4}\theta\,{\mathcal{A}}^{\rm tc}_{\lambda}
, is an on-shell solution of the following flow equation

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\lambda}\mathcal{A}^{\text{tc}}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{8}\left(\mathcal{{\mathcal{R}}}_{++}\mathcal{{\mathcal{R}}}_{--}-2\mathcal{B}\bar{\mathcal{B}}\right)~.

(11)

Here
{\mathcal{R}}_{\pm\pm}(x,\theta)
,
\mathcal{B}(x,\theta)
and its complex conjugate
\bar{\mathcal{B}}(x,\theta)

are the local operators describing the
{\mathcal{R}}
-multiplet of currents
for
D=2

{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry
that arise by applying (
9
) to the FZ multiplet of the chiral theory
(
8a
)
[
Chang:2019kiu
]
.
They satisfy the conservation equations,

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mathcal{R}_{--}=i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}{\cal B}~,\qquad D_{-}\mathcal{R}_{++}=iD_{+}{\cal B}~,\qquad\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}{\cal B}=D_{-}{\cal B}=0~,

(12)

together with their complex conjugates.
Like the case of the FZ-multiplet,
the supercurrent-squared operator

\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)=\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{O}^{{\mathcal{R}}}(x,\theta)~,\quad{\mathcal{O}}^{{\mathcal{R}}}(x,\theta):=-\mathcal{R}_{++}(x,\theta)\mathcal{R}_{--}(x,\theta)+2\mathcal{B}(x,\theta)\bar{\mathcal{B}}(x,\theta)~,

(13)

satisfies (
1
); namely,
\mathcal{T}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\mathcal{T}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(x)

is equivalent to
T\bar{T}(x)
up to total derivatives and
{\rm EOM}
, as we showed in Chapter
3
.

Note that the bosonic truncation of both (
8a
) and (
10a
) give the Lagrangian

{\cal L}_{\lambda,\text{bos}}=\frac{\sqrt{1+2\lambda a+\lambda^{2}b^{2}}-1}{4\lambda}=\frac{a}{4}-\lambda\frac{\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\phi\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}}{1+\lambda a+\sqrt{1+2\lambda a+\lambda^{2}b^{2}}}~,

(14)

where

a=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}+\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\phi~,\qquad b=\partial_{++}\phi\partial_{--}\bar{\phi}-\partial_{++}\bar{\phi}\partial_{--}\phi~,

(15)

and
\phi
is either
\phi=X|_{\theta=0}
or
\phi=Y|_{\theta=0}
.
This is the Lagrangian for the gauge-fixed Nambu-Goto string in four dimensions

[
Cavaglia:2016oda
]
.

The aim of the remainder of this section is to show
that the Lagrangians (
8a
) and (
10a
)
are structurally identical to the Bagger–Galperin action
for the
D=4

{\cal N}=1
supersymmetric Born-Infeld theory

[
Bagger:1996wp
]
, which we will analyse in detail in section
4
.
Since the Bagger–Galperin action possesses a second non-linearly realized
D=4
,
{\cal N}=1
supersymmetry,
we will show that the theories described by (
8a
) and (
10a
) also possess an extra set of non-linearly realized
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetries.

2
The
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformed twisted-chiral model and partial-breaking

Let us start with the twisted-chiral Lagrangian (
10a
)
which, as we will show, is the one more directly related to the
D=4
Bagger-Galperin action.
In complete analogy to the
D=4
case, we are going to show that (
10a
) is a model for a Nambu-Goldstone multiplet of
D=2

{\cal N}=(4,4)\to{\cal N}=(2,2)
partial supersymmetry breaking.
The analysis is similar in spirit to the
D=4
construction of the Bagger-Galperin action using
D=4

{\cal N}=2
superspace proposed by Roček and Tseytlin
[
Rocek:1997hi
]
; see also

[
Kuzenko:2015rfx
,
Antoniadis:2017jsk
,
Antoniadis:2019gbd
]
for more recent analysis.

To describe manifest
{\cal N}=(4,4)
supersymmetry we can use
{\cal N}=(4,4)
superspace which augments the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace coordinates
\zeta^{M}=(x^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})
of the previous section with
the following additional complex Grassmann coordinates
(\eta^{\pm},\bar{\eta}^{\pm})
.
The extra supercovariant derivatives and supercharges are given by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta^{+}}+i\bar{\eta}^{+}\partial_{++}~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{+}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\eta}^{+}}-i\eta^{+}\partial_{++}~,

(16a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta^{+}}+\bar{\eta}^{+}\partial_{++}~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{+}=-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\eta}^{+}}-\eta^{+}\partial_{++}~,

(16b)

with similar expressions for
{\mathcal{D}}_{-}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{-}
. They satisfy

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}^{2}=\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}^{2}=0~,\quad\{{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm},\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}\}=-2i\partial_{\pm\pm}~,\quad[{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=[\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=0~,~~~~~~

(17)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}^{2}=\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}^{2}=0~,\quad\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm},\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}\}=-2i\partial_{\pm\pm}~,\quad[{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=[\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm},\partial_{\pm\pm}]=0~,~~~~~~

(18)

while they (anti-)commute with all the usual
D_{\pm}
and
Q_{\pm}
operators.

Two-dimensional
{\cal N}=(4,4)
supersymmetry can also be usefully described in the language of
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace.
In this section, we will largely refer to
[
Ivanov:2004yv
]
for such a description.
In this approach from the full
(4,4)
supersymmetry, one copy of
(2,2)
is manifest while a second
(2,2)
is hidden.
For our goal of describing a model of partial supersymmetry breaking,
we view the hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry as broken and non-linearly realized.
We will derive such a description starting from
{\cal N}=(4,4)
superspace and describe the broken/hidden supersymmetry
using the
\eta^{\pm}
directions.

The hidden supersymmetry transformation of a generic
D=2

{\cal N}=(4,4)
superfield

U=U(x^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm},\eta^{\pm},\bar{\eta}^{\pm})
under the hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry is

\delta U=i(\epsilon^{+}{\mathcal{Q}}_{+}+\epsilon^{-}{\mathcal{Q}}_{-}-\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{+}-\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{-})U~.

(19)

The
(2,2)
supersymmetry, generated by the
Q_{\pm}
and
\bar{Q}_{\pm}
operators,
will always be manifest and preserved,
so we will not bother to discuss it in detail.
For convenience, we also introduce the chiral coordinate
y^{\pm\pm}=x^{\pm\pm}+i\eta^{\pm}\bar{\eta}^{\pm}
.
Using this coordinate, the spinor covariant derivatives and supercharges take the form

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta^{\pm}}+2i\bar{\eta}^{\pm}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{\pm\pm}}~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}=-\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\eta}^{\pm}}~,

(20a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta^{\pm}}~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{Q}}_{\pm}=-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\eta}^{\pm}}-2\eta^{\pm}\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{\pm\pm}}~.

(20b)

After this technical introduction, let us turn to our main construction.
Consider a
(4,4)
superfield which is chiral under the hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry:

\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}=0~.

(21)

We can expand it in terms of hidden fermionic coordinates,

{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}=X+\eta^{+}X_{+}+\eta^{-}X_{-}+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}F~,

(22)

where
X=X(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})
,

X_{\pm}=X_{\pm}(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})

and
F=F(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})

are themselves
(2,2)

superfields. In the following discussion, we will keep
the
\theta^{\pm},\,\bar{\theta}^{\pm}
dependence implicit.
The hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry transformation rules
can then be straightforwardly computed using (
19
) and (
22
).
They take the form

\displaystyle\delta X

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{+}X_{+}-\epsilon^{-}X_{-}~,

(23a)

\displaystyle\delta X_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp\epsilon^{\mp}F-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}X,

(23b)

\displaystyle\delta F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}X_{+}+2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}X_{-}~.

(23c)

The
\bm{\mathcal{X}}
superfield is still reducible under
{\cal N}=(4,4)
supersymmetry so we can put additional constraints on the
(2,2)
superfields
X
,
X_{\pm}
and
F
.
Here we will consider
(4,4)
twisted multiplets, and refer the reader to
[
Gates:1984nk
,
Gates:1983py
,
Gates:1995aj
,
Gates:1998fr
,
Ivanov:1984ht
,
Ivanov:1984fe
,
Ivanov:1987mz
]
for a more detailed analysis.
For this discussion, we will follow the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superspace description of
[
Ivanov:2004yv
]
.
One type of twisted multiplet with
(4,4)
supersymmetry can be defined by setting

X_{+}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y},\qquad X_{-}=-\bar{D}_{-}Y~,

(24)

where
X
and
Y
are chiral and twisted-chiral, respectively, under the manifest
(2,2)
supersymmetry:

\bar{D}_{+}X=\bar{D}_{-}X=\bar{D}_{+}Y=D_{-}Y=0~,\qquad D_{+}\bar{X}=D_{-}\bar{X}=D_{+}\bar{Y}=\bar{D}_{-}\bar{Y}=0~.

(25)

The superfield (
22
) becomes

{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}=X+\eta^{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\bar{Y}-\eta^{-}\bar{D}_{-}Y+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}F~.

(26)

The supersymmetry transformation rules then become

\displaystyle\delta X

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}+\epsilon^{-}\bar{D}_{-}Y~,

(27a)

\displaystyle\delta F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}\bar{D}_{-}Y~,

(27b)

while
\delta X_{\pm}
remains the same as (
23b
).
By using the conjugation property for two fermions,

\overline{\chi\xi}=\bar{\xi}\bar{\chi}=-\bar{\chi}\bar{\xi}
,
and the conjugation property
\overline{D_{+}A}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{A}

for a bosonic superfield
A
, it follows that

\delta\bar{X}=\bar{\epsilon}^{+}D_{+}Y-\bar{\epsilon}^{-}D_{-}\bar{Y}~.

(28)

One can check that

\delta\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}=\bar{D}^{2}\delta\bar{X}=\bar{D}^{2}\Big(\bar{\epsilon}^{+}D_{+}Y-\bar{\epsilon}^{-}D_{-}\bar{Y}\Big)=2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}\bar{D}_{-}Y+2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}\bar{D}_{+}Y~,

(29)

where
\bar{D}^{2}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{D}_{-}
. Note that in the first equality we made use of the fact that the manifest and hidden

(2,2)
supersymmetries are independent. The supersymmetry transformation rule for
-\bar{D}^{2}X
is then exactly that of the auxiliary field
F
. Thus we can consistently set

\displaystyle F=-\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}~,

(30)

which is the last constraint necessary to describe a version of the
(4,4)
twisted multiplet
in terms of a chiral and twisted-chiral
{\cal N}=(2,2)
superfields.
The resulting
(4,4)
superfield
{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}
,
expanded in terms of the hidden
(2,2)
fermionic coordinates, takes the form

{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}=X+\eta^{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\bar{Y}-\eta^{-}\bar{D}_{-}Y-\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}~,

(31)

which closely resembles the expansion of a
D=4

{\cal N}=2
vector multiplet when one identifies
the analogue of the
D=4

{\cal N}=1
chiral vector multiplet field strength
W_{\alpha}
with the
(2,2)
chiral superfields

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\bar{Y}

and

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}Y
.
Note in particular that
{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}
turns to be
(4,4)
chiral:

\bar{D}_{\pm}{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}=0~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}=0~.

(32)

To summarize: the entire
(4,4)
off-shell twisted multiplet is described in terms of one chiral and one twisted-chiral
(2,2)
superfield, which
possess the following hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry transformations:

\displaystyle\delta X

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}+\epsilon^{-}\bar{D}_{-}Y~,

(33a)

\displaystyle\delta Y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{\epsilon}^{-}D_{-}X+\epsilon^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}~.

(33b)

Let us now introduce the action for a free
{\cal N}=(4,4)
twisted multiplet.
Taking the square of
{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}
in (
31
) we obtain

{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}^{2}=\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(-2X\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}+2\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\bar{D}_{-}Y\Big)+\ldots~,

(34)

where the ellipses denote terms that are not important for our analysis.
Since
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}
and therefore
{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}
are chiral superfields, we can consider the chiral integral in the hidden
direction

\int d\eta^{+}d\eta^{-}{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}^{2}=2X\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}-2\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y~.

(35)

Note also that, since
X
and
Y
are chiral and twisted-chiral under the manifest supersymmetry (
25
), it follows that

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{D}_{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})~,

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}\Big(X\bar{D}_{+}\bar{D}_{-}\bar{X}-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y\Big)\,,

(36)

which can also be rewritten as

\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})=\int d^{2}x\,d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\Big(\bar{X}D_{+}D_{-}X-D_{+}Y\cdot D_{-}\bar{Y}\Big)~.

(37)

The sum of the two equations above yields

4\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\eta^{+}d\eta^{-}{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}^{2}+{}c.c.~.

(38)

The left-hand side has an enhanced
{\cal N}=(4,4)
supersymmetry as discussed in
[
Ivanov:2004yv
]
. This becomes manifest from our
(4,4)
superspace construction on the right-hand side.

To describe
{\cal N}=(4,4)\to{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry breaking
we can appropriately deform the
(4,4)
twisted multiplet.
Analogous to the case of a
D=4

{\cal N}=2
vector multiplet deformed by a magnetic Fayet-Iliopoulos term
[
Antoniadis:1995vb
]

(see also
[
Antoniadis:2019gbd
,
Rocek:1997hi
,
Ivanov:1998jq
,
Kuzenko:2015rfx
,
Antoniadis:2017jsk
]
),
we add a deformation parameter to the auxiliary field
F
of
{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}
,
which is deformed to

{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}_{\text{def}}=X+\eta^{+}D_{+}\bar{Y}-\eta^{-}\bar{D}_{-}Y-\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}+\kappa\Big)~.

(39)

Assuming that the auxiliary field
F
gets a VEV,
\langle F\rangle=\kappa
or equivalently
\langle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\bar{X}\rangle=0
,
then by looking at the supersymmetry transformations of
X_{\pm}
for the deformed multiplet

\displaystyle\delta X_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm\epsilon^{\mp}\Big(\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}+\kappa\Big)-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}X~,

(40)

we can see the
{\cal N}=(4,4)\to{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry breaking pattern arises; specifically, the hidden
{\cal N}=(2,2)
is spontaneously broken and non-linearly realized.
For later use, it is important to stress that, though the hidden transformations of
\delta X_{\pm}
are modified by the non-linear term
proportional to
\kappa
,
the hidden transformation of
X
remains the same as in the undeformed case given in eq. (
27a
).

In analogy to the
D=4
case of
[
Rocek:1997hi
,
Kuzenko:2015rfx
,
Antoniadis:2017jsk
]
,
to describe the Goldstone multiplet associated to partial supersymmetry breaking
we impose the following nilpotent constraint on the deformed
(4,4)
twisted superfield:

{{\bm{\mathcal{X}}}}_{\text{def}}^{2}=0=-2\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(X(\kappa+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X})-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y\Big)+\ldots~.

(41)

This implies the constraint

X\Big(\kappa+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}\Big)-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y=0~,

(42)

which requires

X=\frac{\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y}{\kappa+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}}=\frac{W^{2}}{{\kappa}+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}}~,

(43)

and its conjugate

\bar{X}=-\frac{D_{+}Y\cdot D_{-}\bar{Y}}{\kappa+D^{2}X}=-\frac{\bar{W}^{2}}{{\kappa}+D^{2}X}~.

(44)

Here
\bar{D}^{2}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{D}_{-},D^{2}=-D_{+}D_{-}
and we have introduced the superfields:

\displaystyle W^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-X_{+}X_{-}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{D}_{-}(Y\bar{Y})=\bar{D}^{2}(Y\bar{Y})~,

(45a)

\displaystyle\bar{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{X}_{+}\bar{X}_{-}=-D_{+}Y\cdot D_{-}\bar{Y}=-D_{+}D_{-}(Y\bar{Y})=D^{2}(Y\bar{Y})~.

(45b)

The constraint (
42
) is the
D=2
analogue of the Bagger-Galperin constraint for a Maxwell-Goldstone multiplet for

D=4\,

{\cal N}=2\to{\cal N}=1
supersymmetry breaking
[
Bagger:1996wp
]
.
Combining (
43
) and (
44
) gives

\kappa X=\bar{D}^{2}(Y\bar{Y}-X\bar{X})=\bar{D}^{2}\Big[Y\bar{Y}-\frac{\bar{D}_{+}\bar{Y}\cdot\bar{D}_{-}Y\cdot D_{-}\bar{Y}\cdot D_{+}Y}{({\kappa}+D^{2}X)({\kappa}+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X})}\Big]~,

(46)

which is consistent thanks to the
\kappa
terms in the denominator.
Because of the four fermion coupling in the numerator of the last term,
no fermionic terms can appear in the denominator. So effectively we have the equation

(\kappa+D^{2}X)_{\text{eff}}=\Big(\kappa+D^{2}\frac{W^{2}}{{\kappa}+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X}}\Big)_{\text{eff}}=\kappa+\frac{D^{2}W^{2}}{\kappa+(\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X})_{\text{eff}}}~,

(47)

and its conjugate

(\kappa+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X})_{\text{eff}}=\kappa+\frac{\bar{D}^{2}\bar{W}^{2}}{\kappa+(D^{2}X)_{\text{eff}}}~.

(48)

Solving them we get

\displaystyle(D^{2}X)_{\text{eff}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{B-\kappa^{2}+\sqrt{B^{2}+2\kappa^{2}A+\kappa^{4}}}{2\kappa}~,

(49a)

\displaystyle(\bar{D}^{2}\bar{X})_{\text{eff}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-B-\kappa^{2}+\sqrt{B^{2}+2\kappa^{2}A+\kappa^{4}}}{2\kappa}~.

(49b)

Substituting these expressions into (
46
) gives

X=\frac{1}{\kappa}\bar{D}^{2}\Upsilon~,\qquad\bar{X}=\frac{1}{\kappa}D^{2}\Upsilon~,\qquad\Upsilon=\bar{\Upsilon}=Y\bar{Y}-\frac{2W^{2}\bar{W}^{2}}{A+\kappa^{2}+\sqrt{B^{2}+2\kappa^{2}A+\kappa^{4}}}~,

(50)

where

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=D^{2}W^{2}+\bar{D}^{2}\bar{W}^{2}=\{D^{2},\bar{D}^{2}\}(Y\bar{Y})=\partial_{++}Y\partial_{--}\bar{Y}+\partial_{++}\bar{Y}\partial_{--}Y~,

(51)

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=D^{2}W^{2}-\bar{D}^{2}\bar{W}^{2}=[D^{2},\bar{D}^{2}](Y\bar{Y})=\partial_{++}Y\partial_{--}\bar{Y}-\partial_{++}\bar{Y}\partial_{--}Y~.

(52)

The result is that the
{\cal N}=(2,2)
chiral part
X
of the
{\cal N}=(4,4)
twisted multiplet is expressed
in terms of the
(2,2)
twisted-chiral superfield
Y
.
Thanks to the linearly realized construction in terms of
(4,4)
superfields, it is straightforward
to obtain the non-linearly realized
{\cal N}=(2,2)
supersymmetry transformations for
Y
. In particular,
it suffices to look at the transformations of

D_{+}Y
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}Y
that
can be obtained by substituting back the composite expression for
X=X[Y]
into the
transformations (
40
). By construction, these expressions ensure that
\delta X
transforms according to (
27a
).

Since
X
is chiral under the manifest
(2,2)
supersymmetry (
25
), we can consider the chiral integral

\displaystyle S_{\kappa^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\kappa\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}X+c.c=-\frac{1}{2}\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}\bar{D}^{2}\Upsilon+c.c.

(53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\Upsilon~.

A remarkable property of this action is that
it is invariant under the hidden non-linearly realized supersymmetry.
Using (
27a
), we see that

\displaystyle\delta S_{\kappa^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\kappa\int d^{2}xD_{+}D_{-}\delta X\Big|_{\theta=\bar{\theta}=0}+c.c.~,

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\kappa\int d^{2}x\Big(-2i\epsilon^{-}\partial_{--}D_{+}Y-2i\epsilon^{+}\partial_{++}D_{-}\bar{Y}\Big)\Big|_{\theta=\bar{\theta}=0}+c.c.=0~,

(54)

where we used the fact that
Y
is a twisted-chiral superfield (
25
).

Explicitly, the action reads

S_{\kappa^{2}}=-\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\Bigg(Y\bar{Y}-\frac{2W^{2}\bar{W}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}+A+\sqrt{\kappa^{4}+2\kappa^{2}A+B^{2}}}\Bigg)~,

(55)

which precisely matches the model of eq. (
10a
) if we
identify the coupling constants:

\lambda=-\frac{2}{\kappa^{2}}~.

(56)

This shows explicitly that the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformation of the free twisted-chiral action possesses a non-linearly realized

{\cal N}=(2,2)
hidden
supersymmetry.

3
The
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
-deformed chiral model and partial-breaking

Let us now turn to the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of the free chiral model of eq. (
8a
). The construction follows the previous
subsection with the difference that we will start with a different formulation of the
(4,4)
twisted multiplet
described in terms of
(2,2)
superfields.
Consider again an
{\cal N}=(4,4)
superfield which is chiral under the hidden (2,2) supersymmetry:

\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{+}{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{-}{{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}}=0~.

(57)

Its expansion in hidden superspace variables is

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=Y+\eta^{+}Y_{+}+\eta^{-}Y_{-}+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}G~,

(58)

where
Y=Y(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})
,
Y_{\pm}=Y_{\pm}(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})

and
G=G(y^{\pm\pm},\theta^{\pm},\bar{\theta}^{\pm})

are themselves superfields with manifest
(2,2)
supersymmetry.
The hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetry transformation rules of the components are

\displaystyle\delta Y

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{+}Y_{+}-\epsilon^{-}Y_{-}~,

(59a)

\displaystyle\delta Y_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp\epsilon^{\mp}G-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}Y~,

(59b)

\displaystyle\delta G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}Y_{+}+2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}Y_{-}~.

(59c)

This representation of
(4,4)
off-shell supersymmetry is again reducible so we can impose constraints. As in the construction of the previous section, we impose

Y_{+}=\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}~,\qquad Y_{-}=D_{-}X~,

(60)

then

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=Y+\eta^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}+\eta^{-}D_{-}X+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}G~.

(61)

Here
X
and
Y
are consistently chosen to be chiral and twisted-chiral under the manifest
(2,2)
supersymmetry:

\bar{D}_{+}X=\bar{D}_{-}X=\bar{D}_{+}Y=D_{-}Y=0~,\qquad D_{+}\bar{X}=D_{-}\bar{X}=D_{+}\bar{Y}=\bar{D}_{-}\bar{Y}=0~.

(62)

Then we have

\delta Y=-\epsilon^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}-\epsilon^{-}D_{-}X~,

(63)

as well as its conjugate

\delta\bar{Y}=\bar{\epsilon}^{+}D_{+}X+\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\bar{D}_{-}\bar{X}~.

(64)

Hence it follows that

\delta(\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y})=\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\delta\bar{Y}=2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}D_{-}X-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}~.

(65)

This should be compared with

\delta G=2i\bar{\epsilon}^{+}\partial_{++}D_{-}X-2i\bar{\epsilon}^{-}\partial_{--}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}~,

(66)

showing that
\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}
transforms exactly like the auxiliary field
G
.
This enables us to further constrain the
(4,4)
multiplet by setting

G=\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}~.

(67)

Imposing these conditions gives a
(4,4)
twisted superfield

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=Y+\eta^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}+\eta^{-}D_{-}X+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}~,

(68)

which by construction is twisted-chiral and chiral with respect to the manifest and hidden
(2,2)
supersymmetries, respectively:

\bar{D}_{+}{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=D_{-}{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=0~,\quad\bar{\mathcal{D}}_{\pm}{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}=0~.

(69)

Its free dynamical action can be easily constructed by considering its square

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}^{2}=2\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(Y\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X\Big)+\ldots~.

(70)

In fact, the following relations hold:

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})~,

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\Big(D_{+}X\cdot\bar{D}_{-}\bar{X}-\bar{Y}D_{+}\bar{D}_{-}Y\Big)\,.

(71)

Alternatively,

\displaystyle\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\theta^{-}D_{+}\bar{D}_{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})\,,

\displaystyle=\int d^{2}x\,d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\theta^{-}\Big(\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X-Y\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}\Big)\,.

(72)

These relations imply

4\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}(X\bar{X}-Y\bar{Y})=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}d\eta^{+}d\eta^{-}\ {{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}}^{2}+c.c.~.

(73)

Once again the
(4,4)
supersymmetry of the left hand side becomes manifest on the right hand side.

As in the
(4,4)
twisted multiplet considered in the previous subsection,
we can deform this representation
to induce the partial breaking.
The deformed multiplet is described by the following
(4,4)
superfield:

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\text{def}}=Y+\eta^{+}\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}+\eta^{-}D_{-}X+\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}+\kappa\Big)~.

(74)

The hidden supersymmetry transformations of the component
(2,2)
superfields can be straightforwardly computed using the arguments of the previous subsection. For the goal of this section, it is enough to mention that

\delta Y
is the same as the undeformed case of eq. (
63
).

To eliminate half of the degrees of freedom of
{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}_{\text{def}}

and describe a Goldstone multiplet for
{\cal N}=(4,4)\to{\cal N}=(2,2)
partial supersymmetry breaking,
we again impose the nilpotent constraint

{\bm{\mathcal{Y}}}^{2}_{\text{def}}=0=2\eta^{+}\eta^{-}\Big(Y(\kappa+\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y})-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X\Big)+\ldots~.

(75)

This yields the following constraint for the
(2,2)
superfields

Y(\kappa+\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y})-\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X=0~,

(76)

which is equivalent to

Y=\frac{\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X}{\kappa+\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}}=\frac{\widetilde{W}^{2}}{\kappa+\overline{\widetilde{D}}^{2}\bar{Y}}~,\qquad\bar{Y}=\frac{\bar{D}_{-}\bar{X}\cdot D_{+}X}{\kappa+\bar{D}_{+}D_{-}\bar{Y}}=\frac{\bar{\widetilde{W}}^{2}}{\kappa+\widetilde{D}^{2}\bar{Y}}~.

(77)

Here
\bar{\widetilde{D}}^{2}=\bar{D}_{+}D_{-},\widetilde{D}^{2}=-D_{+}\bar{D}_{-}
and we have introduced
the following bilinears:

\widetilde{W}^{2}\equiv\bar{D}_{+}\bar{X}\cdot D_{-}X=\bar{\widetilde{D}}^{2}(X\bar{X}),\qquad\bar{\widetilde{W}}^{2}\equiv\bar{D}_{-}\bar{X}\cdot D_{+}X=\widetilde{D}^{2}(X\bar{X})~.

(78)

Using exactly the same tricks as before
and inspired by the
D=4
Bagger-Galperin model,
we can solve the constraints (
76
) to find

Y=\frac{1}{\kappa}\bar{\widetilde{D}}^{2}\widetilde{\Upsilon}~,\quad\bar{Y}=\frac{1}{\kappa}\widetilde{D}^{2}\widetilde{\Upsilon}~,\qquad\widetilde{\Upsilon}=\bar{\widetilde{\Upsilon}}=X\bar{X}-\frac{2\widetilde{W}^{2}\bar{\widetilde{W}}^{2}}{\widetilde{A}+\kappa^{2}+\sqrt{\widetilde{B}^{2}+2\kappa^{2}\widetilde{A}+\kappa^{4}}}~,

(79)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}=\widetilde{D}^{2}\widetilde{W}^{2}+\bar{\widetilde{D}^{2}}\bar{\widetilde{W}^{2}}=\{\widetilde{D}^{2},\bar{\widetilde{D}^{2}}\}(X\bar{X})=\partial_{++}X\partial_{--}\bar{X}+\partial_{++}\bar{X}\partial_{--}X~,

(80)

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}=\widetilde{D}^{2}\widetilde{W}^{2}-\bar{\widetilde{D}}^{2}\bar{\widetilde{W}}^{2}=[\widetilde{D}^{2},\bar{\widetilde{D}^{2}}](X\bar{X})=\partial_{++}X\partial_{--}\bar{X}-\partial_{++}\bar{X}\partial_{--}X~.

(81)

Since
Y
is twisted-chiral under the manifest
(2,2)
supersymmetry (
62
), we can consider the
twisted-chiral integral

S_{\kappa^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}Y+c.c=\frac{1}{2}\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\bar{\widetilde{D}}^{2}\widetilde{\Upsilon}+c.c.=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\widetilde{\Upsilon}~.~~~~~~~~~

(82)

By using arguments analogous to those around eqs. (
54
) of the previous subsection,
the action (
82
) proves to be
{\cal N}=(4,4)
supersymmetric.

Explicitly, the action reads

\displaystyle S_{\kappa^{2}}=\int d^{2}x\,d\theta^{+}d\theta^{-}d\bar{\theta}^{+}d\bar{\theta}^{-}\Bigg(X\bar{X}-\frac{2\widetilde{W}^{2}\bar{\widetilde{W}}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}+\widetilde{A}+\sqrt{\kappa^{4}+2\kappa^{2}\widetilde{A}+\widetilde{B}^{2}}}\Bigg)~,

(83)

which precisely matches the model of eq. (
8a
) if we
identify the coupling constants:

\lambda=-\frac{2}{\kappa^{2}}~.

(84)

This shows explicitly that the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of the free chiral action possesses a non-linearly realized

{\cal N}=(2,2)

supersymmetry.

2
T^{2}
Deformations in
D=4
and Supersymmetric Extensions

In Section
1
we exhibited the non-linear supersymmetry possessed by two
D=2
,
{\cal N}=(2,2)

models constructed in
[
Chang:2019kiu
]
from the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation of free actions. The striking relationship with the
D=4
supersymmetric
Born-Infeld (BI) theory naturally makes one wonder whether some kind of
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow equation is satisfied by
supersymmetric
D=4
BI,
and related actions. We will spend the rest of the chapter exploring this possibility. In this section, we start with a few general
observations on
T^{2}
or supercurrent-squared operators in
D>2
.

1
Comments on the
T^{2}
operator in
D=4

In two dimensions, by
T\bar{T}
we mean the operator

T_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}-(T_{\mu}^{\mu})^{2}
, which is proportional to
\det[T_{\mu\nu}]

[
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
,
Smirnov:2016lqw
,
Cavaglia:2016oda
]
.
One can attempt to generalize this structure to
D>2
.
In general, one could consider the following stress-tensor squared operator

O_{T^{2}}^{[r]}=T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-r\,\Theta^{2}~,\qquad\Theta\equiv\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}~,

(1)

with
r
a real constant parameter.
In two dimensions, the unique choice
r=1
yields a well defined operator which is free of short distance singularities
[
Smirnov:2016lqw
,
Zamolodchikov:2004ce
]
.
However, to the best of our knowledge, there is no analogous argument in higher dimensions that guarantees a well-defined irrelevant operator
O_{T^{2}}^{[r]}
