), are worked out in Appendix
7.G
. The resulting partial differential equation, equation (
7
), is copied here for convenience:

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=f^{2}-4fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2fy\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+4xy\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}.

(4)

Our goal in the following subsections will be to solve (
7
) by several methods, just as we did in the case of pure gauge theory.

1
Series solution of flow equation

We know that (
7
) reduces to the Dirac action, (
9
), when the gauge field is set to zero, and that it reduces to the hypergeometric action of Section
2
, (
11
), when the scalar is set to zero. Therefore, in the coupled case it is natural to make an ansatz of the form

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}-1\right)+{}_{3}F_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4},1,\frac{5}{4};\frac{4}{3},\frac{5}{3},2;\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda x\right)\cdot x

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n=3}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}c_{n,k}\lambda^{n-1}x^{k}y^{n-k}.

(5)

The sum on the final line of (
5
) allows for all possible couplings between
F^{2}
and
|D\phi|^{2}
, with the appropriate power of
\lambda
required by dimensional analysis. One can then determine the coefficients
c_{n,k}
by plugging the ansatz (
5
) into (
7
) and solving order-by-order in
\lambda
. The result, up to coupled terms of order
\lambda^{8}
, is

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+16\lambda y}-1\right)+{}_{3}F_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4},1,\frac{5}{4};\frac{4}{3},\frac{5}{3},2;\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda x\right)\cdot x

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda^{2}xy^{2}+\lambda^{3}\left(4\ xy^{3}-4\,x^{2}y^{2}\right)+\lambda^{4}\left(18\,x^{2}y^{3}-22\,x^{3}y^{2}-14\,xy^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{5}\left(-140\,x^{4}y^{2}+104\,x^{3}y^{3}-65\,x^{2}y^{4}+48\,xy^{5}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{6}\left(-165\,xy^{6}+220\,x^{2}y^{5}-364\,x^{3}y^{4}+680\,x^{4}y^{3}-969\,x^{5}y^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{7}\left(572\,xy^{7}-726\,x^{2}y^{6}+1120\,x^{3}y^{5}-2244\,x^{4}y^{4}+4788\,x^{5}y^{3}-7084\,x^{6}y^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\lambda^{8}\Big(-2002\,xy^{8}+2392\,x^{2}y^{7}-3160\,x^{3}y^{6}+5814\,x^{4}y^{5}-14630\,x^{5}y^{4}+35420\,x^{6}y^{3}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-53820\,x^{7}y^{2}\Big).

(6)

We were unable to find an closed-form expression for the function which generates the couplings (
6
). However, it is interesting to study the corrections in various approximations.

For instance, consider the coupled terms between
F^{2}
and
|D\phi|^{2}
to leading order in the variable
y=|D\phi|^{2}
. Retaining only the couplings in (
6
) proportional to
y^{2}
, one finds

\displaystyle\hskip-10.0ptf(\lambda,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+16\lambda y}-1\right)+{}_{3}F_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4},1,\frac{5}{4};\frac{4}{3},\frac{5}{3},2;\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda x\right)\cdot x-\lambda^{2}xy^{2}-4\lambda^{3}\,x^{2}y^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2\lambda^{4}\,x^{3}y^{2}-140\lambda^{5}\,x^{4}y^{2}-969\lambda^{6}\,x^{5}y^{2}-7084\lambda^{7}\,x^{6}y^{2}-53820\lambda^{8}\,x^{7}y^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{9},\lambda^{3}xy^{3}\right).

(7)

These series coefficients resum into another hypergeometric function
[
hypergeometricCoupledSequence
]
,

\displaystyle\hskip-30.0ptf(\lambda,x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+16\lambda y}-1\right)+{}_{3}F_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4},1,\frac{5}{4};\frac{4}{3},\frac{5}{3},2;\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda x\right)\cdot x

\displaystyle\quad-\lambda^{2}xy^{2}\,{}_{3}F_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4};\frac{2}{3},\frac{4}{3};\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda x\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\lambda^{3}xy^{3}\right).

(8)

Defining the hypergeometric appearing in the correction term as

\displaystyle g(\chi)={}_{3}F_{2}\left(\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4};\frac{2}{3},\frac{4}{3};\frac{256}{27}\cdot\chi\right),

(9)

one can show that
g
satisfies the functional relation

\displaystyle\chi=\frac{g(\chi)-1}{g(\chi)^{4}}.

(10)

The maximum of the function
\frac{g-1}{g^{4}}
occurs when
g=\frac{4}{3}
, at which this function takes the maximal value of
\frac{27}{256}
. Therefore, the maximum value of
\chi
for which the function (
10
) is defined is
\chi=\frac{27}{256}
, giving a critical field strength

\displaystyle F^{2}<\frac{27}{256\lambda}.

(11)

We note that this is the same value of the critical electric field as that in the uncoupled term involving
{}_{3}F_{4}
in (
8
), which we saw in (
40
) for the case of pure gauge theory. It is reasonable to expect that the value of the critical electric field is modified if one includes corrections to all orders in
|D\phi|^{2}
, as is the case for the Dirac-Born-Infeld action.

2
Implicit solution

We can instead solve (
7
) in terms of a complete integral. First, we refine our ansatz to

\displaystyle f(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\lambda}g(\chi,\eta)\quad,\quad\chi=\lambda x~,~\eta=\lambda y~.

(12)

After doing this, the differential equation becomes

\displaystyle 0=4\chi^{2}\left(\frac{\partial g}{\partial\chi}\right)^{2}+4\eta\chi\frac{\partial g}{\partial\chi}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\eta}-4\chi g\frac{\partial g}{\partial\chi}-\chi\frac{\partial g}{\partial\chi}-2\eta g\frac{\partial g}{\partial\eta}-\eta\frac{\partial g}{\partial\eta}+g^{2}+g.

(13)

Making a change of variables to

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle=\log( \chi)\quad,\quad q=\log( \eta)~,

(14)

and writing
g(\chi,\eta)=w(p,q)
, the differential equation for
h
becomes

\displaystyle 0=4\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial p}\right)^{2}+4\frac{\partial w}{\partial p}\frac{\partial w}{\partial q}-\left(4w+1\right)\frac{\partial w}{\partial p}-\left(2w+1\right)\frac{\partial w}{\partial q}+w^{2}+w~.

(15)

This can be solved by consulting a handbook of partial differential equations (see, for instance, equation 15 in section 2.2.6 of
[
polyanin
]
). For any partial differential equation of the form

\displaystyle 0=f_{1}(w)\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+f_{2}(w)\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}\frac{\partial w}{\partial y}+f_{3}(w)\left(\frac{\partial w}{\partial y}\right)^{2}+g_{1}(w)\frac{\partial w}{\partial x}+g_{2}(w)\frac{\partial w}{\partial y}+h(w),

(16)

the solution
w(x,y)
is given implicitly by the following complete integral:

\displaystyle C_{3}

\displaystyle=C_{1}x+C_{2}y+\int\frac{2F(w)\,dw}{G(w)\pm\sqrt{G(w)^{2}-4F(w)h(w)}},

\displaystyle F(w)

\displaystyle=C_{1}^{2}f_{1}(w)+C_{1}C_{2}f_{2}(w)+C_{2}^{2}f_{3}(w),

\displaystyle G(w)

\displaystyle=C_{1}g_{1}(w)+C_{2}g_{2}(w).

(17)

Our equation (
15
) is precisely of the form (
16
), after identifying the independent variables
p\sim x
,
q\sim y
, and with the following functions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f_{1}=f_{2}=4\quad,\quad f_{3}=0\quad,\quad g_{1}=-4w-1~,\\
&g_{2}=-2w-1\quad,\quad h(w)=w^{2}+w.\end{split}

(18)

Therefore, the functions
F
and
G
are

\displaystyle F(w)

\displaystyle=4C_{1}^{2}+4C_{1}C_{2},

\displaystyle G(w)

\displaystyle=C_{1}\left(-4w-1\right)+C_{2}\left(-2w-1\right)

\displaystyle=\left(-4C_{1}-2C_{2}\right)w-\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right).

(19)

Our solution, then, is

\displaystyle C_{3}=C_{1}p+C_{2}q

(20)

\displaystyle\hskip-15.0pt+\int\frac{8\left(C_{1}^{2}+C_{1}C_{2}\right)\,dw}{-\left(4C_{1}+2C_{2}\right)w-\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)-\sqrt{\left(\left(4C_{1}+2C_{2}\right)w+\left(C_{1}+C_{2}\right)\right)^{2}-16\left(C_{1}^{2}+C_{1}C_{2}\right)\left(w^{2}+w\right)}}~.

where we have taken the negative root in the denominator, appropriate if
C_{1}+C_{2}<0
.

Choosing values of the constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
in (
2
) gives an implicit relation for the function
w(p,q)
which solves the
{T\overline{T}}
flow equation. For instance, if we set
C_{2}=0
and
C_{1}=-1
, equation (
2
) becomes

\displaystyle C_{3}+\log( \chi)=\int\frac{8\,dw}{4w+1-\sqrt{1-8w}},

(21)

which reproduces the result (
15
) which we found in the case of pure gauge theory. In this sense, our implicit solution is a generalization of the technique of section
2
to the case where
|D\phi|^{2}\neq 0
.

The integral appearing in (
2
) can be computed explicitly in terms of logarithms (or, equivalently, inverse hyperbolic tangents). The integral is of the form

\displaystyle I(w)=\int\frac{\alpha\,dw}{-\beta w-\gamma-\sqrt{\left(\beta w+\gamma\right)^{2}-2\alpha\left(w^{2}+w\right)}},

(22)

where

\displaystyle\alpha

\displaystyle=8(C_{1}^{2}+C_{1}C_{2}),

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=4C_{1}+2C_{2},

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=C_{1}+C_{2}.

(23)

The result can be written as

\displaystyle I(w)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Bigg((\gamma-\beta)\tanh^{-1}\left(\frac{\alpha(w+1)+(\beta-\gamma)(\gamma+\beta w)}{(\beta-\gamma)\sqrt{\gamma^{2}+w^{2}\left(2\alpha+\beta^{2}\right)+2w(\alpha+\beta\gamma)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\gamma\tanh^{-1}\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}+w(\alpha+\beta\gamma)}{\gamma\sqrt{\gamma^{2}+w^{2}\left(2\alpha+\beta^{2}\right)+2w(\alpha+\beta\gamma)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sqrt{2\alpha+\beta^{2}}\tanh^{-1}\left(\frac{\alpha+2\alpha w+\beta(\gamma+\beta w)}{\sqrt{2\alpha+\beta^{2}}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}+w^{2}\left(2\alpha+\beta^{2}\right)+2w(\alpha+\beta\gamma)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\beta-\gamma\right)\log(w+1)+\gamma\log(w)\Bigg).

(24)

Exponentiating both sides then gives

\displaystyle\exp\left(C_{3}-C_{1}p-C_{2}q\right)=\exp\left(I(w)\right).

(25)

After simplifying the exponentials of the inverse hyperbolic tangents in (
25
), the right side only involves rational functions and radicals. This relation, therefore, gives an algebraic equation in
w
whose roots are the solution to the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
flow.

By construction, a function
w(p,q)
which satisfies (
25
) solves the differential equation (
15
). However, this technique is more unwieldy than the direct series solution for generating Taylor coefficients. The main utility of this strategy is the conceptual result that the solution
w(p,q)
is, in principle, defined by the root of an equation which involves only radicals and quotients, as we saw for the pure gauge theory case in (
19
).

3
Solution via dualization

One can also apply the dualization technique of section (
2
) to the coupled action. Begin with the undeformed action

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}=|D\phi|^{2}+F^{2}~,

(26)

where we put
k=1
since the sign will not affect this calculation. Exactly as before, this action is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}=|D\phi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}+\chi\epsilon^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}~,

(27)

after integrating out the field
\chi
, although this form of the Lagrangian hides some complexity because the covariant derivative
D
is now non-local in
\chi
. Ignoring this for the moment, we again note that the action coupled to a background metric is of the form

\displaystyle S_{0}=\left(\int\sqrt{-g}\,d^{2}x\,\left(|D\phi|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}\right)\right)+\left(\int d^{2}x\,\chi\epsilon^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}\right)~.

(28)

As far as the
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformation is concerned, (
28
) is simply the action of a complex scalar
\phi
with a constant potential
V=\frac{1}{2}\chi^{2}
. The solution to the flow equation at finite
\lambda
is
[
Cavaglia:2016oda
,
Bonelli:2018kik
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda)=\frac{1}{2\lambda}\sqrt{\frac{\left(1-\lambda\chi^{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda\chi^{2}\right)^{2}}+2\lambda\,\frac{2|D\phi|^{2}+\chi^{2}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda\chi^{2}}}-\frac{1}{2\lambda}\,\frac{1-\lambda\chi^{2}}{1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda\chi^{2}}+\chi\epsilon^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}~.

(29)

As in section (
2
), one might hope to iteratively integrate out the auxiliary field
\chi
in (
29
) in order to express the result in terms of
F^{2}
. The equation of motion for
\chi
resulting from (
29
), after solving for
F_{01}
(and assuming that
\lambda\chi^{2}<2
), is

\displaystyle F_{01}=\frac{\chi\left(-|D\phi|^{2}\lambda\left(2-\lambda\chi^{2}\right)-\sqrt{2|D\phi|^{2}\lambda\left(2-\lambda\chi^{2}\right)+1}-1\right)}{\left(2-\lambda\chi^{2}\right)^{2}\sqrt{2|D\phi|^{2}\lambda\left(2-\lambda\chi^{2}\right)+1}}~.

(30)

Solving (
30
) by series inversion to give
\chi
as a function of
F_{01}
, then substituting back into (
29
), then determines the full
T\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu
deformed action. The result, up to order
F^{8}
and using the shorthand
x=F^{2},y=|D\phi|^{2}
, is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda)=\frac{\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}-1}{2\lambda}+\frac{2x\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+2\lambda y\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+2\right)+1\right)}{\left(2\lambda y+\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{16\lambda x^{2}}{\left(2\lambda y+\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)^{5}}\cdot\Bigg[2\lambda^{3}y^{3}\left(3\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+14\right)+\lambda^{2}y^{2}\left(17\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+31\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+2\lambda y\left(4\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+5\right)+\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{128\lambda^{2}x^{3}}{\left(2\lambda y+\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)^{8}}\cdot\Bigg[4\lambda^{5}y^{5}\left(13\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+96\right)+2\lambda^{4}y^{4}\left(183\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+496\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+8\lambda^{3}y^{3}\left(57\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+101\right)+2\lambda^{2}y^{2}\left(106\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+145\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+6\lambda y\left(7\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+8\right)+3\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1024\lambda^{3}x^{4}}{\left(2\lambda y+\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)^{11}}\cdot\Bigg[2\lambda^{7}y^{7}\left(323\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+3266\right)+\lambda^{6}y^{6}\left(8471\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+30585\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+2\lambda^{5}y^{5}\left(9879\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+22795\right)+4\lambda^{4}y^{4}\left(4589\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+8019\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+2\lambda^{3}y^{3}\left(4244\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+6093\right)+\lambda^{2}y^{2}\left(2083\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+2577\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt+26\lambda y\left(10\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+11\right)+13\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda y}+1\right)\Bigg]~.

(31)

We have checked by explicit computation that the series expansion (
31
) solves the flow equation (
7
) to order
x^{4}
.

Chapter 6
{T\overline{T}}
Deformations of Gauge Theories in Arbitrary Dimension

This chapter aims to extend the study of
{T\overline{T}}
deformations of pure gauge theories – which was discussed, in part, in Chapters
4
and
5
– to general spacetime dimension. Because an unambiguous definition of the
{T\overline{T}}
operator at the quantum level is available only in
d=2
, the treatment of this chapter will be totally classical. We will view stress tensor deformations as merely a mechanism for generating interesting one-parameter families of classical Lagrangians, rather than an irrelevant deformation by an operator which admits a definition by point-splitting.

Unlike the majority of this thesis, the current chapter contains new results which do not appear in any previous publications. I thank Savdeep Sethi and Gabriele Tartaglino-Mazzucchelli for useful discussions which prompted this work.
1
1

1

I am also grateful to Mukund Rangamani, Julio Virrueta, Shruti Paranjape, Brendan Halstead, and Luna Dole for fruitful conversations about extensions of these results to the
p
-form and non-abelian settings.

1
Motivation and
3d
Example

In the preceding chapters, we have seen that interesting gauge theories can be built from
{T\overline{T}}
flows in
d=2
dimensions and in
d=4
dimensions. In
d=2
, deforming the theory of a gauge field coupled to charged matter gives a quantum-mechanically well-defined theory which is compatible with maximal supersymmetry. This theory does not reduce to the Dirac-Born-Infeld (DBI) action, even in the case of an abelian gauge group, and the pure gauge part of the action is written in terms of a hypergeometric function. In
d=4
, deforming the theory of an abelian gauge field using a
{T\overline{T}}
-like operator gives the Born-Infeld action, although in this case we do not expect that the operator is well-defined by point-splitting without imposing extended supersymmetry.

It is natural to ask whether interesting actions arise from deformations by stress tensor bilinears in other numbers of spacetime dimensions besides
d=2
and
d=4
. Although the analysis for
d=3
is a special case of the one that we will develop later in this chapter, we will include it explicitly here by way of motivating some of the ideas which appear in the general case. In particular, we will ask whether a Born-Infeld-type Lagrangian involving a square root can be obtained from deforming the
d=3
Maxwell Lagrangian by any combination of stress tensor bilinears.

First consider a general Lagrangian depending on a field strength
F_{\mu\nu}
and parameter
\lambda
. As we will prove later, in
d=3
the only scalar which can be constructed from
F_{\mu\nu}
is
F^{2}=F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}
. Thus an arbitrary Lagrangian can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=f(\lambda,F^{2}).

(1)

For convenience, let
x=F^{2}
. We can now compute the stress-energy tensor by coupling to a background metric and varying with respect to the metric, where our conventions will be given in (
8
). One finds

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}\,f-4\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\nu\sigma}.

(2)

We then compute the two combinations
T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}
and
\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}
. The first is

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}\,f-4\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\mathchoice{F^{{{\mu}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}}{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{ f}{\partial x}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\nu\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=3f^{2}-8f\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}F^{2}+16\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}F^{\mu\sigma}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makeb (3)

The last term,
F^{\mu\sigma}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\rho\nu}
, naively appears to be a new scalar quantity constructed from
F_{\mu\nu}
which defines an independent quantity in the action. However, in three spacetime dimensions, one has the relation

\displaystyle F^{\mu\sigma}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\rho\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\left(F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right)^{2}.

(4)

This is analogous to the fact that, in
d=2
spacetime dimensions, one has
\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\sigma\nu}=-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}\left(F_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha\beta}\right)
. This was used in Appendix
7.G
to obtain the general flow equation for two-dimensional theories which was analyzed in Chapter
5
; in both cases, we are exploiting the fact that in low spacetime dimension (
d=2,3
), the number of independent scalars that can be constructed from
F_{\mu\nu}
is small.

Replacing
F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}=x
, we then find

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}=3f^{2}-8fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+8\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}x^{2}.

(5)

The second combination is

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(3f-4F^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}=9f^{2}-24fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+16\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}x^{2}\,.

(6)

We would like to consider an arbitrary flow equation for the Lagrangian which is built out of these stress tensor bilinears,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\lambda}=aT^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-b\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}\,.

(7)

Of course, we can absorb an overall factor of
a
into the normalization of
\lambda
and then define the relative coefficient
-\frac{b}{a}
as a new parameter
r
. So it suffices to consider flow equations of the form

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\lambda}=T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-r\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}\,.

(8)

Using our expressions for the two stress tensor products derived above, the general flow equation is

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}

\displaystyle=T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-r\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=3\left(1-3r\right)f^{2}-8\left(1-3r\right)fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+8\left(1-2r\right)\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}x^{2},

(9)

This flow equation may have interesting solutions for various values of the relative coefficient
r
. For the moment, we will content ourselves by asking whether it has any solutions of Born-Infeld type. In three spacetime dimensions, one has

\displaystyle\sqrt{-\det( \eta_{\mu\nu} + \alpha F_{\mu\nu} )}=\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{2}\alpha^{2}F^{2}}\,.

(10)

Viewing
\lambda
as playing the role of
\alpha^{2}
, we ask whether we can ever get solutions of the form

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x)=\frac{a}{\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+b\lambda x}-1\right)\,,

(11)

which by construction is proportional to the Maxwell action as
\lambda\to 0
.
2
2

2

We briefly comment on dimensional analysis. Ordinarily, the free action in
d
spacetime dimensions is
S=-\frac{1}{4g^{2}}\int d^{d}x\,F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}
and
F_{\mu\nu}
has mass dimension
2
. However, it is convenient to re-scale
F\to\frac{1}{g}F
and eliminate the prefactor of
\frac{1}{g^{2}}
in the Lagrangian. In these conventions, the combination
x=F^{2}
has mass dimension
d
so the field strength
F_{\mu\nu}
has mass dimension
\frac{d}{2}
. Similarly, the stress tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
has mass dimension
d
in any number of dimensions, so stress tensor bilinears like
{T\overline{T}}
have mass dimension
2d
. Therefore, by dimensional analysis of the flow equation
\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\lambda}={T\overline{T}}
, we see that
\lambda
must have length dimension
d
. Thus in the case
d=3
of interest here,
\lambda
has length dimension
3
. This means that the combination
\lambda x
under the square root in (
11
) is dimensionless, as required, and the overall factor of
\frac{1}{\lambda}
has the same dimension as
F^{2}
, which is the appropriate dimension for a Lagrangian density.

By using the ansatz (
11
) in the general flow equation

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=c_{1}f^{2}+c_{2}fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+c_{3}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}x^{2}\,,

(12)

one can see that (
12
) will have solutions of this square-root-type only if
c_{3}=0
and
c_{2}=-2c_{1}
, in which case the solution is

\displaystyle f(\lambda,x)=\frac{1}{2\lambda c_{1}}\left(\sqrt{1+4c_{2}\lambda x}-1\right)\,,

(13)

where
c_{1},c_{2}
are fixed by the initial condition, which in this case is
f(0,x)=-\frac{x}{4}
. However, even if we choose
r=\frac{1}{2}
so that the unwanted final term in (
1
) drops out, the differential equation for our three-dimensional flow is

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=-\frac{3}{2}f^{2}+4fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}.

(14)

This does not have a square-root-type solution, since it is not of the form (
12
) with
c_{2}=-2c_{1}
; instead we have
c_{2}=4
and
-2c_{1}=3
.
Thus the Born-Infeld action cannot be obtained via any
{T\overline{T}}
-like deformation of the Maxwell action in three spacetime dimensions.

The above analysis raises several questions. Can the Born-Infeld action be realized as a
{T\overline{T}}
flow in any other dimension
d>4
? Can we obtain other interesting actions by applying
{T\overline{T}}
-like deformations to seed theories other than the free Maxwell action? For instance, perhaps we would like to begin with the Born-Infeld action as the seed theory, rather than as a desired output of deformation, and see what action is produced by the flow. What additional complications arise when deforming non-abelian gauge theories? For instance, can one define non-abelian analogues of BI in higher dimensions using
{T\overline{T}}
-like flows as well?

The aim of this chapter is to present a framework for answering questions like this by considering a
{T\overline{T}}
-like deformation of a quite general gauge theory in arbitrary spacetime dimension. Throughout this chapter, we will restrict to pure gauge theory without any charged matter. In the abelian case, we will be able to explicitly derive a flow equation for a family of
{T\overline{T}}
-type deformations of such general theories. Interestingly, the only case for which deformation of a free Maxwell theory yields a Lagrangian of Born-Infeld type occurs for
d=4
. We will also discuss the more subtle case of deformations for non-abelian gauge theories, where it is immediate that one can never obtain a deformed Lagrangian of Born-Infeld type because
{T\overline{T}}
flows cannot generate terms with the appropriate trace structure.

2
Deformations of General Abelian Gauge Theories

In this section, we will restrict to the simpler case of a
U(1)
gauge field
A_{\mu}
, which avoids complications from traces over group indices.

1
Signature and Sign Conventions

Unlike in the
3d
example of the introductory section
1
, for the remainder of this chapter we will work in Euclidean signature in
d
spacetime dimensions. In this signature the field strength
F
can be thought of as a
d\times d
matrix regardless of the positioning of the indices, which are raised or lowered with
\delta_{\mu\nu}
.

Our conclusions will be valid for either Euclidean or Minkowski signature, so the choice of restricting to Euclidean signature is merely for simplicity; we did not feel that the additional generality of treating both cases merited the introduction of a constant
k=\pm 1
and writing
\mathcal{L}=\frac{k}{4}F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}
as in Chapter
5
. We can afford to be somewhat cavalier about the overall sign of the Lagrangian because our goal is to understand deformations by stress tensor bilinears, and since reversing the sign of the Lagrangian also flips the sign of
T_{\mu\nu}
, all bilinears in
T_{\mu\nu}
are insensitive to the sign of
\mathcal{L}
. However, to avoid sign confusion and reiterate our conventions, we will state a few formulas here.

We will take the Euclidean action for free Maxwell theory to be

\displaystyle S_{\text{Euc}}=\frac{1}{4}\int d^{d}x\,F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{1}{4}\int d^{d}x\,F^{2}\,.

(1)

The factors of the coupling
g
have been absorbed into the field strength, following the choice in footnote
2
, so that
F_{\mu\nu}
now has mass dimension
\frac{d}{2}
. We emphasize that this is in contrast to the Lorentz signature action,

\displaystyle S_{\text{Lor}}=-\frac{1}{4}\int d^{d}x\,F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\,,

(2)

which was only used in the introductory discussion of Section
1
but will be mostly discarded in favor of (
1
) from here on.

In this chapter, expressions like
F^{2n}_{\mu\nu}
(which we will often write with indices suppressed as
F^{2n}
) will always refer to the
d\times d
matrix obtained by successively performing
2n
matrix multiplications of
F_{\mu\nu}
by itself, leaving the first and last indices free:

\displaystyle F^{2n}_{\mu\nu}=\underbrace{F_{\mu\alpha_{1}}\mathchoice{F^{{{\alpha_{1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.14876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.38454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.48895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2}}}}}\cdots\mathchoice{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2n-1}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2n-1}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2n-1}}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-2}}\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{2n-1}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\alpha_{2n-1}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.17323pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.40201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[9.50642pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}}_{2n\text{ copies}}\,.

(3)

Likewise,
\tr( M_{\mu\nu} )=\delta^{\mu\nu}M_{\mu\nu}
refers to the trace of any matrix
M_{\mu\nu}
with the Kronecker delta.
3
3

3

As in Chapter
5
, we use the notation
\tr
for the trace over tensor indices and reserve
\Tr
for the trace over gauge indices in the non-abelian setting.

At the risk of being pedantic, we point out that this convention introduces a potentially confusing sign in expressions like
\tr( F^2 )
. In our conventions, the matrix
F^{2}_{\mu\nu}
is defined according to (
3
) as

\displaystyle F^{2}_{\mu\nu}=F_{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\,,

(4)

and therefore its trace is

\displaystyle\tr( F^2_{\mu\nu} )=\delta^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}=F^{\nu\rho}F_{\rho\nu}\,.

(5)

In particular, this means that

\displaystyle\tr( F^2 )=F^{\nu\rho}F_{\rho\nu}=-F^{\rho\nu}F_{\rho\nu}=-F^{2}\,,

(6)

using the notation
F^{2}=F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}
as above and in previous chapters. For this reason, in our notation the Euclidean action
S_{\text{Euc}}
can be written as

\displaystyle S_{\text{Euc}}=\frac{1}{4}\int d^{d}x\,F^{2}\,=-\frac{1}{4}\int d^{d}x\,\tr( F^2 )\,.

(7)

Our convention for the Hilbert stress energy tensor is

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{-2}{\sqrt{g}}\frac{\delta S}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}=g_{\mu\nu}\mathcal{L}-2\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial g^{\mu\nu}}\,.

(8)

For example, the stress tensor associated with the Euclidean Lagrangian (
1
) is

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{\text{Euc}}=\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}F_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha\beta}-F_{\mu\alpha}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,,

(9)

while that for the Lorentzian Lagrangian (
2
) is

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{\text{Lor}}=-\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}F_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha\beta}+F_{\mu\alpha}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=-T_{\mu\nu}^{\text{Euc}}\,.

(10)

So for instance (
9
) agrees with the Maxwell stress tensor (
5
) in Chapter
4
. But as we have emphasized, the stress tensor bilinears which enter into our
{T\overline{T}}
-like deformations are identical in the two cases:

\displaystyle\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)_{\mu\nu}\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)_{\mu\nu}\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{\mu\nu}\,,

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Euc}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{\left(T^{\text{Lor}}\right)^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\,.

(11)

One might object that, although the
deformations
constructed from bilinears in
T_{\mu\nu}
are invariant under reversing the sign of
\mathcal{L}
, the
solution
to flow equations with initial condition
\mathcal{L}_{0}
might differ since
\mathcal{L}_{0}
disagrees by a sign in the two signatures. However, this ambiguity can be absorbed into a re-scaling of the flow parameter
\lambda
. Since all of the analysis of this chapter will hold for either sign of
\lambda
, we are free to modify a flow equation of the form

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathcal{L}}{\partial\lambda}=T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-r\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}

(12)

by sending
\lambda\to\lambda^{\prime}=-\lambda
, so that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(-\mathcal{L}\right)}{\partial\lambda^{\prime}}=T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}-r\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}\,.

(13)

This operation allows us to map a solution to a flow equation with given initial condition
\mathcal{L}_{0}
to a solution with initial condition
-\mathcal{L}_{0}
.

Of course, we have seen in the preceding chapters that the two signs of the
{T\overline{T}}
flow parameter
\lambda
lead to very different behavior. Consider the example of deforming a
2D
CFT. For a “good sign” choice, all of the finite-volume energies in the deformed theory remain real, at least for sufficiently small
\lambda
. The other “bad sign” choice generically leads to a deformed theory where all but finitely many of the energies are complex. One might therefore say that we should be more careful about making arguments which require reversing the sign of
\lambda
, since this can lead to pathologies in the quantum theory.

However, as we mentioned above, in this chapter we are concerned exclusively with flow equations for classical Lagrangians. Indeed, since we work in arbitrary dimension, our deforming operators are not even known to exist at the quantum level. Thus it is perfectly acceptable to treat
\lambda
as a formal variable labeling a one-parameter family of Lagrangians and treat either sign of this variable as equally valid.

This concludes the (somewhat tedious) discussion of our sign manifesto and conventions for the present chapter. We now return to the more interesting task of obtaining a flow equation for an arbitrary abelian gauge theory in
d
dimensions.

2
Enumeration of Trace Structures

We must first parameterize the independent scalar quantities which can be built from
F_{\mu\nu}
. This is obviously a dimension-dependent question. For instance, in
d=4
, every scalar constructed from
F_{\mu\nu}
can be expressed in terms of two combinations

\displaystyle x_{1}=F_{\mu\nu}F^{\nu\mu}=\tr( F^2 )\,,\qquad x_{2}=F^{\mu\sigma}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\rho}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\rho\mu}=\tr( F^4 )\,.

(14)

Equivalently, if we express the
4d
Euclidean field strength tensor as

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}=\begin{bmatrix}0&E_{x}&E_{y}&E_{z}\\
-E_{x}&0&-B_{z}&B_{y}\\
-E_{y}&B_{z}&0&-B_{x}\\
-E_{z}&-B_{y}&B_{x}&0\end{bmatrix}_{\mu\nu}\,,

(15)

then the two invariants satisfy

\displaystyle x_{1}

\displaystyle=-2\left(\vec{E}^{2}+\vec{B}^{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle x_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}x_{1}^{2}-4\left(\vec{E}\cdot\vec{B}\right)^{2}\,.

(16)

The fact that all scalars can be written in terms of
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
might be more familiar if phrased in Lorenztian signature, where the two Lorentz invariants that can be constructed from the field strength tensor in
d=4
are
\vec{E}^{2}-\vec{B}^{2}
and
\vec{E}\cdot\vec{B}
, which are related to
F\wedge\ast F
and
F\wedge F
respectively.

To begin the enumeration of independent scalars, we first recall a very basic fact which follows from the Cayley-Hamilton theorem: every matrix satisfies its own characteristic equation. That is, any
d\times d
matrix
M
obeys a condition of the form

\displaystyle p(M)=M^{d}+c_{d-1}M^{d-1}+\cdots+c_{1}M+(-1)^{d}\det( M )I_{d}=0\,,

(17)

where
I_{d}
is the
d\times d
identity matrix. The coefficients
c_{i}
are functions only of the traces
\mathrm{tr}\big(M^{l}\big)
for
l=1,\cdots,d
. Explicitly, they are given by

\displaystyle c_{i}=\sum_{\{k_{l}\}}\,\prod_{l=1}^{d}\,\frac{(-1)^{k_{l}+1}}{l^{k_{l}}k_{l}!}\left(\mathrm{tr}\,\big(M^{l}\big)\,\right)^{k_{l}}\,,

(18)

where the sum runs over all sets of non-negative integers
k_{l}
which satisfy

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{d}lk_{l}=d-i\,.

(19)

The condition (
17
) implies a limit on the number of independent trace structures that a matrix can have, as we now review.
4
4

4

Another way to think about this fact is that each
\tr( M^k )
is the sum
\lambda_{1}^{k}+\cdots+\lambda_{d}^{k}
where the
\lambda_{i}
are eigenvalues of
M
. Knowing the first
d
such sums gives
d
equations, which is sufficient to determine all
d
of the eigenvalues. From this data, any trace
\tr( M^n )
can be computed. For antisymmetric matrices the eigenvalues come in pairs
\pm\lambda
, except in odd dimensions where there is one unpaired zero eigenvalue.

Suppose that we are given all of the traces

\displaystyle\tr( M )\,,\,\tr\big( M^2 \big)\,,\,\cdots\,,\,\tr\big( M^d \big)\,.

(20)

We claim that all higher traces
\tr\big( M^n \big)
for
n>d
can then be determined. To see this, first note that
\det( M )
is also known, since we can take the trace of equation (
17
) and then solve for
\det( M )
in terms of the known traces:

\displaystyle\det( M )=\frac{(-1)^{d+1}}{d}\left(\tr( M^d )+c_{d-1}\tr( M^{d-1} )+\cdots+c_{1}\tr( M )\right)\,.

(21)

On the other hand, we can first multiply (
17
) by
M^{n-d}
and then trace to find that

\displaystyle\tr( M^{n} )=-\left(c_{d-1}\tr( M^{n-1} )+\cdots+c_{1}\tr( M^{d - n + 1 } )+(-1)^{d}\det( M )\tr( M^{d-n} )\right)\,.

(22)

Since all the coefficients
c_{i}
and
\det( M )
are known, we have therefore solved for
\tr( M^n )
in terms of the traces
\tr\big( M^k \big)
for
k<n
. By induction, this shows that all traces
\tr( M^n )
for
n>d
