c}\mathchoice{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{d}}}}{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{d}}}}{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{d}}}}{F^{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{d}}}}\Tr\left(T^{a}T^{b}T^{c}T^{d}\right)\,.

(3)

Four of the combinations in (
5
) are “double-trace” in the sense that they are the products of two factors, each of which involves a trace over gauge indices. These double-trace terms, in this case, only depend on traces of products of two generators
T^{a}T^{b}
, which have been normalized to
\delta^{ab}
. However, two terms are “single-trace” expressions which involve only one trace over gauge indices, and therefore depend on the trace of a product of four generators.

It is clear that the flow equation (
38
) which we developed in the abelian case is not sufficient here. Whereas a general scalar constructed from an abelian field strength
F_{\mu\nu}
could be expressed in terms of a fairly limited set of variables
x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}
, in the non-abelian setting one seems to need a larger set of variables
x_{i,j}
, where the
j
index distinguishes between terms with inequivalent trace structures over gauge indices. For instance, we have introduced a (fairly
ad hoc
) example of such a naming convention in (
5
): one might use the notation
\big(x_{1,1}\big)^{2}
for the expression in the first line, then call the remaining five expressions
x_{2,1},\cdots,x_{2,5}
, respectively. Note that, in the abelian setting, the objects in first three lines of (
5
) are all equivalent and equal to
x_{1}^{2}
whereas the expressions in the final three lines are all proportional to
x_{2}
. Although the terms
x_{2,1}
and
x_{2,2}
reduce to
x_{1}^{2}
for abelian gauge groups, in the non-abelian case they cannot be written as the square of any scalar quantity, so we have chosen to label them as versions of
x_{2}
.

Although one might be able to write a version of the master flow equation (
38
) in the case of a non-abelian theory, keeping track of all inequivalent
x_{i,j}
, it seems that this analysis would become very involved unless one developed some more clever way to organize the various trace structures. We will not attempt such an analysis in the present work. However, we can already make an obvious, but general, observation: if a seed Lagrangian is only a function of the combination
F^{2}=\Tr( F_{\mu\nu} F^{\mu\nu} )
, then a
{T\overline{T}}
-like flow will never introduce dependence on combinations involving a trace of four or more field strengths. For instance, if we begin with a Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}=f(x)
, where
x=F^{2}
, then

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=\delta_{\mu\nu}\mathcal{L}-4\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\Tr\left(F_{\mu\rho}\mathchoice{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}{F^{{{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}}\right)\,,

(4)

and therefore any bilinears in the stress tensor like
T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}
or
\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}
will involve only double-trace combinations. To leading order, then, the
{T\overline{T}}
-deformed Lagrangian will have terms which involve products of two traces over gauge indices, with two factors of the field strength inside of each trace, but it will contain no terms which are written as a single trace with four field strengths inside the trace. This statement generalizes to higher orders: that is, if a given Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}
contains terms which involve traces of at most
2n
generates
T^{a}
, then the stress tensor
T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{-2}{\sqrt{g}}\frac{\delta\mathcal{L}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}
also involves traces of at most
2n
generators, and therefore bilinears constructed from
T_{\mu\nu}
will contain traces of no more than
2n
generators (although they will involve
products
of such traces).

It follows that, in the non-abelian context, a
{T\overline{T}}
-like deformation of free Yang-Mills theory can never yield the non-abelian Born-Infeld action. As we saw in equation (
8
) of Chapter
5
, the
F^{4}
terms in non-abelian DBI are purely single-trace, whereas
{T\overline{T}}
deformation of a seed theory
\mathcal{L}_{0}\sim F^{2}
will yield only double-trace terms at order
\lambda^{2}
. This deformation, therefore, can never agree with Born-Infeld.

Chapter 7
Details of Supercurrent and Flow Equation Computations

Appendix 7.A
Derivation of
(1,1)
Flow Equation

Here we will show some steps of the calculation which leads to the partial differential equation (
23
) defining the supercurrent-squared deformation of a
(1,1)
free theory with a potential. By setting the superpotential
h
to zero, this calculation also reproduces the PDE (
3
) which describes deformations of the free theory.

We would like to consider what happens when we deform the superspace Lagrangian
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+h(\Phi)
, according to the flow equation (
8
),

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=\mathcal{T}_{+++}^{(t)}\mathcal{T}_{---}^{(t)}-\mathcal{T}_{--+}^{(t)}\mathcal{T}_{++-}^{(t)}.

It will help to introduce some shorthand: we define
A=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
so that
\mathcal{A}^{(0)}=A
, and let
x=t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
and
y=t\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}
as before. Also define the dimensionful combinations

\displaystyle X=\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi=\frac{x}{t},\qquad Y=\left(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)^{2}=\frac{y}{t}.

(1)

Our ansatz for the superspace Lagrangian at finite
t
will be
\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=F(x,y)A+h(\Phi)
.

With this ansatz, some of the terms in (
6
) will not contribute to the right side of (
8
). For instance, the terms
\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}D_{\pm}\partial_{\pm}\Phi
will be proportional to
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi=A
. However, every term in the superspace supercurrent is proportional to
D_{+}\Phi
,
D_{-}\Phi
, or
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
. Therefore, when we construct a bilinear in
\mathcal{T}
, any term containing
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
will not contribute because it can only appear multiplying another term which contains at least one of
D_{\pm}\Phi
, which vanishes because
\left(D_{\pm}\Phi\right)^{2}=0
.

For our special ansatz, we will re-write the components of
\mathcal{T}
keeping only terms which contribute to bilinears,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++-}&\sim\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{+}\mathcal{A},\\
\mathcal{T}_{+++}&\sim\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{---}&\sim\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{--+}&\sim\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}+\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)-D_{-}\mathcal{A}.\end{split}

(2)

The terms are

\displaystyle D_{+}\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\sim FD_{+}A+h^{\prime}(\Phi)D_{+}\Phi,

\displaystyle D_{-}\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\sim FD_{-}A+h^{\prime}(\Phi)D_{-}\Phi,

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim F\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\Phi,

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim-F\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\Phi,

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim F\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\Phi,

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{-}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim-F\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi,

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{+}A,

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{-}A,

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{+}A,

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{-}A,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{Y}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{+}A,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\sqrt{Y}\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{+}A,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim-\sqrt{Y}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{-}A,

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim-\sqrt{Y}\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{-}A,

where
\sim
means “equal modulo terms which are proportional to
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
,” since any products involving these terms will contain two nilpotent factors and thus vanish.

The first piece of supercurrent-squared is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++|+}\mathcal{T}_{--|-}&=\left(-F\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\Phi+\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{-}A+\sqrt{Y}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{+}A\right)\\
&\times\left(F\partial_{--}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}D_{+}A-\sqrt{Y}\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{-}A\right),\\
&=-F^{2}XA-FX\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi D_{+}A+FX\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}AD_{-}\Phi\\
&+X^{2}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}\right)^{2}D_{-}AD_{+}A+F\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\sqrt{Y}XD_{+}AD_{-}\Phi\\
&+FX\sqrt{Y}\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}A-YX\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\right)^{2}D_{+}AD_{-}A.\end{split}

(3)

The second piece is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++|-}\mathcal{T}_{--|+}&=\left(F\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)D_{+}A-G^{\prime}D_{+}\Phi-\sqrt{Y}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{-}A\right)\\
&\times\left(-F\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi+\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)D_{-}A-G^{\prime}D_{-}\Phi+\sqrt{Y}\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\cdot D_{+}A\right),\\
&=F^{2}XA+F\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\Phi D_{-}A+FX\sqrt{Y}\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{-}\Phi D_{+}A\\
&+FX\sqrt{Y}\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{-}AD_{+}\Phi-F\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}AD_{+}\Phi\\
&+\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)^{2}D_{+}AD_{-}A-YX\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\right)^{2}D_{-}AD_{+}A\\
&-G^{\prime}\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)D_{+}\Phi D_{-}A+\left(G^{\prime}\right)^{2}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi-G^{\prime}\sqrt{Y}\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{+}\Phi D_{+}A\\
&-G^{\prime}\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)D_{+}AD_{-}\Phi+G^{\prime}\sqrt{Y}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}D_{-}AD_{-}\Phi.\end{split}

(4)

Using the definitions
A=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
,
X=\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
, and
\sqrt{Y}=D_{+}D_{-}\Phi
, we see that the products appearing in the above bilinears can be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{+}\Phi D_{+}A&=D_{+}\Phi D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right)=D_{+}\Phi D_{+}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi=A\partial_{++}\Phi,\\
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}A&=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right)=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi=-A\sqrt{Y},\\
D_{-}\Phi D_{+}A&=D_{-}\Phi D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right)=-D_{-}\Phi D_{+}\Phi D_{+}D_{-}\Phi=A\sqrt{Y},\\
D_{-}\Phi D_{-}A&=D_{-}\Phi D_{-}\left(D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi\right)=-D_{-}\Phi D_{+}\Phi D_{-}D_{-}\Phi=A\partial_{--}\Phi,\\
D_{+}AD_{-}A&=\left(\partial_{++}\Phi D_{-}\Phi-D_{+}\Phi\sqrt{Y}\right)\left(-\sqrt{Y}D_{-}\Phi-\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\Phi\right)=(X+Y)A.\end{split}

(5)

So after simplifying,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++|+}\mathcal{T}_{--|-}&=-F^{2}XA-2FX^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}A-X^{2}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}\right)^{2}A(X+Y)-2F\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}YXA,\\
\mathcal{T}_{++|-}\mathcal{T}_{--|+}&=F^{2}XA+2FX\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)A+2FXY\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}A+\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)^{2}(X+Y)A\\
&\quad+\left(\left(h^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2h^{\prime}\sqrt{Y}\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)-2\sqrt{Y}Xh^{\prime}\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}\right)A.\end{split}

(6)

In particular, we see that every term appearing in (
6
) is proportional to
A=D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
. This means that the deformation only generates a change in the first term of our ansatz
\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=FD_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi+h(\Phi)
, but it does not source any change in the potential. This justifies our choice of ansatz which leaves the potential as
h(\Phi)
rather than allowing a more general function
G(t,\Phi)
with
G(0,\Phi)=h(\Phi)
.

Adding the contributions gives,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++|+}\mathcal{T}_{--|-}+\mathcal{T}_{++|-}\mathcal{T}_{--|+}&=\Big[\left(Y-X\right)F^{2}-2FX(X+Y)\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}+2h^{\prime}\sqrt{Y}\left(X\frac{\partial F}{\partial X}-F\right)\\
&\quad-2\sqrt{Y}Xh^{\prime}\frac{\partial F}{\partial Y}+\left(h^{\prime}\right)^{2}\Big]A.\end{split}

(7)

Setting this deformation equal to
\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathcal{A}^{(t)}
, and multiplying both sides by
t
to convert dimensionlful variables
X
and
Y
into their dimensionless counterparts
x
and
y
, gives our final result (
23
),

\displaystyle x\frac{\partial}{\partial x}F+y\frac{\partial}{\partial y}F

\displaystyle=\left(y-x\right)F^{2}-2Fx(x+y)\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}+\left(h^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2h^{\prime}\sqrt{y}\left(x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}-F\right)

(8)

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{y}xh^{\prime}\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}.

(9)

We were unable to find a closed-form solution to (
23
) in the general case. However, we can find the solution in a few special cases. If
y=0
, (
9
) reduces to

\displaystyle xF^{\prime}(x)=-x\left(F(x)^{2}+2F(x)F^{\prime}(x)x\right),

(10)

which is solved by the Dirac-type ansatz
F(x)=\frac{\sqrt{1+4x}-1}{2x}
. If
x=0
, equation (
9
) is solved by
F(y)=\frac{1}{1-y}
. If
y=-x
, the second term on the right side of (
9
) drops out and the solution is
F(x)=\frac{1}{1+2x}
. See also Section
2
, where we show that an exact solution can be found in the case where
h=0
if we are willing to impose part of the superspace equations of motion.

Appendix 7.B
Derivation of
(0,1)
Flow Equation

Next we would like to write down a partial differential equation, similar to (
3
) in the
(1,1)
case, which determines the Lagrangian deformed by the
(0,1)
supercurrent-squared operator at finite
t
.

Define the three combinations of fields

\displaystyle x=t\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi,\qquad y=t\left(D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)^{2},\qquad z=tD_{+}\Phi D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi,

(1)

and their dimensionful counterparts
X=\frac{x}{t}
,
Y=\frac{y}{t}
,
Z=\frac{z}{t}
. Our ansatz for the Lagrangian at finite
t
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(t)}=F(x,y,z)\left(D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi+\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)+F_{2,-}(x,y,z)\left(\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi\right).

(2)

Since the function
F_{2,-}
is fermionic, it actually contains several different functions since we may combine the fields
\Phi,\Psi_{-}
and derivatives in a few independent ways to obtain a fermionic function. We will expand
F_{2,-}
as follows:

\displaystyle F_{2,-}=G(x,y,z)D_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+H(x,y,z)\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+J(x,y,z)\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}.

(3)

Altogether our ansatz for the deformed Lagrangian is,

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=F\left(D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi+\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(GD_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+H\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+J\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\right)\left(\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi\right).

(4)

This is a
(0,1)
superspace Lagrangian with the functional dependence

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{A}\left(\Phi,\Psi_{-},D_{+}\Phi,D_{+}\Psi_{-},\partial_{\pm\pm}\Phi,\partial_{\pm\pm}\Psi_{-},D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi\right).

(5)

Following the procedure of section (
1
), we can consider a transformation
x^{\pm\pm}\to x^{\pm\pm}+a^{\pm\pm}
and extract the components of conserved currents. In this case, they are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++++}&=\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}+\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}}-\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}\right),\\
\mathcal{T}_{++--}&=\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}+\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}}-\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)-\mathcal{A},\\
\mathcal{S}_{++-}&=\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}-\mathcal{A}\right)+\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Psi_{-}}\cr&+\left(\partial_{--}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi},\\
\mathcal{S}_{---}&=\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}+D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)+\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Psi_{-}}+\partial_{--}^{2}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}.\end{split}

(6)

Next we will compute each of these contributions. As before, we will drop terms which are proportional to
\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi
, since every term in
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{T}
is proportional to either
\Psi_{-}
or to
D_{+}\Phi
, so any terms involving both of these nilpotent factors will not contribute to bilinears. We will also introduce the shorthand
A=D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
and
B=\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}
.

Doing this, we see that:

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim FD_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi+\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)^{2}\left(A+B\right),

(7)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim FA+x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\left(A+B\right),

(8)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}\left(D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)\left(A+B\right)+Fx

(9)

\displaystyle\quad-\partial_{++}\Phi\left(GD_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+H\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+J\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\right)\Psi_{-},

(10)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}\left(D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)+F\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}

(11)

\displaystyle\quad-\partial_{--}\Phi\left(GD_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+H\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+J\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\right)\Psi_{-},

(12)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}}

\displaystyle\sim 0,

(13)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}}

\displaystyle\sim 0,

(14)

\displaystyle D_{+}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim D_{+}\left(x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}(A+B)\right)+\Big(x\frac{\partial G}{\partial x}D_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+x\frac{\partial H}{\partial x}\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}

(15)

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+x\frac{\partial J}{\partial x}\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\Big)\cdot\Big(D_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi-\Psi_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi\Big),

(16)

\displaystyle D_{+}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta\partial_{++}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim D_{+}\left(\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}(A+B)\right)+\Big(\frac{\partial G}{\partial x}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi

(17)

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial H}{\partial x}\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+\frac{\partial J}{\partial x}\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{3}\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\Big)\Big(D_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi-\Psi_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi\Big),

(18)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}-D_{+}\Phi\left(D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)^{2}\right),

(19)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Phi D_{+}\left(\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}-D_{+}\Phi\left(D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right)^{2}\right),

(20)

\displaystyle\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Psi_{-}}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}(A+B)+F\Psi_{-}\right),

(21)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\Psi_{-}}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}(A+B)+F\Psi_{-}\right),

(22)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{--}\partial_{++}\Phi\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}D_{+}\Phi(A+B)\right)\sim 0,

(23)

\displaystyle\partial_{--}^{2}\Phi\frac{\delta\mathcal{A}}{\delta D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi}

\displaystyle\sim\partial_{--}^{2}\Phi\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}D_{+}\Phi(A+B)\right)\sim 0,

(24)

\displaystyle D_{+}\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle\sim\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}D_{+}x+\frac{\partial F}{\partial y}D_{+}y+\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}D_{+}z\right)(A+B)+F\left(D_{+}A+D_{+}B\right)

(25)

\displaystyle+\left(GD_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi+H\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Psi_{-}+J\partial_{--}\Phi\partial_{++}\Psi_{-}\right)

(26)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(D_{+}\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Phi-\Psi_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi\right).

(27)

We will argue that the coupled differential equations for
F,G,H
, and
J
resulting from (
7
) are consistent. This will be the case if they do not source any additional combinations of fields that do not appear in the ansatz (
4
).

The only thing that could spoil consistency is a
D_{+}x
term, since

\displaystyle D_{+}x=\left(D_{+}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)\partial_{--}\Phi+\partial_{++}\Phi D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi.

(28)

We have already allowed for dependence on
D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi
in our Lagrangian, but terms proportional to
D_{+}\partial_{++}\Phi
are forbidden. We will show that, in the
\mathcal{S}\cdot\mathcal{T}
deformation resulting from (
7
), all
D_{+}x
terms drop out.

Tracking only the
D_{+}x
terms in bilinears, the supercurrent components are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}_{++-}&\sim xD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)\left(A+B\right)+\ldots,\\
\mathcal{T}_{++--}&\sim x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}(A+B)-FB-\partial_{--}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right),\\
\mathcal{S}_{---}&\sim\left(\partial_{--}\phi\right)^{2}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)(A+B)+\ldots,\\
\mathcal{T}_{++++}&\sim FD_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi+\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)^{2}(A+B)-\partial_{++}\Phi\frac{\partial F}{\partial z}D_{+}\left(D_{+}\Phi\Psi_{-}D_{+}\Psi_{-}\right),\end{split}

(29)

where
\ldots
indicates terms that are not proportional to
D_{+}x
or
D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)
.

The relevant contributions in the deformation are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\hskip-20.0pt\mathcal{T}_{++++}\mathcal{S}_{---}-\mathcal{T}_{++--}\mathcal{S}_{++-}\sim\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)(A+B)\cdot\left(FD_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi+\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\left(\partial_{++}\Phi\right)^{2}(A+B)\right)\\
&\quad-xD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)(A+B)\cdot\left(x\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}(A+B)-FB\right)+\ldots,\\
&=\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)(FBD_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi)-xD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)(-FAB)+\ldots,\end{split}

(30)

where we have used the fermionic nature of
A
and
B
so
A^{2}=B^{2}=(A+B)^{2}=0
. However in the last line, we recognize that
\left(\partial_{--}\Phi\right)^{2}D_{+}\Phi\partial_{++}\Phi=xA
, since
x=\partial_{++}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
and
A=D_{+}\Phi\partial_{--}\Phi
, so

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{T}_{++++}\mathcal{S}_{---}-\mathcal{T}_{++--}\mathcal{S}_{++-}&\sim xD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)FBA+xD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)FAB=0,\end{split}

(31)

and thus the problematic
D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial F}{\partial x}\right)
terms do not contribute.

Appendix 7.C
The
{\cal S}
-multiplet in Components

In this appendix, we provide the component expansion of the superfields of the
{\cal S}
-multiplet introduced in section
1
. The results presented below are equivalent to the results first obtained in
[
Dumitrescu:2011iu
]
up to differences in notation.

The constraints (
1
)
are solved in terms of component fields by,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{\pm\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j_{\pm\pm}-i\theta^{\pm}S_{\pm\pm\pm}-i\theta^{\mp}\left(S_{\mp\pm\pm}\mp 2\sqrt{2}i\bar{\rho}_{\pm}\right)-i{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}\bar{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}

(6)

\displaystyle-i{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\left(\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}\pm 2\sqrt{2}i\rho_{\pm}\right)-\theta^{\pm}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm}+\theta^{\mp}{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\left(A\mp{\frac{k+k^{\prime}}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\bar{Y}_{\pm\pm}+i{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}Y_{\pm\pm}\pm i\theta^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\bar{G}_{\pm\pm}\mp i\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}G_{\pm\pm}

\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{2}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}S_{\mp\pm\pm}\mp\frac{1}{2}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\partial_{\pm\pm}\left(S_{\pm\mp\mp}\pm 2\sqrt{2}i\bar{\rho}_{\mp}\right)

\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{2}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\theta^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}\mp\frac{1}{2}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\theta^{\mp}\partial_{\pm\pm}\left(\bar{S}_{\pm\mp\mp}\mp 2\sqrt{2}i\rho_{\mp}\right)

\displaystyle+{\frac{1}{4}}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{\pm\pm}^{2}j_{\mp\mp}~.

Let us introduce the usual useful combinations:

y^{\pm\pm}=x^{\pm\pm}-\frac{i}{2}\theta^{\pm}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}
and
\tilde{y}^{\pm\pm}=x^{\pm\pm}\mp\frac{i}{2}\theta^{\pm}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}
.
The chiral superfields
\chi_{\pm}
are

\displaystyle\chi_{+}

\displaystyle=-i\lambda_{+}(y)-i\theta^{+}\bar{G}_{++}(y)+\theta^{-}\left(E(y)+{\frac{k}{2}}\right)+{\bar{\theta}}^{-}C^{(-)}+\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{++}\bar{\lambda}_{-}(y)\,,

(7)

\displaystyle\chi_{-}

\displaystyle=-i\lambda_{-}(y)-\theta^{+}\left(\bar{E}(y)-{\frac{k}{2}}\right)+i\theta^{-}G_{--}(y)-{\bar{\theta}}^{+}C^{(+)}-\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{--}\bar{\lambda}_{+}(y)~,

(8)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}

\displaystyle=\pm\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}+\sqrt{2}i\rho_{\pm}~,

(9)

\displaystyle E

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\Theta-A\right)+{\frac{i}{4}}\left(\partial_{++}j_{--}-\partial_{--}j_{++}\right)~,

(10)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\partial_{++}G_{--}-\partial_{--}G_{++}~,

(11)

and the twisted-(anti-)chiral superfields
{\cal Y}_{\pm}
are given by

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{+}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\rho_{+}(\bar{\tilde{y}})+\theta^{-}\left(F(\bar{\tilde{y}})+{\frac{k^{\prime}}{2}}\right)-i{\bar{\theta}}^{+}Y_{++}(\bar{\tilde{y}})-{\bar{\theta}}^{-}C^{(-)}+\sqrt{2}i\theta^{-}\bar{\theta}^{+}\partial_{++}\rho_{-}(\bar{\tilde{y}})\,,

(12)

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{-}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\rho_{-}(\tilde{y})-\theta^{+}\left(F(\tilde{y})-{\frac{k^{\prime}}{2}}\right)+{\bar{\theta}}^{+}C^{(+)}-i{\bar{\theta}}^{-}Y_{--}(\tilde{y})+\sqrt{2}i\theta^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{--}\rho_{+}(\tilde{y})~,

(13)

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\Theta+A\right)-{\frac{i}{4}}\left(\partial_{++}j_{--}+\partial_{--}j_{++}\right)~,

(14)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\partial_{++}Y_{--}-\partial_{--}Y_{++}~.

(15)

For the FZ-multiplet defined by the constraints (
10
), the
{\cal S}
-multiplet reduces to a set of
4+4
real independent
component fields described by the
j_{\pm\pm}

U(1)_{A}
axial conserved
R
-symmetry current
(
\partial_{++}j_{--}-\partial_{--}j_{++}=0
). In addition, there is a complex scalar field
v(x)
,
see eq. (
14
),
together with the independent supersymmetry current and energy momentum tensor:

\displaystyle S_{\pm\pm\pm}(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle~~iD_{\pm}{\mathcal{J}}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(16a)

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}{\mathcal{J}}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(16b)

\displaystyle S_{\mp\pm\pm}(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-iD_{\mp}{\mathcal{J}}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=\pm i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(16c)

\displaystyle\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle~~i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}{\mathcal{J}}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=\mp iD_{\pm}{\cal V}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(16d)

\displaystyle T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm}(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle~~\frac{1}{2}{[}D_{\pm},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}{]}{\mathcal{J}}_{\pm\pm}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~,

(16e)

\displaystyle\Theta(x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}{[}D_{+},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}{]}{\mathcal{J}}_{--}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}=-\frac{1}{2}{[}D_{-},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}{]}{\mathcal{J}}_{++}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}

(16f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}D_{+}D_{-}{\cal V}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}+\frac{1}{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{{\cal V}}(\zeta)|_{\theta=0}~.

For the FZ-multiplet, the following relation holds:

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{\pm\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j_{\pm\pm}-i\theta^{\pm}S_{\pm\pm\pm}-i{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}\bar{S}_{\pm\pm\pm}+i\theta^{\mp}S_{\mp\pm\pm}+i{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}

(21)

\displaystyle-\theta^{\pm}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}T_{\pm\pm\pm\pm}+\theta^{\mp}{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\Theta+i\theta^{+}\theta^{-}\partial_{\pm\pm}\bar{v}+i{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{\pm\pm}v

\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{2}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}S_{\mp\pm\pm}\pm\frac{1}{2}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{\mp}\partial_{\pm\pm}S_{\pm\mp\mp}

\displaystyle\mp\frac{1}{2}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\theta^{\pm}\partial_{\pm\pm}\bar{S}_{\mp\pm\pm}\pm\frac{1}{2}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\theta^{\mp}\partial_{\pm\pm}\bar{S}_{\pm\mp\mp}

\displaystyle+{\frac{1}{4}}\theta^{+}\theta^{-}{\bar{\theta}}^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{\pm\pm}^{2}j_{\mp\mp}~.

Moreover, the chiral superfields
\chi_{\pm}
are set to zero
and the twisted-(anti-)chiral superfields
{\cal Y}_{\pm}=D_{\pm}{\cal V}
are given by

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\bar{S}_{-++}(\bar{\tilde{y}})+\theta^{-}\,G(\bar{\tilde{y}})-i{\bar{\theta}}^{+}\partial_{++}v(\bar{\tilde{y}})+\theta^{-}\bar{\theta}^{+}\partial_{++}\bar{S}_{+--}(\bar{\tilde{y}})~,

(22a)

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\bar{S}_{+--}(\tilde{y})-\theta^{+}G(\tilde{y})-i{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{--}v(\tilde{y})+\theta^{+}{\bar{\theta}}^{-}\partial_{++}\bar{S}_{---}(\tilde{y})~,

(22b)

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Theta-{\frac{i}{2}}\partial_{++}j_{--}~.

(22c)

Appendix 7.D
Details of the
(2,2)
FZ Multiplet Calculation

In this appendix, we compute the fields
\mathcal{J}_{\pm\pm}
and
\sigma
appearing in
the FZ-multiplet for Lagrangians of a chiral superfield
\Phi
with the general form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}=\left(\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{A}(\Phi,D_{\pm}\Phi,D_{+}D_{-}\Phi,\partial_{\pm\pm}\Phi,\text{c.c.})\right)+\left(\int d^{2}\theta\,W(\Phi)\right)+\left(\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\Phi})\right)~,

(1)

where “
c.c.
” indicates dependence on the conjugates
\overline{\Phi},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
, and

\partial_{\pm\pm}\overline{\Phi}
. To do this, we will minimally couple the theory to supergravity using the old-minimal supergravity
formulation and extract the currents which couple to the metric superfield
H^{\pm\pm}
and the chiral compensator
\sigma
.
The minimal coupling prescription involves promoting
\mathcal{L}_{0}
to
1
1

1

Conforming to
notation of
[
Grisaru:1994dm
,
Grisaru:1995dr
,
Grisaru:1995kn
,
Grisaru:1995py
,
Gates:1995du
]
,
in this section we will sometimes
use the index notations
\alpha=+,-
and
m=++,--
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}\longrightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\text{SUGRA}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int d^{4}\theta\,E^{-1}\,\mathcal{A}(\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{\pm\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\text{c.c.})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int d^{2}\theta\,\mathcal{E}^{-1}\,W(\boldsymbol{\Phi})\right)+\left(\int d^{2}\overline{\theta}\,\overline{\mathcal{E}}^{-1}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu(\overline{\boldsymbol{\Phi}})\right)~.

(2)

Here
\nabla_{\pm}
is the derivative which is covariant with respect to the full local supergravity gauge group,

E^{-1}
is the full superspace measure,
\mathcal{E}^{-1}
is the chiral measure, and
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
is the covariantly
chiral version of the chiral superfield
\Phi
—that is,
\overline{\nabla}_{\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi}=0
whereas
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\Phi=0
.

Expressions for these supercovariant derivatives and measures have been worked out in a series of papers

[
Grisaru:1994dm
,
Grisaru:1995dr
,
Grisaru:1995kn
,
Grisaru:1995py
,
Gates:1995du
]
from which we will import the results
that we need for our analysis.
To leading order in
H^{m}
,
the linearized inverse superdeterminant of the supervielbein is

\displaystyle E^{-1}=1-\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+},D_{+}\right]H^{++}-\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-},D_{-}\right]H^{--}\,,

(3)

while the chiral measure is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{-1}

\displaystyle=e^{-2\sigma}\left(1\cdot e^{iH^{m}\overset{\leftarrow}{\partial_{m}}}\right)=1-2\sigma+i(\partial_{m}H^{m})+\cdots~,

(4)

where the ellipsis are terms of higher-order in
H^{m}
and
\sigma
.
The covariantly chiral superfield
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
is related to the ordinary chiral superfield
\Phi
by

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Phi}=e^{iH^{m}\partial_{m}}\Phi=\Phi+i\left(H^{++}\partial_{++}+H^{--}\partial_{--}\right)\Phi+\mathcal{O}(H^{2})~.

(5)

The spinor supercovariant derivatives
\nabla_{\pm}
are

\displaystyle\nabla_{\alpha}=E_{\alpha}+\Omega_{\alpha}M+\Gamma_{\alpha}\overline{M}+\Sigma_{\alpha}N~,

(6)

where
M
and
N
are linear combinations of the Lorentz,
U(1)_{V}
, and
U(1)_{A}
generators which act on spinors as

\displaystyle[M,\psi_{\pm}]

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\psi_{\pm}~,

\displaystyle[M,\overline{\psi}_{\pm}]

\displaystyle=0~,

(7a)

\displaystyle[\overline{M},\overline{\psi}_{\pm}]

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{2}\overline{\psi}_{\pm}~,

\displaystyle[\overline{M},\psi]

\displaystyle=0~,

(7b)

\displaystyle[N,\psi_{\pm}]

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}\psi_{\pm}~,

\displaystyle[N,\overline{\psi}_{\pm}]

\displaystyle=+\frac{i}{2}\overline{\psi}_{\pm}~.

(7c)

The spinor inverse of the
supervielbein
E_{\alpha}=E_{\alpha}{}^{M}\partial_{M}
,
and the structure group connections

\Omega_{\alpha}
,
\Gamma_{\alpha}
, and
\Sigma_{\alpha}
can be expressed to linear order in terms of the metric
superfield
H^{\pm\pm}
and an unconstrained complex
scalar compensator
S
. In the case of old-minimal supergravity,
the unconstrained superfield
S
is related to the chiral compensator
\sigma
by

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\sigma-\frac{i}{2}\partial_{m}H^{m}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+},D_{+}\right]H^{++}-\frac{1}{2}\left[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-},D_{-}\right]H^{--}~,

(8)

to linear order.
In the following analysis we will first obtain expressions for the supercovariant derivatives in terms of

S=S(H^{m},\sigma)
,
and use (
8
)
to give them in terms of
H^{m}
and
\sigma
.

The spinorial inverse of the supervielbein is given at first order in the prepotentials by

E_{\pm}=(1+\overline{S})D_{\pm}+i(D_{\pm}H^{m})\partial_{m}-2\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}D_{\pm}H^{\mp\mp}\right)D_{\mp}~,

(9)

together with their complex conjugates.
Meanwhile, the connections
\Omega_{\alpha}
,
\Gamma_{\alpha}
, and
\Sigma_{\alpha}
can be written to leading order as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm 2D_{\pm}\left(S+\overline{S}\right)\mp 2D_{\mp}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}D_{\pm}H^{\mp\mp}~,

(10a)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2iD_{\pm}\overline{S}+2iD_{\mp}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}D_{\pm}H^{\mp\mp}~,

(10b)

\displaystyle\Omega_{\pm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp 2D_{\mp}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}D_{\pm}H^{\mp\mp}~.

(10c)

Using (
6
), the vielbeins (
9
), and the expression (
5
) for
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
,
we find the supercovariant derivatives

\displaystyle\nabla_{\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\overline{S}\right)D_{\pm}\Phi+2i(D_{\pm}H^{m})\partial_{m}\Phi+iH^{m}(D_{\pm}\partial_{m}\Phi)-2\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}D_{\pm}H^{\mp\mp}\right)D_{\mp}\Phi~,

(11)

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{\pm}\overline{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}

\displaystyle=\left(1+S\right)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi}-2i\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}H^{m}\right)\partial_{m}\overline{\Phi}-iH^{m}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\partial_{m}\overline{\Phi})-2\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}D_{\mp}H^{\mp\mp}\right)\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\mp}\overline{\Phi}\,.

(12)

To compute the second supercovariant derivatives
acting on
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
and
\overline{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}
, we must include the contributions from

\Omega_{\alpha}
,
\Gamma_{\alpha}
,
\Sigma_{\alpha}
, and their conjugates. One finds

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{+}\nabla_{+}\boldsymbol{\Phi}

\displaystyle=i(1+S+\overline{S})\partial_{++}\Phi-2(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{+}H^{--})D_{-}\Phi+2i(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{+}H^{m})\partial_{m}\Phi

\displaystyle\quad-H^{m}\partial_{++}\partial_{m}\Phi+2\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}(S+\overline{S})+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}H^{--}\right)D_{+}\Phi~,

(13)

\displaystyle\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\boldsymbol{\Phi}

\displaystyle=(1+2\overline{S})D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+2i(D_{+}D_{-}H^{m})\partial_{m}\Phi-2i(D_{-}H^{m})D_{+}\partial_{m}\Phi

\displaystyle\quad+2i(D_{+}H^{m})D_{-}\partial_{m}\Phi+iH^{m}D_{+}D_{-}\partial_{m}\Phi-2(D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{-}H^{++})D_{+}\Phi

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{+}H^{--}\right)D_{-}\Phi~,

(14)

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{-}\nabla_{-}\boldsymbol{\Phi}

\displaystyle=i(1+S+\overline{S})\partial_{--}\Phi-2(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{-}H^{++})D_{+}\Phi+2i(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{-}H^{m})\partial_{m}\Phi

\displaystyle\quad-H^{m}\partial_{--}\partial_{m}\Phi+2\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}(S+\overline{S})+\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}H^{++}\right)D_{-}\Phi~,

(15)

together with their complex conjugates.
Armed with these expressions, we can linearize the supergravity couplings in (
2
).
First let us consider the contribution from the D-term. We would like to extract the terms proportional to
H^{\pm\pm}
and
\sigma
in

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=

\displaystyle~\int d^{4}\theta\,E^{-1}\,\mathcal{A}(\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\nabla_{\pm\pm}\boldsymbol{\Phi},\text{c.c.})~,

\displaystyle\sim\int d^{4}\theta\Big(H^{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}[D_{\alpha},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}]\mathcal{A}+i\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\Phi}H^{m}\partial_{m}\Phi+\left(\nabla_{\alpha}\boldsymbol{\Phi}-D_{\alpha}\Phi\right)\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{\alpha}\Phi}

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}\left(\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\boldsymbol{\Phi}-D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{m}\Phi}\left(\nabla_{m}\boldsymbol{\Phi}-\partial_{m}\Phi\right)+\text{c.c.}\Big)~,

(16)

where
\nabla_{\pm\pm}=-i\left\{\nabla_{\pm},\overline{\nabla}_{\pm}\right\}
. Doing so, we see that the currents which couple to

H^{\pm\pm}
are

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\mathcal{J}_{++}

\displaystyle=[D_{+},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}]\Bigg[\,\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+i\Bigg[\,\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)-\partial_{++}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)-\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{++}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)-\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\partial_{++}\Phi-2D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{-}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)+2D_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\partial_{++}\Phi+2iD_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+2iD_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}^{2}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}\partial_{++}\Phi\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\Bigg[\,-D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)-D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+iD_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)-i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\text{ c.c. }~,

(17)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-50.0pt\mathcal{J}_{--}

\displaystyle=[D_{-},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}]\Bigg[\,\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{A}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}\Phi

\displaystyle\hskip 60.0pt+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+i\Bigg[\,\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)-\partial_{--}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\Phi\right)-\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\partial_{--}\Phi

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{-}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{--}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)-\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi-2D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{-}D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)+2D_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt-2D_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\partial_{--}\Phi+2iD_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt+2iD_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}\Phi\right)+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}\partial_{++}\partial_{--}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}\partial_{--}^{2}\Phi\,\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+2\Bigg[\,-D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)+D_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}D_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+iD_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)-i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}D_{-}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\text{ c.c. }~,

(18)

where
+\text{c.c.}
means to add the complex conjugates of
all
preceding terms (including the real quantity

\frac{1}{2}[D_{\pm},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}]\mathcal{A}
for which the complex conjugate merely removes the factor of
\frac{1}{2}
).

The field
\mathcal{V}
which appears in our deformation (
10
) receives two contributions, one from the D-term coupling
which depends only on
\mathcal{A}
, and one from the F-term coupling which depends only on the superpotential
W
.
Adding them, we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\Bigg[\,-\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{\alpha}\Phi}D_{\alpha}\Phi+2\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{+}\nabla_{-}\Phi}D_{+}D_{-}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{m}\Phi}\partial_{m}\Phi+\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{m}\overline{\Phi}}\partial_{m}\overline{\Phi}

(19)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\Phi}D_{+}\Phi\right)+2iD_{+}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{++}\overline{\Phi}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~+2i\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\right)+2iD_{-}\left(\frac{\partial\mathcal{A}}{\partial\nabla_{--}\overline{\Phi}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+2W(\Phi)~.

Appendix 7.E
On-Shell Simplification of Chiral Scalar Theories

In this appendix, we prove the following claim which applies to
(2,2)
theories of a chiral superfield of the form considered in Chapter
3
: one can drop all terms which involve products of
(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)
or
(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi})
and the four-fermion term
|D\Phi|^{4}=D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}

when the equations of motion are satisfied. This is the
(2,2)
analogue of the statement that terms involving products of
y=t(D_{+}D_{-}\Phi)^{2}
and the two-fermion term
D_{+}\Phi D_{-}\Phi
can be dropped on-shell in
(1,1)
theories, as shown in Section
2
.

To see this for the
(2,2)
models we consider,
it suffices to consider a superspace Lagrangian of
the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}\theta\,\mathcal{A}\left(\Phi,D_{\pm}\Phi,D_{+}D_{-}\Phi,\partial_{\pm\pm}\Phi,\text{c.c.}\right)

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}\theta\,\left(K(\Phi,\overline{\Phi})+f(x,\overline{x},y)|D\Phi|^{4}\right)~,

(1)

which has the superspace equation of motion

\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}K_{\Phi}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\Bigg(D_{\alpha}\left[\frac{\partial(f|D\Phi|^{4})}{\partial D_{\alpha}\Phi}\right]-D_{+}D_{-}\left[\frac{\partial(f|D\Phi|^{4})}{\partial D_{+}D_{-}\Phi}\right]-\partial_{m}\left[\frac{\partial(f|D\Phi|^{4})}{\partial(\partial_{m}\Phi)}\right]\Bigg)

(2)

for
\Phi
, and the conjugate equation of motion for
\overline{\Phi}
.
If we multiply (
2
) on both sides by the four-fermion term

|D\Phi|^{4}=D_{+}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\overline{\Phi}D_{-}\Phi\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
then any term containing
(D_{\pm}\Phi)
and

(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\pm}\overline{\Phi})

fermions in (
2
) will vanish by nilpotency. On the left, the only surviving term is

K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}
, while on the right we get contributions from the first and second terms:

\displaystyle K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)|D\Phi|^{4}=\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)|D\Phi|^{4}\Bigg(\lambda\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left[\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi})(\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi})\right]-\left(\frac{x+\overline{x}}{\lambda}\right)f\Bigg)\,.

(3)

On collecting terms, the previous equation turns into

\displaystyle\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)|D\Phi|^{4}\left\{K_{\Phi\overline{\Phi}}+\left(\frac{x+\overline{x}}{\lambda}\right)f-\lambda\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\left[\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}(\partial_{--}\overline{\Phi})(\partial_{++}\overline{\Phi})\right]\right\}=0~.

(4)

The parenthesis multiplying

(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi})|D\Phi|^{4}

in the previous expression
does not vanish in general,
at least for

\lambda
small enough.
Then for (
4
) to be satisfied,
the equation

\displaystyle\left(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{+}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{-}\overline{\Phi}\right)|D\Phi|^{4}=0

(5)

has to hold
when the equations of motion are satisfied. This justifies our claim in section
1

that we may drop all terms involving the product
y|D\Phi|^{4}
in the deformation, assuming we restrict to on-shell configurations.

Appendix 7.F
On-Shell Simplification of Born-Infeld-Type Theories

This appendix is devoted to deriving the on-shell relation (
37
).
We are going to prove this holds for an action of the form (
26
).
Let us start by considering the following Lagrangian

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int d^{2}\theta\,W^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\int d^{2}\bar{\theta}\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}+\int d^{2}\theta d^{2}{\bar{\theta}}\,W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\,\Omega\big[D^{2}W^{2},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\big]~.

(1)

We recall that
W_{\alpha}
and
\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}
satisfy the Bianchi identity

D^{\alpha}W_{\alpha}=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\dot{\alpha}}
, whose solution is given in terms of a real but otherwise unconstrained scalar prepotential superfield
V
:

W_{\alpha}=-1/4\,\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}D_{\alpha}V

and

\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}=-1/4\,D^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}V
.
It is a straightforward calculation to derive the
{\rm EOM}

by varying the action (
1
) with respect to the prepotential
V
.
The
{\rm EOM}
reads

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-D^{\alpha}W_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}D^{\alpha}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\Big(W_{\alpha}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\Omega\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}D^{2}\Big(W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\dot{\alpha}}\Omega\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}D^{\alpha}\Big[W_{\alpha}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}D^{2}\Big(W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\frac{\partial\Omega}{\partial(D^{2}W^{2})}\Big)\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{\dot{\alpha}}\Big[\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{\dot{\alpha}}\Big(D^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\frac{\partial\Omega}{\partial(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2})}\Big)\Big]\,.

(2)

Because of the constraint that
W_{\alpha}W_{\beta}W_{\gamma}=0
and its complex conjugate,
multiplying eq. (
2
) by
W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}
and using the

{\rm EOM}
gives the following condition

\displaystyle W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}(D^{\alpha}W_{\alpha})\Big(1+f(\Omega)\Big)=0~,

(3)

where the functional
f(\Omega)
is given by

\displaystyle f(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}+D^{2}W^{2})\Omega

(4)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[(D^{2}W^{2})(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2})\frac{\partial\Omega}{\partial(D^{2}W^{2})}+(D^{2}W^{2})(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2})\frac{\partial\Omega}{\partial(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muD\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2})}\Bigg]~.

This implies

\displaystyle W^{2}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muW\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{2}(D^{\alpha}W_{\alpha})=0~,

(5)

which is precisely condition (
37
).

Appendix 7.G
Derivation of General Flow Equation for Gauge Field and Scalars

In this appendix, we will obtain the flow equation of a sufficiently general Lagrangian for all cases involving gauge fields and scalars that are of interest in Chapter
5
.

Consider a general
\lambda
-dependent Lagrangian for a complex scalar
\phi
and field strength
F
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}=f(\lambda,F^{2},|D\phi|^{2}),

(1)

For convenience, we will also define
x=F^{2}
and
y=|D\phi|^{2}
. As in the main body of Chapter
5
,
D
is the gauge-covariant derivative and the field strength
F
need not be abelian; we use the shorthand

\displaystyle F^{2}=F_{\mu\nu}^{a}F^{\mu\nu}_{a}=\Tr\left(F^{2}\right),

(2)

and we will suppress gauge group indices in what follows.

We can now compute the stress-energy tensor by coupling to a background metric and varying with respect to the metric, which gives

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}^{(\lambda)}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\mu\nu}\,f-4\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\sigma\nu}-2\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}D_{\mu}\phi D_{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu

\displaystyle=\eta_{\mu\nu}\,f+2\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\eta_{\mu\nu}F^{2}-2\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}D_{\mu}\phi D_{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu~,

(3)

where we have used that
\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}F_{\sigma\nu}=-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\nu}\left(F_{\alpha\beta}F^{\alpha\beta}\right)
in two dimensions.

The determinant of
T
is then expressed in terms of the combinations

\displaystyle T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\left(\eta^{\mu\nu}\,f+2\eta^{\mu\nu}F^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2D^{\mu}\phi D^{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)\left(\eta_{\mu\nu}\,f+2\eta_{\mu\nu}F^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2D_{\mu}\phi D_{\nu}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\phi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)

\displaystyle=2f^{2}-8F^{2}f\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-4|D\phi|^{2}f\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+8F^{4}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+8F^{2}|D\phi|^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4|D\phi|^{4}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2f^{2}-8xf\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-4yf\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+8x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+8xy\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4y^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)^{2}~,

(4)

and

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(2f-4F^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2|D\phi|^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=4f^{2}-16F^{2}f\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-8|D\phi|^{2}f\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+16F^{4}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+4|D\phi|^{4}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)^{2}+16F^{2}\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}|D\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle=4f^{2}-16xf\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-8yf\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+16x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+16xy\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4y^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}\right)^{2}~.

(5)

Using these, we can write the
{T\overline{T}}
operator as

\displaystyle\det( T )

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\left(\mathchoice{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}{T^{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}}\right)^{2}-T^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}\right)

\displaystyle=f^{2}-4fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2fy\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+4xy\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}~,

(6)

and hence the
{T\overline{T}}
-flow equation as

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=f^{2}-4fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}-2fy\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}+4x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}+4xy\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}~.

(7)

This is the main differential equation of interest which we will study in Chapter
5
. Although we have used
\eta_{\mu\nu}
for the metric, these results are valid either in Minkowski signature or in Euclidean signature – replacing
\eta_{\mu\nu}
with
\delta_{\mu\nu}
in the intermediate steps of these calculations does not affect our final result (
7
).

In the case where we turn off the field strength, setting
x=0
, this differential equation becomes

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=f^{2}-2fy\frac{\partial f}{\partial y}~.

(8)

Imposing the boundary condition that
f(\lambda=0)=|D\phi|^{2}
, we find

\displaystyle f(\lambda,|D\phi|^{2})=\frac{1}{2\lambda}\left(\sqrt{1+4\lambda|D\phi|^{2}}-1\right)~.

(9)

On the other hand, in the case where we turn off the scalars (setting
|D\phi|^{2}=0
), the differential equation (
7
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{df}{d\lambda}=f^{2}-4fx\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}+4x^{2}\left(\frac{\partial f}{\partial x}\right)^{2}.

(10)

which has the solution

\displaystyle f(\lambda,F^{2})=F^{2}\cdot{}_{3}F_{4}\left(\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{4},1,\frac{5}{4};\frac{4}{3},\frac{5}{3},2;\frac{256}{27}\cdot\lambda F^{2}\right)~.

(11)
